# Known Issues — engine registry

This is the factory's **append-only** registry of known issues, limitations, accepted
constraints, and by-design behaviours. Read it before changing the engine; add a `KI-*`
row in the SAME change that introduces or fixes an issue. A change that adds a limitation
without a row here is the silent-divergence failure mode this registry exists to prevent.

The lib self-test (`node _workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs`) is the regression net for the
correctness-class entries — keep it green.

> This shipped registry documents the **engine invariants** only. Your own operational
> findings (per-cycle issues, host-specific quirks) accumulate here as you run the factory.

## Engine invariants & operational constraints (by design — not bugs, but must be known)

| ID | Constraint |
|----|-----------|
| KI-E1 | **No mutating git, ever** — no `commit`/`add`/`checkout`/`restore`/`stash`/`reset`/`clean` by the factory or any subagent. Fixes land on `factory/<id>` worktree branches as UNSTAGED changes; **the human commits**. |
| KI-E2 | **The Workflow runtime cannot touch the filesystem or git, cannot `require()`, and `Date.now()`/`Math.random()`/argless `new Date()` throw.** Hence the two-half split: `driver.mjs` (Node — fs/git/ledger) + `factory.js` (Workflow — agents only). Pure helpers used inside `factory.js` are inlined byte-for-byte from `_workflow/lib/`; change both copies together. |
| KI-E3 | **The filesystem is the checkpoint** (`state/ledger.json` + worktree inspection) — resumable after a kill. The ledger has a **single writer** (the driver); hand-editing it risks corruption. The findings-graph is hand-editable. |
| KI-E4 | **Scope red-lines are hard stops** — an item whose only fix would cross a scope boundary you have declared MUST `scope-stop` (→ the human queue), never be "fixed" by adding the forbidden surface. |
| KI-E6 | **Graceful-stop drain guard** — a `state/STOP_REQUESTED.md` marker puts the factory in a graceful drain: `driver.mjs group` refuses while it exists (in-flight lanes finish, nothing new launches). `fold`/`reconstruct`/`escalations` stay unguarded so running work completes. Resuming is deliberate: delete the marker (or pass `group --stop-override`). |
| KI-E7 | **Telemetry is observational, never evidentiary.** Every factory action lands as an event on the append-only stream `telemetry/data/events.jsonl` (`FACTORY_TELEMETRY=0` disables). An emitted event never feeds a fold verdict; the factory runs fine with the telemetry stack down. `driver.mjs telemetry-report` renders the evaluation report from the JSONL directly. |
| KI-E17 | **Portable mounts** — the factory is mountable at any host path (submodule or plain clone). `_workflow/lib/rootfind.mjs` detects the host repo root (walk up from the mount's parent to the first `.git`; `FACTORY_REPO_ROOT` overrides) and rewrites the committed stock-prefixed config paths onto the real mount; an optional gitignored `config/factory.config.local.json` overlays per-host knobs. See `SETUP.md`. |
| KI-E18 | **AcceptanceScan** — a pre-band clause-coverage probe: the item's `acceptance` splits into checkable clauses (`lib/acceptance.mjs`, inlined byte-identically in factory.js — change both together, the selftest pins parity); a cheap probe answers per clause "does the diff carry concrete evidence?" BEFORE the gate band. Gaps feed ONE bounded fixer amend + one re-probe; still-uncovered clauses FAIL pre-band with clause-level gateDetails. Skipped for verificationOnly + single-clause acceptances; fail-open on a null/malformed probe. **Fix (multi-lens review, ported from a host-mount session, 2026-08-25):** the ACCEPTANCE-GAP AMEND prompt explicitly offers the fixer a note-only response ("or state in note precisely why a clause is already satisfied or out of this item's scope"), but the re-probe fired ONLY on `amend.applied` — a fixer taking that offered option got the item failed anyway on the stale pre-amend verdict, never re-evaluated. The re-probe now also fires on a genuine note-only response (non-empty `note`, no code change), explicitly feeding the probe that explanation and instructing it to judge the explanation critically rather than rubber-stamp it. Found and fixed as the twin of the identical bug independently introduced into KI-E87's sibling amend flow when it was ported here (see that entry) — this bug predates KI-E87 and was inherited when KI-E87 mirrored the pattern. |
| KI-E19 | **Evidence manifest** — `build-test.sh` trails every subcommand with a keyed `FACTORY::SUMMARY::<sub> …` marker (the suite carries its own counts); `parseVerifyRaw` prefers the keyed markers over the ambient type-agnostic dotnet summary line, so transcript append order can never misattribute counts. The fold adds a FULL-band PAIR rule: a FULL code item must show a green build marker AND a green suite signal in at least one transcript (results without `band` skip the rule — backward-compatible). |
| KI-E20 | **`driver recover <id>`** — the direct-recovery scaffold for a FAILED/ESCALATED item: dissent digest from the checkpoint's structured gateDetails, one delta re-gate prompt per dissenting role (role brief inlined), the evidence contract (integrate-raw vs mutation-proof separation + red-marker rules), the `#<cycle>r` fold skeleton (attemptsDelta:0; machine-evidence flags carried so recovery is never a lighter evidentiary path — review fix: `band` is among the carried flags, so a FULL-band prior arms the KI-E19 build+suite pair rule on the recovery fold too), and a `recovery_prepared` telemetry event. Prepare-only: the operator applies, re-gates, folds. Review fix: the fold override announces a gone/unreadable worktree (`debris/P9 diff checks SKIPPED`) instead of silently skipping the diff checks — transcript-only evidence is a visible state. |
| KI-E21 | **Sweep routing in `suggest`** — a cluster where one signature keyword spans >= 6 members (`--sweep-min` overrides) is recommended to the SWEEP band (`cluster.mjs --emit-pattern` + `driver sweep`) instead of pair-group lines — design-once + cheap applies + one pattern gate is measured ~4-10x cheaper per finding than pair lanes. |
| KI-E22 | **Acceptance-surface lint** — `graphaudit.mjs acceptanceSurfaceGaps`: path-like tokens + PascalCase type names in `acceptance` that resolve to a tracked repo file absent from `files[]` → advisory WARN at `graph-audit` and at `group` time (the file-lock cannot serialize an unlisted surface, so the fixer can be lock-forbidden from meeting acceptance). `graph-audit --fix` also appends the standards-ledger path for flagged shared-file gaps (the KI-E16 mechanical triage). |
| KI-E23 | **Telemetry completeness** — editorial verdicts are recorded in the gates map + gateDetails (advisory stays non-blocking); results and `item_folded` events carry `band` (LIGHT/FULL cost + gate-value split); the factory returns `usage` from the runtime budget counter and the fold emits a `usage` event, so cost analyses stop extrapolating from call counts. |
| KI-E24 | **`driver decisions-digest`** — one ranked page (severity x age) for the parked owner queue, with a one-line reply format (`<ID>: <letter>`) and same-target rule-together bundles; hand the digest to the owner instead of the raw queue wall. |
| KI-E40 | **The telemetry report counted only the literal `APPROVED` — 100%-passing reaudit/adjudicator/regate/direct-recovery rows rendered 0%, and KI-E23 usage events were never rendered.** The fold gates map carries role-specific ok-vocabularies (reaudit `code=ok security=ok`, adjudicator `UPHELD`, adjudicator-regate `CONFIRMED`, direct-recovery `converged-remedy …` prose); `renderTelemetryReport` counted `APPROVED` only, so those gates read as 0% ok — a poisoned quality signal (live: reaudit 0/11, adjudicator 0/7, both actually 100%). Fix: `verdictOk()` classifier in `lib/telemetry.mjs` (APPROVED/UPHELD/CONFIRMED, `converged-remedy` prefix, all-`key=ok` families) feeds the table; vocabulary the classifier does NOT recognize counts not-ok AND self-reports in a footnote (drift is visible, never silently 0%). `OVERRULED` joins the known-fail set deliberately — the adjudicator row's Ok-rate reads as the dissent-upheld calibration. Also: `usage` events aggregate + render (`## Fold-time token usage`), and the failure-concentration section carries a reading note (final=checkpoint = late-failure spend, not a checkpoint crash). Report-side only — fold evidence and emit paths untouched (KI-E7). |
| KI-E41 | **KI-L65 main-contamination detection ran only at FOLD — a relaunch after a killed run re-ran the full band against poisoned main** (witnessed cycle 47: the dead run's fixers had leaked fix variants — one factually wrong — into the MAIN tree; the sanctioned verbatim relaunch burned 2h+ of band before the fold detector fired). Fix: `resume` diffs each relaunch candidate's claim-time `main-snapshot.json` BEFORE printing the `Workflow({scriptPath})` launch line — dirty drift prints a loud ⚠ REPAIR-MAIN-FIRST block per item (committed-only drift prints the KI-E35 ℹ human-delivery variant). Detection-only, never blocks (same posture as the fold check: repairing main is operator judgment). A check that itself fails (corrupt snapshot, git error) ANNOUNCES `MAIN-GUARD <id> SKIPPED` — silence never reads as clean (review fix). |
| KI-E42 | **Killed-run debris survives into the relaunch artifact dir and misleads the relaunch's agents/reporters** (witnessed cycle 47: a dead attempt's stray `RESULT.md` — "false positive — already fixed, no action taken" — was read mid-relaunch; two gates burned findings on the debris). Canonical per-item artifact vocabulary = `STAGE_ARTIFACTS` + the `gate-*/review-*.md` patterns + the driver control files (`nonCanonicalArtifacts()` / `CONTROL_ARTIFACTS` in `lib/telemetry.mjs`). `resume` detects non-canonical files on every relaunch candidate and prints them; `resume --quarantine` moves them into `state/items/<id>/quarantine-<ts>/` (a plain `resume` stays read-only; directories are never touched; nothing is deleted). A scan that itself fails announces `DEBRIS-CHECK <id> SKIPPED` — never silently reads as clean (review fix). |
| KI-E43 | **LIGHT-band integrate false-fails on a Docker-less host — `baselineFailures` folds empty while the integrate full suite sees environmental failures** (witnessed cycle 47: ITEM-H15, 6 pre-existing Docker-unavailable Testcontainers failures vs baseline 0 → a 10/10-APPROVED item overridden to FAILED). Root cause: the LIGHT-band verify brief deliberately SKIPS the full suite (speed), so the runner's `baselineFailures` is structurally empty, yet the fold override compares the integrate suite's failure count against it. Fix — baseline capture, NOT an override weakening: (1) the RED brief instructs a pre-fix full-suite baseline capture (`state/items/<id>/baseline-raw.txt`) when `docker info` FAILS on the host — the tree is still unfixed at RED time, so the capture can never launder a fix-broken test; (2) the checkpoint prefers the RED-stage names (`test.baselineFailures`) over the verify-stage report; (3) the fold override merges `effectiveBaseline(reported, parse(baseline-raw.txt))` — the LARGER count wins, and failures beyond it stay regressions. A green baseline transcript contributes 0 (no free failure allowance). **reFix fence (review fix):** "the tree is still unfixed at RED time" holds only on a FIRST attempt — on a reFix (prevState FAILED/ESCALATED) the worktree already carries the prior fix, so the fold trusts the transcript only when its mtime PREDATES the reFix claim (a re-capture is ignored loudly) and the RED brief tells the reFix test-author not to re-capture. |
| KI-E44 | **`reconstruct` lost the run-level token usage — a reconstructed fold emitted no KI-E23 `usage` event.** Checkpoints carry no usage (factory.js reports the run total only at RETURN time, which a killed run never reaches), so a killed run's output tokens were visible only in the harness task output — invisible to cost telemetry. Fix: `reconstruct --usage-tokens <N>` stamps `payload.usage.outputTokens` into the reconstructed results file; the fold's existing KI-E23 usage emit then fires exactly as on the live path. Without the flag, reconstruct prints a loud hint naming the gap. Observational only — never affects a verdict. |
| KI-E45 | **The KI-L65 main-snapshot covered only `files[]` — contamination on an acceptance-named file OUTSIDE the lock set was invisible to every fold-time drift check** (witnessed cycle 48: ITEM-H16's lane wrote an acceptance-named CONTEXT.md in MAIN — the fold flagged 3 other lanes' drift but was structurally blind to this file; only a manual broad `git status` sweep caught it). The KI-E22 probe had predicted exactly this hole at group time. Fix: `group` retains the KI-E22 acceptance-resolved paths per item (`gapsByItem`) and the claim-time `main-snapshot.json` now hashes the UNION of `files[]` and those paths — the fold's KI-L65 re-hash and the KI-E41 resume main-guard both inherit the widened net for free. Detection-only widening; the lock set itself is unchanged. |
| KI-E46 | **The direct-recovery KPI recognized only the legacy gates-map signature — the factory's DOMINANT close path was invisible.** The KI-E20 `recover` scaffold folds with resultId `<id>#<cycle>r` and a note starting `direct-recovery`; only pre-scaffold hand-rolled folds carried a literal `direct-recovery` gates key. The KPI counted the key alone, so every scaffolded recovery close read as a plain re-band close — live: `Direct-recovery rate 4/29 = 14%` rendered while the true figure was **13/29 = 45%**. Fix: pure `isRecoveryResultId()` (`#Nr`/`#NrK`) + `isDirectRecoveryFold()` (emit-time `direct` stamp / gates key / recovery resultId / prefix-anchored note — a mid-note mention never classifies) in `lib/telemetry.mjs`; `aggregateEvents` classifies with it; the fold stamps `attrs.resultId` + `attrs.direct` on `item_folded` so future streams never need prose sniffing. |
| KI-E47 | **Recovery folds polluted the derived stage-duration authority.** A recovery fold re-derived the artifact-mtime timeline over the item dir, so the operator-paced remedy/re-gate window folded in as fake band durations (live: one item's 4 re-gate rounds over hours contributed to the `gates` p95 nearly doubling, 3692s → 6549s). AD-12's duration authority measures BAND-paced stage work only. Fix: the fold derives + emits NO stage timeline for a recovery result; the recovery's telemetry is its `item_folded` record (with the KI-E46 stamps). Historical polluted events stay in the append-only stream and age out of relevance — disclosed here. |
| KI-E48 | **Two report sections were structurally dead.** (a) The band-split KPI (FULL vs LIGHT first-pass) had data since `band` stamping (KI-E23) but was never rendered. (b) "Stage durations — agent-reported" rendered `_none_` forever: agents never pass `--durMs` (they cannot know their own wall-clock), and the aggregation only consumed `durMs`. Fix: (a) `renderTelemetryReport` renders per-band first-pass rows keyed by the band of the item's FIRST fold; a pure-legacy (unstamped) stream renders no rows. (b) `aggregateEvents` pairs each agent `stage_start`/`stage_end` per item+role and derives the duration from the two `ts` stamps (latest-start-wins for retries; gap-fenced; skipped when `durMs` is present) — an independent best-effort cross-check of the mtime-derived authority (AD-12 unchanged: still non-evidentiary). |
| KI-E49 | **Agents free-typed `--event` names into the stream** (live: `probe`, `dummy_probe`, `tool_use` — one review agent used it as a running commentary channel). The AD-10 agent seam defines exactly two events; unbounded vocabulary pollutes `byEvent` and invites drift. Fix: `clampAgentEvent()` in `lib/telemetry.mjs`; `telemetry-emit.mjs` clamps any non-canonical `--event` to `agent_note`, preserving the original name in `attrs.origEvent`. Bounded vocabulary, breadcrumb kept, still always-exit-0. |
| KI-E50 | **Main-tree contamination detection ran only at FOLD (KI-L65) and RESUME (KI-E41) — a mid-band write stayed invisible for hours while sibling agents read the poisoned tree** (witnessed cycle 48: 4/6 lanes wrote main during the band). Fix: new read-only, lock-free, lease-free `driver main-check <id> […]`: re-hashes each item's claim-time `main-snapshot.json` against MAIN (reuses the `mainguard` drift + KI-E35 committed/dirty split), prints ⚠ MAIN-DRIFT / ℹ committed-delivery / clean, always exits 0. The `factory.js` verifyHint has the runner execute it between verify and the gate band and paste any ⚠ line VERBATIM into its note — the gates + checkpoint see drift the moment it exists. WARN-ONLY by the same posture as fold/resume; prevention (sandboxing main read-only vs failing the lane fast) remains a host-owner policy decision the engine deliberately does not make. |
| KI-E51 | **"Fix-introduced defects" — errors the fix's OWN additions carry — became the #1 rejection class and no brief targeted it** (live: 3/4 of one cycle's FAILs + the next cycle's only FAIL: a countable claim the tree grep-disproves; a false cross-reference clause; narrative comments in the fix's own test file). The pre-band probes are RIGHT to pass these (clause coverage / deferral scans target different classes); only the adversarial band catches them, at full FAILED-round price. Fix, prompt-level, zero new agents: `fixer.md` **COUNT-CLAIM SELF-CHECK** (every countable/enumerable claim the diff adds is re-derived by grep/ls before finishing, derivation quoted in fix.json) + `test-author.md` **COMMENT POLICY** (no narrative comments in the new test file). Complements the deterministic KI-E11 path-claims lint, which covers paths but not counts. |
| KI-O1 | **`_workflow/opencode/` — an alternate runtime binding for a controlling session with no native `Workflow` tool** (e.g. OpenCode/other agentic CLIs — KI-E2's split assumed the controlling session IS Claude Code; this is the first binding for one that isn't). `factory.js` cannot run outside Claude Code's sandboxed Workflow runtime (it needs that runtime's `agent()` primitive); this directory re-implements the DETERMINISTIC half of `runItem()` as a real Node CLI (`runtime.mjs`) — routing/band/flow selection (`routing.mjs`, verbatim port), prompt composition (`compose.mjs`, verbatim port, reads the real `agents/*.md` briefs directly since this runs unsandboxed), structured-output schema validation (`schemas.mjs`, a minimal zero-dep JSON-Schema-subset validator, byte-checked against every `*_SCHEMA` const in `factory.js`), and build/test execution (`buildtest.mjs`, spawns the SAME unmodified `verify/build-test.sh` — no reimplementation — and **imports** `lib/verify.mjs`'s transcript parsers + `lib/preflight.mjs`'s docker/dotnet probes directly rather than re-deriving them, so there is zero drift risk from what the fold/preflight actually use). The controlling session drives one item at a time through a checkpoint protocol (`init`/`next`/`submit --role <key>`/`mech`/`status`/`finalize`) — `next` returns either a MECHANICAL step (run it directly, no LLM) or one-or-more AGENT steps (dispatch via the session's own subagent-launch tool, e.g. OpenCode's `Task`, in parallel when `next` returns more than one — mirrors `Promise.all` pooling), `mech checkpoint` writes a `state/items/<id>/result.json` in the same shape `driver.mjs fold` validates, and `finalize` wraps it into the `{mode,cycle,results:[...]}` envelope `fold` actually requires (a bare per-item file folds as "ZERO results" otherwise — a real stumble the first validation pass hit, fixed here rather than left as an operator trap). **Validated twice, end to end, on non-production .NET fixtures** (never against a real product item): (a) a hand-crafted-JSON pass through every phase including the adjudicator/re-gate path; (b) a REAL subagent-dispatch pass (`Task`, `general` subagent) through test-author → fixer → `mech verify` → review-edgecase → **a genuine CHANGES_REQUIRED** (the reviewer independently built a scratch harness and found a real regression: negative-length input now throwing where the pre-fix code didn't) → bounded amend → re-scan convergence → gates → PO → re-audit → `mech integrate` → `CLOSED`, with `driver.mjs fold` correctly rejecting the finished result for the expected reason (`unknown-id` — a non-graphed test item) and zero ledger side effects either time. **Documented fidelity gaps, not silently papered over:** (1) **no per-call model tiering** — `factory.js`'s `agent(prompt, opts.model)` selects opus/sonnet/haiku/fable-5 per role; a session without that capability (e.g. OpenCode's `Task` tool, which only selects a `subagent_type`, not a model) runs every role on whatever model backs its subagent — the RT/FLOW_RT tables in `routing.mjs` are kept as intended-routing metadata only, and `res.cost` records the actual session model used under a distinctly-named bucket, never claims the intended tier ran; (2) **no sweep-mode** (`cmdSweep`'s cluster-fix path) and **no true multi-item batch parallelism** — this binding drives ONE item at a time, by design, matching an operator-driven session's natural cadence. **Fixed during hardening (not left as gaps):** the decision-framer is now a REAL dispatched phase (`decision_frame`, `DECISION_SCHEMA`) — `frameAndBlock` no longer short-circuits straight to BLOCKED, matching `factory.js` exactly; `LEFTOVER_SCHEMA`'s `punt.line` was typed `number` in this port's first draft but is a STRING in the real schema (would have wrongly rejected a valid subagent response — caught by diffing against `factory.js` directly, not by inference); `ACCEPT_SCHEMA`/`LEFTOVER_SCHEMA`/`DECISION_SCHEMA`'s nested item schemas now carry the real `additionalProperties:false`/`required` too; `mech verify` now REFUSES a codeChange item on a non-FULL band with no `--filter` (previously fell through to a build-only run that `verdictFromParse` still reports as "tests green" — a silent false-green risk, since build-only machine evidence is indistinguishable downstream from "test ran and passed"). **Dry-run against a REAL production item (2026-07-28) surfaced + fixed one more gap:** `compose()` only told a dispatched agent to check for pre-existing worktree changes when `reFix` was true, but a shared/reused worktree can carry uncommitted work from a killed run even when `reFix` computes false (exactly this item's state: `prevState` was `READY` at the last `group` claim, so `reFix=false`, yet the worktree still held the killed cycle-4 attempt's uncommitted diff) — a dispatched agent could be confused finding unexpected changes with no cue they might be relevant. Fixed: an unconditional WORKTREE STATE CHECK guardrail line (always run `git status --porcelain` + `git diff HEAD` in the worktree first, regardless of reFix). Also closed a real coverage gap the same dry run exposed: every prior validation run used a LIGHT-band item, so the FULL-band 5-gate selection and the realInfra marker-probe FAILED path (`needsRealInfra:true` + a build+suite that genuinely passes but carries no `FACTORY::REALINFRA::` marker) had never been exercised. Both now validated: `gateRolesFor('FULL', ...)` pinned in `_selftest.mjs`, and the marker-probe FAILED path proven live against a real .NET project (build clean, suite green, correctly FAILED anyway for the missing container marker — the exact fail-closed behavior a `realInfra:true` production item depends on). **A follow-up review pass (this same change) fixed lifecycle divergences in the port:** edge-case-hunter verdicts are blocking again; the resultId cycle stamp matches the driver's `cycle+1`; `next` is truly idempotent and completed phases reject stale re-submits; `realInfraLikely`/pureCoverage match factory.js exactly; the escalate tier parks only AFTER refute+re-audit; the PO gate respects the band's gate set; the P10 codeChange refinement is implemented; count markers parse anchored/last-match; `extractJson` takes the LAST fenced block; FAILED/BLOCKED/ESCALATED terminals write result.json via checkpoint; and doc-only items skip the dotnet build. A schema-parity selftest now diffs `schemas.mjs` against factory.js's `*_SCHEMA` consts mechanically, and the opencode suite is invoked from the main selftest so the release/install gates cover it. |
| KI-O2 | **`FIX_SCHEMA.divergence` rejected the exact shape `agents/fixer.md` tells fixers to return.** The brief's own "Write + return" section says `divergence (null or {rule, ledgerAnchor})` — an OBJECT — but `FIX_SCHEMA` in `factory.js` typed the field `['string','null']` only. Live-caught 2026-07-28 (a real production item, real production run): a fixer subagent followed the brief exactly, returned `divergence:{rule,ledgerAnchor}`, and the structured-output layer rejected it (a real `agent()` StructuredOutput-retry-cap class failure in the native pipeline too, not just this port's validator — the same schema gates both). Confirmed nothing downstream ever reads `.divergence` programmatically (`driver.mjs`/`lib/*.mjs`/`factory.js` all grepped clean) — it is purely informational for human fold review — so the fix widens the schema (`['string','null','object']`) to match the brief's own contract instead of narrowing the brief to match an accidentally-stricter schema. Fixed in both `factory.js` (canonical) and `_workflow/opencode/schemas.mjs` (KI-O1's `FIX_SCHEMA` copy) in the same change. |
| KI-O3 | **`mech verify`/`mech integrate` silently tested the WRONG tree when given an absolute path outside the item's worktree.** Live-caught 2026-07-28 (a real production item): passed an absolute path to the host's MAIN repo instead of the item's linked-worktree copy — the main repo builds and tests fine on its own (unrelated to the fix), so `mech verify` reported a false green having tested the UNFIXED tree entirely; the new test genuinely didn't exist there ("No test matches the given testcase filter", a VACUOUS pass at exit=0 that the P3/verdictFromParse layer cannot distinguish from a real one on its own). Two related fixes in `runtime.mjs`'s `cmdMech`: (1) a bare/relative target (e.g. `src/<Solution>.sln`) now resolves against `ctx.worktreePath` automatically — removing the #1 way to hand-type the wrong absolute path; (2) an absolute target outside the item's worktree prefix now prints a loud WARNING (not a hard block — a deliberate cross-tree check is occasionally legitimate) instead of silently proceeding. Same KI-O1 dry run also caught a SECOND, compounding bug: FULL-band `mech verify` ran `suite` only (see the FULL+filter fix logged in this same entry's history) — both bugs together meant a FULL-band realInfra item's marker-probe FAILED for two independent reasons simultaneously (wrong tree AND wrong-shaped verify), which took two real re-runs to fully isolate and fix. |
| KI-E52 | **Versioned releases + team install/upgrade + per-developer telemetry bootstrap.** Before this, the factory had no version identity (no tags/releases), onboarding was the manual SETUP.md ritual, upgrades were ad-hoc pulls with no safety gate, and the per-developer observability infra (the compose stack + the KI-E33 session cost-telemetry env) was left to each developer — the env opt-in failed silently on real hosts three sessions in a row. Now: `VERSION` + `CHANGELOG.md` + `setup/release.sh` (maintainer: selftest-gated cut — bump, changelog prepend, annotated `vX.Y.Z` tag, push, GitHub Release) and `setup/install.sh` (team: curl-able one-liner). `install` clones at the latest release tag (falls back to `main` with a notice while no releases exist), runs `setup/init.mjs`, gates on the selftest, and bootstraps telemetry; `upgrade` is IN-PLACE and **selftest-gated with automatic rollback** to the previous ref on red — per-host state (`state/`, `reports/`, `queue/`, `telemetry/data/`, `telemetry/.env`, `*.local.*`) is gitignored and structurally untouched, making `upgrade --yes` unattended-safe (cron-able); `status` compares installed vs latest; `telemetry-up` writes the per-host `telemetry/.env` (+ now-gitignored — E2E-caught), generates `claude-code-telemetry.env` with the stack's OTLP port, merges the env block into the host `.claude/settings.local.json`, optionally installs a marker-guarded shell-profile block (the reliable KI-E33 path — some runtimes do not forward `OTEL_*` from settings env), starts the compose stack idempotently and health-checks Prometheus. A vendored (.git-less) mount is refused, never half-upgraded. E2E-tested end to end via the in-tree hermetic harness `setup/_e2e.sh` (local fixture remote, no network: broken-tag rollback, transactional-install cleanup, PR-only-main release fallback, stale-cut guard; an explicit `--host` beats script self-location — a redirect bug the E2E caught). **Deep-review hardening (2026-07-27):** release.sh verifies local main == origin/main BEFORE the cut and pushes `--atomic` (a rejected `--follow-tags` branch push still published the tag — demoed), then falls back to tag + `release/vX.Y.Z` branch + PR when main is PR-only (direct pushes blocked by ruleset — the factory's own repo is configured so); a failed install removes the mount it created (no "already installed" dead-end with init never run) and a telemetry failure never fails a good engine install; upgrade short-circuits when already current, warns on a downgrade, re-runs `setup/init.mjs` (host skill/scaffolding refresh), and a FAILED rollback announces itself; latest-release resolve dies loudly on an unreachable remote (never a silent channel-switch to main) and filters to strict `vX.Y.Z` (an rc/date tag never wins); an existing-but-unparseable host `settings.local.json` is REFUSED, never clobbered; hand-edited `telemetry/.env` files with missing keys fall back to defaults instead of dying under pipefail; shell-profile markers are mount-scoped (KI-E25 second stack). |
| KI-E39 | **Telemetry stack as a host-compose profile (documented alternative to the standalone project).** `telemetry/compose-profile.example.yaml` + a README section: the four services fold into the HOST repo's `docker-compose.yaml` under an opt-in `factory` profile — a dedicated network with aliases `exporter`/`otel-collector`/`prometheus` keeps this stack's configs byte-for-byte unmodified, no `container_name` (compose project scoping isolates hosts, so the KI-E25 collision class disappears), and host-owned ports are remapped (example: OTLP 4417/4418, Grafana 3100). Proven live in a host integration (3/3 Prometheus targets up, dashboard provisioned, session `claude_code_*` metrics flowing at the remapped port). The session env must then point at the REMAPPED OTLP port — `claude-code-telemetry.env.example` says so. |
| KI-E38 | **Real-shape seeding: a minimal test seed greens write paths that throw on real rows.** Live: a backfill endpoint's 5/5-green integration tests seeded a JSON-owned aggregate WITHOUT one of its optional owned collections; real rows carried it, and the apply path threw EF's `'…__synthesizedOrdinal' is part of a key` (whole-aggregate replacement re-parents owned-collection children) — after every gate had approved. Rule added to `test-author.md` (REAL-SHAPE SEEDING: populate every optional collection/nullable member the model declares, or justify the omission in the note) + a matching `review-testreview.md` quality check. Prompt-level only — no machinery change. |
| KI-E37 | **`gc` sweeps worktree-scoped docker-compose projects.** Agent/verify runs can `docker compose up` inside a worktree (default project name = worktree dir); removing the worktree orphans the project — live: 12 Created containers + 3 named volumes outlived their worktree. `gc` now lists (dry-run) / `down -v --remove-orphans`s (`--yes`) every compose project whose ConfigFiles ALL sit under the CONFIGURED worktrees root (`abs(cfg.paths.worktreesState)`, separator-anchored prefix per comma-joined entry — review fix: sibling dirs and hybrid host+override projects can never match; `worktree.parseComposeLs` + `strayComposeProjects`, behaviorally selftest-pinned). Best-effort: docker absent (ENOENT) → silently skipped; any OTHER enumeration failure prints a one-line SKIPPED notice (review fix — silence must not read as "no strays"). |
| KI-E36 | **The queue hints when a parked item was likely delivered in HEAD.** Delivered-in-HEAD detection existed only at graph seeding; an item ruled + hand-delivered MID-RUN sat BLOCKED with its acceptance already satisfied in HEAD (live: HOST-ITEM-A — output committed by the operator while the ledger still said BLOCKED). `escalations` AND the KI-E24 decisions-digest now carry a per-item hint when EVERY `files[]` entry has a commit newer than the park baseline — the history entry that ENTERED the parked state (`ledger.parkedAtMs`; `updatedAt` moves on later row writes — review fix), with the date logic pure + selftest-pinned (`ledger.allCommittedAfter`): "possibly DELIVERED in HEAD — verify, then `driver recover <id>`". Advisory-only (git log timestamps); never a state change; a corrupt graph disables hints with a stderr notice instead of silently (review fix). |
| KI-E35 | **Fold's KI-L65 warning distinguishes human-committed delivery from agent contamination.** KI-L65 always shouted "an agent likely wrote outside its worktree — inspect + repair", which is exactly wrong for the legitimate case of the operator committing the item's output mid-run (live: it fired on the intended human delivery of HOST-ITEM-A). The factory never commits — so drift on a path `git status --porcelain` reports clean (committed; not edited, not untracked) came via human commits: now reported as `ℹ COMMITTED DELIVERY … verify intent, no repair needed`; ANY uncommitted state — a dirty edit OR an untracked NEW file (invisible to `git diff HEAD`; the live ITEM-H5 stray-contamination shape — review fix) — keeps the ⚠ contamination wording (`mainguard.splitDriftByStatus`, behaviorally selftest-pinned; git failure → contamination, the conservative direction). Same WARN-only posture; still never blocks the fold. |
| KI-E34 | **`recover` handles BLOCKED (owner-ruling recovery).** Resolving a ruled BLOCKED item previously required internals surgery: hand-edit the graph, know that BLOCKED's only legal edge is → READY, hand-author a recovery fold with the `#<cycle>r` resultId + the full CLAIMED re-entry chain (live: HOST-ITEM-A took exactly that). `recover <id>` now accepts BLOCKED and scaffolds the protocol with the BLOCKED-specific steps spelled out: record the ruling on the graph item (`ownerDecision` + `ownerDecisionResolved: true`, ratified files into `files[]`), run `driver reset <id>` BEFORE the fold, and — when the ruling ratifies already-delivered work — the prior round's transcripts stand as evidence. `recoveryTransitions('BLOCKED')` = the full 10-hop chain (selftest-pinned). The fold's deterministic evidence override applies unchanged — a BLOCKED recovery is never a lighter evidentiary path (review fix: a prior-less skeleton — the never-ran seed-BLOCKED shape — FILL-prompts `codeChange`; unfilled reads truthy, so the override demands full code evidence and fails closed instead of silently taking the doc/config no-evidence path; the scaffold spells out the never-ran protocol, and `recover` explains the run-recover-before-reset ordering when refused). |
| KI-E33 | **A run's cost/token telemetry is silently NOT gathered unless the session opts in — now surfaced at preflight.** The dashboard's two cost panels read `claude_code_token_usage_*`, emitted by the **Claude Code session** (via OTLP), not by the factory. If the session was launched without `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TELEMETRY=1` + `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`, an entire run's token telemetry is lost with no cue — `factory_*` metrics (driver/agent-stage/fold/transition/aggregate-usage from `events.jsonl`) still land, so it looks like telemetry "worked" (live: two full cycles gathered `factory_*` but zero `claude_code_*`). `preflight` now reports `cost telemetry: ready → <endpoint>` or `NOT gathered — <reason>` (`costTelemetryReady`, pure/selftest-covered — review fix: `ready` also requires `OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER` to include `otlp`, because Claude Code has NO default metrics exporter and enable+endpoint alone export nothing; the metrics-specific `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_METRICS_ENDPOINT` counts; a missing `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL` rides as a caution on ready; every missing var is named; `FACTORY_TELEMETRY` is no longer consulted — it mutes only the factory's own events.jsonl, an independent plane), and the `/ai-factory` session-start step tells the operator to fix it before running (set the env — `telemetry/claude-code-telemetry.env.example`, KI-E28 — or put it in the host's `.claude/settings.local.json` `env`). The collector→Prometheus path itself was already correct (verified by pushing a synthetic OTLP metric end-to-end); the gap was purely session opt-in with no warning. |
| KI-E31 | **`fold` accepts the Workflow harness envelope directly.** The runtime wraps the factory's return as `{summary, agentCount, logs, result:{mode,cycle,results:[…]}}`, so a raw task-output file carries the fold payload under `.result`. `cmdFold` read `results.results` at the top level → `undefined` → it silently folded **0** items, forcing the operator to hand-extract `.result` into a file every cycle. `unwrapResultEnvelope` (pure, in `ledger.mjs`, selftest-covered) now unwraps that one shape; a direct results file (array, or an object already carrying `.results`) passes through unchanged. `fold <task-output-file>` now Just Works. Review fix: a ZERO-result payload (crashed/empty-run envelope, or an unexpected shape) no longer folds with success affect — loud error, exit 1, no cycle bump, no ledger write. |
| KI-E32 | **Acceptance-surface lint (KI-E22) no longer flags references/citations/exclusions.** The lint nudges files named in `acceptance` but absent from `files[]`. It fired on every run for files the item names to *model on*, *cite* (`SomeEndpoint.cs:739`), or *exclude* (`do NOT touch SomeHelper`) — none of which the file-lock should serialize. `acceptanceSurfaceGaps` now skips a token whose immediate context carries a strong reference cue (`modeled on`, `same … uses`, `File.cs:NN` citation, `do not touch`, `non-goal`, `rules out`, `instead of`, …). Deliberately conservative: an actively-named edit target (`ItemsController clamps …`, `modify X`, `in X`) still surfaces, so genuine "you forgot file X in the lock-set" gaps stay visible. Review fix: the cue set was TIGHTENED — bare `existing`/`see`/`per` and trailing `uses`/`does`/`already` suppressed everyday ACTIVE phrasings ("the existing X must clamp", "X does not clamp", "X uses raw Skip; fix it"); clause stops (`;:!?`) now end a cue's reach, and a bare `TypeName.cs:NN` citation is recognized (previously only pathed citations were). Both directions selftest-pinned. |
| KI-E29 | **A CLOSED dependency whose fix is uncommitted provides no code to dependents.** Worktrees branch from HEAD, and fixes stay UNCOMMITTED on `factory/<id>` branches (KI-E1) — so a dependency being `CLOSED` in the ledger does *not* put its code in HEAD until the human commits it. A dependent scheduled before that commit builds from a HEAD missing the dependency's code (live: `HOST-ITEM-A` depended on CLOSED-but-uncommitted `HOST-ITEM-B`; the fixer correctly re-derived HOST-ITEM-B's missing method fix inside its own worktree, which then tripped a false lock-set/scope block — KI-E30). `group` now WARNs when a picked item depends on a CLOSED item that still has a live worktree (proxy for "not yet committed to HEAD"); it does not exclude (nothing is clobbered — the owner decides whether to commit the dependency first for a clean base). Distinct from KI-E14, which is about the *main tree's* uncommitted overlap. Review fix: the check reads the LEDGER ROW's recorded `worktree` path (pure `ledger.closedDepsWithLiveWorktree`, selftest-pinned) instead of an assumed `state/worktrees/<id>` dir — SWEEP-closed deps are covered too, and gc (which nulls the field) correctly ends the warn. |
| KI-E30 | **`scopeViolation` conflated a lock-set deviation with a product-scope red-line.** The shared gate schema's `scopeViolation:true` is meant for the one hard-stop — a `product-scope.md` red-line (tax/purchase-fee/SAR/gov-report/shipping). But nothing told gates that touching a file *outside the item's declared `files[]` touch-set* is NOT that, so the developer gate set `scopeViolation:true` for a justified 1-line companion fix outside the lock-set, and `factory.js`'s KI-L57 guard (non-APPROVED + scopeViolation → hard block) framed it to the owner as a "product-scope violation" — a false-premise block on green, fully-reviewed code (live: `HOST-ITEM-A`). Fixed in the shared gate prompt: `scopeViolation` means a product-scope red-line ONLY; a touch-set deviation is a normal `finding` (note whether justified) and the verdict rests on its merits. The lock-set-overlap *observation* remains valuable — it stays a finding, it just no longer masquerades as a red-line hard-stop. Follow-up (same live case): when the hard stop DOES fire, `factory.js` echoes the flagging gate's own headline into the block reason AND the queue note keeps that raw reason verbatim — bracketed after the decision-framer's headline when framing succeeds, bare otherwise (review fix; previously the framer's paraphrase could displace the gate headline from the note) — so a mislabelled block is self-evident from the queue. |
| KI-E28 | **Dashboard cost panels need session-side OTLP.** The Grafana dashboard's two cost panels (`Claude Code token rate by type`, `Prompt-cache hit ratio`) read `claude_code_token_usage_tokens_total`, which the **Claude Code session** emits — not the factory exporter. The collector was already wired to accept it (`otel-collector.yaml` metrics pipeline has an `otlp` receiver; :4318 exposed), but nothing configured the session, so the panels were permanently empty with no cue why. Fixed as a setup step, not code: `telemetry/claude-code-telemetry.env.example` + a `telemetry/README.md` section give the exact env (`CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TELEMETRY=1`, `OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER=otlp`, `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` → the collector's host HTTP port). Also documented: the `Span-derived stage latency p90` panel is live-only (spans push only while the exporter runs — AD-13), whereas the exporter-histogram `Stage duration p95` panel is the always-populated duration authority. Every `factory_*` panel needs none of this. |
| KI-E27 | **Multi-source issue ingestion.** `driver ingest` pulls work-items into `state/normalized/<source>.json` (which `merge-graph` folds) from `--github owner/repo` (`--issues` csv or `--label`/`--state`/`--limit`, via the `gh` CLI), `--json` (gh-issue array or ready work-item passthrough), or `--markdown` (a `- [ ]` checklist). The mapping is the pure lib `_workflow/lib/ingest.mjs` (selftest-covered — label→severity/theme, section extraction, id shape); the driver does the I/O (KI-E2 split). **Honest-acceptance invariant:** an ingested item is never `auto` — `blocked` triage when no acceptance section is parseable, at most `escalate` when an "Expected behaviour" section is lifted (a human still confirms it). A raw issue rarely states a checkable acceptance + regressionTest, so ingestion seeds the queue and a human/`bmad-spec` authors the contract before it schedules; it never fabricates a green light. Replaces the SETUP.md §6 "no adapter ships" gap. Review fixes: the invariant is ENFORCED at the command too — `enforceIngestTier` clamps `--json` passthrough tiers (auto/missing → escalate/blocked; selftest-pinned) so merge-graph's auto-default can never see an ingested item, and merge-graph now prints a VERIFY problem line when a hand-authored file's tier defaults to auto; flags are validated loudly up front (`--severity`/`--id-prefix`/`--issues`), gh failures exit 1 with the stderr reason, a --limit-sized result warns of likely truncation, `--out` is sanitized to a plain in-dir name, malformed `--json` elements are counted + skipped instead of crashing; the markdown adapter ingests UNCHECKED boxes only (a checked `[x]` is done work — skipped, count reported) with CONTENT-HASHED ids (stable under backlog edits; positional ids re-attached ledger state to different tasks), and `ingestReport.other` guards the "none are auto-runnable" line. Provenance cites the actual matched heading; fenced code no longer truncates section lifts; severity/theme label boundaries hardened (`not-critical`, `P10`, `docker`). |
| KI-E26 | **Host data must never enter the engine repo.** `SETUP.md` promises "everything under `state/`, `reports/`, and `queue/` is generated per host at runtime and is gitignored, so no project data ever enters this repo" — but `queue/decisions.md` was **tracked**, and `state/{ledger,findings-graph,PROGRESS}` + `reports/*` were merely untracked (so `git add -A` in the mount swept them in). Operating the factory in a host therefore dirtied a tracked file with that host's data: the decision queue quotes owner framings verbatim, which routinely name customers, internal issue ids and production detail. `queue/decisions.md` is now untracked (`queue/.gitkeep` ships instead, matching `state/`/`reports/`) and the per-host runtime artifacts are gitignored, making the shipped promise true. Nothing reads these as input — `setup/init.mjs` scaffolds the dirs and `driver init`/`escalations` regenerate the files on every host. |
| KI-E25 | **Telemetry stack identity is per-host, not just per-port.** The factory mounts in any number of host repos, but `telemetry/docker-compose.yml` pinned `container_name: factory-*` and the compose project `name:`, so a SECOND host on the same machine could not start its stack at all (duplicate container names — and `docker compose -p` does **not** override `container_name`), and the project name scopes the `prometheus-data`/`grafana-data` volumes, so a renamed-by-hand host would silently share host A's data. Both are now `${FACTORY_COMPOSE_PROJECT:-ai-factory-telemetry}` / `${FACTORY_CONTAINER_PREFIX:-factory}`, defaulting to the historical names (existing single-host stacks unaffected). Host B sets both in `.env` alongside the ports — see `telemetry/README.md`. Verified by running two stacks concurrently. Prometheus scrapes by **service** name (`exporter:9464`), never `container_name`, so inter-service DNS is unaffected. |
| KI-E53 | **`escalations` rendered "(no note)" for every ESCALATED/BLOCKED item, always** — the human decision queue's headline reason was structurally silent. `cmdEscalationsSync` reads `r.note` (the ledger row's static field) per item, but nothing in `driver.mjs` ever assigns `ledger.items[id].note` — only per-transition `history[].note` entries carry real reasons (e.g. the landing note on the `GATED -> ESCALATED` hop). The function already defines a correct `lastNote(r)` walk (last note-bearing history entry) for the separate "retry-exhausted FAILED" section a few lines below, but the main per-item line used the dead field instead. Live-caught 2026-07-28 (a real production item, real production run): `queue/decisions.md` showed the item ESCALATED with `- (no note)` despite the ledger history carrying "auto-drafted + fully verified; awaiting human sign-off before integrate (blast-radius)" one hop earlier. Fix: the per-item line now calls the same `lastNote(r)` helper instead of `r.note \|\| '(no note)'` — zero new code, just routes to the already-correct logic sitting right there. Confirmed live: re-running `escalations` after the fix rendered the real reason. |
| KI-E54 | **Every fold-time transcript re-read (`verify-raw.txt`/`integrate-raw.txt`/`verify-red-raw.txt`/`baseline-raw.txt`) hardcoded `readFileSync(p,'utf8')` with ZERO encoding defense.** A producer that emits UTF-16 instead of UTF-8/ASCII — e.g. a dispatched subagent's `\| tee <file>` command whose shell resolves to PowerShell, where `tee`/`Tee-Object`/`>`/`Out-File` all default to UTF-16LE — silently mis-decodes into mojibake under the hardcoded `'utf8'` read; every `FACTORY::` marker regex in `lib/verify.mjs` then finds nothing, collapsing to `hasData:false`/`"no-machine-evidence"` with no error or warning anywhere in the fold path. Live-caught 2026-07-28 (a real production item, real production run, OpenCode-driven session on Windows): a genuine `FACTORY::RED::1` proof existed in the test-author subagent's own transcript (self-reported: "Failed: 1, Passed: 0 ... row count 0 vs expected 1"), but its teed `verify-red-raw.txt` landed as UTF-16LE bytes (confirmed via hex dump: `FF FE` BOM, null-interleaved bytes) and read back unparseable — triggering a false deterministic fold-time override to FAILED on real, correctly-produced evidence, which then burned a resultId before the true root cause was isolated. Fix: `decodeTranscript(buf)` — a pure, dependency-free BOM-sniffing decoder in `lib/verify.mjs` (UTF-16LE/BE BOM -> correct decode incl. byte-swap for BE; UTF-8 BOM -> stripped; no BOM -> unchanged `'utf8'` decode, byte-identical to the old behaviour on the common path) — wired into BOTH of `driver.mjs`'s fold-time read sites (the shared `readIf` helper and the inline `baseline-raw.txt` read) in the same change. Confined to `driver.mjs`/`lib/verify.mjs` — the OpenCode port (`_workflow/opencode/*.mjs`) never re-reads these files from disk (it parses the in-memory transcript immediately after `spawnSync(..., {encoding:'utf8'})`, before writing to disk), so it was never exposed to this class at all; the fix is purely a canonical-engine hardening, not a port-specific patch. |
| KI-E55 | **The intermediate strengthening of KI-E51's comment policy (2026-07-30) — defined here because agent briefs, lint headers, and selftest assertion names cite the ID.** KI-E51 barred narrative comments in the NEW test file only; KI-E55 extended that bar to ALL files a fix touches, while still permitting one narrow class of comment: a line stating a genuinely non-obvious invariant or provider quirk. It shipped as agent-brief text and was superseded the SAME day by the owner's absolute directive — KI-E57: zero new comments, no exceptions, edited pre-existing comments reverted verbatim (now the host-configurable `noNewComments` policy; see KI-E57). This row exists so the KI-E55 references in `build-test.sh`, `comment-lint.mjs`, the four agent briefs, and the selftest assertion names resolve instead of dangling. |
| KI-E56 | **`test-author.md` hardcoded "xUnit + FluentAssertions" as THE testing convention — factually wrong for at least one real target repo.** Live-verified 2026-07-30 across this factory's actual multi-repo production work: one real target repo uses NUnit (`[Test]`, `Assert.That(...)`, `Assert.EnterMultipleScope()`) throughout every one of its test files, while another uses xUnit (`[Fact]`) with FluentAssertions (`.Should()`) throughout every one of ITS test files — two real repos this same factory targets, two different frameworks, and the brief named only one of them as if it were universal. Since neither repo (nor any repo this factory has been pointed at so far) ships a `code-style.md` this claim could have been sourced from, a test-author session with less direct scrutiny than a controlling session gives could plausibly write xUnit-shaped assertions into an NUnit project (or vice versa) — a compile-breaking or at minimum idiom-violating mistake with no gate specifically positioned to catch a wrong-framework choice before build. Fix: `test-author.md` no longer names a specific framework/assertion library at all — it now requires reading a sibling test file in the SAME target project FIRST and matching its actual framework/assertion/fixture idiom exactly, every time, with the two confirmed real conventions cited as the concrete example of why the assumption is unsafe. |
| KI-E57 | **KI-E51/KI-E55's comment policy still allowed a narrow "legitimate non-obvious constraint" comment through — the owner's actual rule is stricter: ZERO new comments, no exceptions, full stop.** Live-caught 2026-07-30 (a real production item, immediately after the KI-E55 fix shipped): the owner reviewed the resulting PR and issued an explicit, repeated, all-caps directive that NO comment may ever be added by this factory's output — not even one meeting KI-E55's "states a real invariant/provider-quirk" bar — and that any PRE-EXISTING comment a diff had edited/reworded must be REVERTED to its original text (not rewritten to stay accurate), even when that means the reverted comment now describes stale/superseded behavior — an explicitly ACCEPTED trade-off, not an oversight. A live worktree audit under the new rule found and removed 13 comment additions in one already-"KI-E55-clean" real target repo's diff that KI-E55's narrower bar had not flagged as violations, confirming the two bars are materially different, not just a wording tweak. Fix: `fixer.md`/`test-author.md`/`gate-developer.md`/`review-code.md` all upgraded from "no NARRATIVE comments" to "no comments, full stop, including reverting edited pre-existing ones verbatim" — the KI-E55/KI-E51 IDs are kept (this is a strengthening of the same rule, not a new class) and this row exists to make the escalation and its date explicit for anyone reading KI-E55 and assuming it is still the operative bar. **HOST-CONFIGURABLE (this same change), not a universal engine absolute:** the zero-comments directive is the ORIGINATING host's rule — another host's own engineering rules REQUIRE certain comments (divergence call-site tags, dependency-justification comments, Arrange/Act/Assert markers) and treat EF migrations as the canonical change mechanism (the KI-E58 rule), so shipping the absolutes unconditionally broke every host but the originating one. `config/factory.config.json` now ships `"policies": { "noNewComments": false, "noSchemaChanges": false }` — both OFF in the public engine; a host enables them in its gitignored `config/factory.config.local.json` (the KI-E17 overlay seam); `lib/policy.mjs`'s `loadPolicies()` is the single loader; the driver prints the effective policy state at every group/sweep; and the briefs' policy sections are conditional — a HOST POLICY block is injected into prompts only when the policy is enabled. |
| KI-E58 | **No brief anywhere in this factory ever told a fixer/planner that a DB/schema change (a migration, a new/renamed/removed column or table) is out of bounds** — the only existing hard-stop class was `product-scope.md` (a business/legal red-line), which schema changes are not, so a fixer proposing an "additive, nullable column on a shared entity" to fully close a correctness finding had no rule telling it that was off-limits. Live-caught 2026-07-30 (a real production item): a real, gate-surfaced MEDIUM finding whose most complete, textbook-correct fix required persisting a correlation token on a shared entity's table — which the owner explicitly refused ("DB - not allowed") when asked to choose between that and accepting the residual risk as analyzed/documented. Fix: `fixer.md` and `planner.md` both gain an explicit HARD STOP (mirroring the product-scope.md one in shape, but a DISTINCT class — narrower/expected, not a business red-line): no migrations, no schema changes, ever, even a seemingly-safe additive nullable field on a shared entity; when only a schema change would fully close a finding, implement the best fix possible within the EXISTING schema and record the residual gap as an accepted, documented trade-off (an architecture-doc entry — never a code comment, per KI-E57) rather than a scope-stop. **HOST-CONFIGURABLE (this same change):** like the comments rule, this is the originating host's directive, not a universal engine absolute — other hosts treat EF migrations as the CANONICAL change mechanism — so it now ships as the `noSchemaChanges` policy: OFF in the public engine's `config/factory.config.json`, enabled per host in the gitignored `config/factory.config.local.json`, loaded by the same `lib/policy.mjs` `loadPolicies()` seam (KI-E57), printed with the effective policy state at every group/sweep, and injected into prompts as the conditional HOST POLICY block only when enabled. |
| KI-E59 | **KI-E57's absolute no-comments rule was prompt-only — nothing MECHANICALLY enforced it, so a slip past the fixer/gate-developer/review-code prompts was only ever caught by luck (or, live, by the owner reading the PR by hand).** The owner's next instruction made the remaining gap explicit: beyond cleaning up the PR comments already landed, the ask was for MECHANISMS that prevent comments going forward, not just more prompt rules — the mechanism half is exactly this gap. KI-E51/KI-E55/KI-E57 are all agent-brief text, and the KI-D12 LeftoverScan precedent already proved prompt-only rules are insufficient alone under load. Fix: a deterministic detector `lib/comment-scan.mjs` (`findComments`/`classifyCommentLine`, mirroring `lib/leftover-scan.mjs`'s shape — scanning the worktree diff's ADDED lines + untracked new files) wired as a THREE-layer mechanical gate, all three layers running only when the host policy `noNewComments` (KI-E57) is ON. Hardened against real-world false-positive classes in this same change: (a) pure MOVES/re-indents are suppressed — an added comment line whose trimmed text byte-matches a REMOVED line anywhere in the same diff is a relocation, not a new/reworded comment (previously a file-scoped-namespace conversion hard-failed with impossible compliance); (b) string literals are stripped before matching — killing the `"//"`-inside-a-literal, protocol-relative-URL, base64 and csproj `Include="**/*.cs"` glob false positives; (c) the scan is extension-aware — `.md`/`.txt`/`.json` are not scanned, `<!-- -->` counts only in markup files, and `#` (shell/yaml/ps1/py), `--` (sql), `@* *@` (razor) and `(* *)` (F#) are covered where they are real comment syntax; (d) untracked files are read via `readFileSync` — the old `execFileSync('cat')` was a silent no-op on Windows, the very platform the gate was born on; (e) a scan failure reports an explicit `FACTORY::COMMENT-SCAN-ERROR` marker, never a silent count 0. Unlike LeftoverScan there is deliberately NO classify step anywhere — the enabled policy admits zero legitimate exceptions, so any hit is an immediate hard violation, checked BEFORE leftover-scan in both runtimes: (1) native `factory.js` — a new fail-open `comment-probe` haiku call (`COMMENT_SCHEMA`) runs `build-test.sh comments` and reports the deterministic count back VERBATIM (no judgment), FAILing the item pre-band on count>0, same fail-open posture as `leftover-probe` (a malformed/unavailable probe never sinks an item alone — layer 3 is the backstop); (2) the opencode port `runtime.mjs` — fully mechanical, ZERO LLM, importing `findComments` directly (no bash dependency) and treating a scan error as gate-unavailable, NEVER as clean, immediately FAILing on any hit before leftover-scan even runs; (3) fold-time — a WARN-only backstop in `driver.mjs` (`findComments`, mirrors the KI-D12/F2 posture exactly: a detection aid, never a fold-blocker) re-greps every folding CLOSED/INTEGRATED result's worktree independent of any LLM verdict; this should never fire, since layers 1/2 and the KI-E57 gate-band prompts all already check it, but an unmissable WARN exists if it ever does. `verify/build-test.sh comments <worktree>` / `_workflow/comment-lint.mjs` is the CLI surface, mirroring `leftovers`/`leftover-lint.mjs` in shape — and `build-test.sh` now handles `comments` (and `leftovers`) BEFORE the `build-test.local.sh` host-override seam, so a pre-existing host override can no longer silently neutralize the gate (previously an old override's usage error parsed as count 0 → a false APPROVED); count markers are parsed anchored + last-match, with the exit code consulted. Selftest-pinned: detector unit tests (hit/no-hit, move-suppression, string-literal and extension-routing cases), static wiring checks across all three layers, and a REAL exec-smoke run of `factory.js`'s actual pipeline logic proving both the short-circuit ordering (a comment-probe hit FAILs before the leftover-probe call ever runs) and the clean CLOSED/gate-recorded happy path. |
| KI-E60 | **KI-E56 fixed ONE symptom (a hardcoded test-framework assumption) but left the root cause standing: every agent brief in `agents/*.md` is ONE universal, shared set of role instructions, and this factory targets multiple real, materially different repos — the very repos KI-E56 describes (one NUnit, one xUnit+FluentAssertions) also differ in naming conventions, comment norms, mocking libraries, and dozens of other real idioms that KI-E56's narrower "read a sibling test file" workaround does not reach.** Fix: a new, purely-ADDITIVE per-repo overlay — `agents/repo-profiles/<target>.md`, one optional file per real target repo, auto-derived from that repo's own recent merged PRs (profiles exist for all four real target repos this factory's hosts point it at, and more land as research covers further repos — but they are HOST-LOCAL data, never engine content: `agents/repo-profiles/` is gitignored except `README.md` + a fictional `_example.*.md` template, because a real profile distills a private repo's conventions and must never enter the engine repo, per KI-E26) — is read alongside the universal `agents/*.md` brief and injected as a clearly-labelled "REPO-SPECIFIC STYLE PROFILE" block AFTER it, never in place of it: the universal briefs remain the default/authoritative source for EVERY repo, and this must never regress — a target with NO profile file behaves EXACTLY as it did before this mechanism existed, the same silent-no-op best-effort target-keyed-lookup posture `buildDocMap`/`solutionFor` already use (a missing file is normal, never an error, never required). Wired everywhere a role prompt is composed — since a review fix in this same change, that includes the SWEEP and SELECT lanes (the first cut injected on group/recover only, contradicting this row's own "wired everywhere" claim): `lib/promptpack.mjs`'s `readRepoProfiles()` mirrors `readRoleBriefs()` exactly (dir-listed, `.md`-only, capped, `{}` on any failure); `driver.mjs` inlines the map into the batch on the group, sweep, and select lanes (`runArgs`, a sibling of the existing `briefs:` key) and separately in the `recover` regate builder (its own per-role prompt string, since that path never builds a `runArgs` object at all), because the sandboxed Workflow runtime (`factory.js`) has no filesystem access of its own to read the file directly (KI-E2); `factory.js`'s `compose()` reads the pre-inlined `A.repoProfiles[item.target]`; the opencode port `compose.mjs` (real fs access, KI-O1) instead reads `agents/repo-profiles/<target>.md` fresh off disk on every call, for EVERY role including probes *(correction, KI-E112: this row previously said "scoped to non-probe roles only exactly like its own brief-reading block". That was factually wrong — the profile block sits OUTSIDE compose.mjs's `!/-probe$/` branch, and both the port's own comment and its selftest assert the opposite. The brief-reading block IS probe-scoped; the profile block is not.)*. Both runtimes cap an injected profile at the SAME exported `PROFILE_CAP` (review fix, this same change: the native path silently truncated at 12000 chars — 3 of the first 4 live profiles lost 30-53% of their content — while the opencode path injected them whole). Every injection carries the same override notice: a profile's own observed facts win over generic assumptions for THIS repo, but the host's `noNewComments` policy (KI-E57), when enabled, always applies regardless of what a profile's own "comment usage observed" section reports about a team's real-world habits. |
| KI-E61 | **KI-L65's main-tree contamination detection (KI-E41/E45/E50/E35) was WARN-only across four separate hardening passes and never found the actual trigger mechanism — because every pass treated cycle-35's incident as agent non-compliance ("the brief already forbids this") rather than re-examining whether a LATER prompt feature had reopened the exact ambiguity the brief's absolute WORKTREE/REPO-ROOT split was meant to close.** Live-caught 2026-08-02 (a real production item, ITEM-H18, cycle 56): the fold's KI-L65 check fired on `doc/data-flows/SomePortal.md` — present in the MAIN tree, uncommitted, changed. Root cause found by re-reading `lib/promptpack.mjs`'s `buildDocMap` (added 2026-07-18, *after* cycle 35's mainguard.mjs was written and its "detection, not another prompt rule" conclusion reached): the per-item DOC MAP feature hands out **bare-relative** paths — `doc/data-flows/<target>.md`, `<target>/CONTEXT.md`, `<target>/AGENTS.md` — with no anchor stated at the point of use, inside a prompt that separately says "read audit docs from the REPO ROOT." An agent correctly Reads a doc-map entry against REPO ROOT; when that SAME work item's acceptance criteria then requires EDITING that identical relative path (the dataflow.md doc-sync rule fires on most code-touching items), Edit naturally reuses the exact path string already in hand from the Read — a silent slide from "read-only reference" to "edited the main tree" with no rule ever knowingly broken. Retroactively explains ALL THREE prior incidents, not just this one: cycle 35's ITEM-H12/ITEM-H5 and cycle 48's ITEM-H16 (an "acceptance-named CONTEXT.md" — one of buildDocMap's own three candidate file types) are the identical shape. Two-part fix: (1) **prevention at the ambiguity** — `factory.js` and the opencode port's `compose.mjs` (verbatim-port parity) now render every DOC MAP entry REPO-ROOT-qualified, and any entry that is ALSO in the item's `files[]` touch-set gets an unmissable second line naming the exact worktree path to edit instead ("THIS path is ALSO in your files[] touch-set... do NOT Edit it. Your edit target... is: `<worktree>/<relpath>`"). (2) **auto-repair as defense in depth** — `mainguard.repairDirtyDrift` closes the loop KI-L65 deliberately left open ("repairing main is operator judgment"): splitDriftByStatus's OWN reasoning already proves dirty/uncommitted drift can only be agent contamination (the factory never commits, so a human's legitimate delivery always lands in the COMMITTED bucket, untouched) — there is no case where dirty drift is anything BUT a bug to fix, so both the fold-time and KI-E41 resume-time checks now restore a present-drifted file from HEAD or remove an absent-drifted stray immediately, and report what was auto-repaired (never silently). The mid-band KI-E50 `main-check` stays read-only/lock-free/report-only by its own explicit design (an in-flight lane's OWN uncommitted evidence would otherwise be at risk of a concurrent repair) — auto-repair applies only at the two points (fold, resume) where the item generating the drift is provably not still running. Behaviorally selftest-pinned: `repairDirtyDrift` restore/remove/failure-is-non-throwing cases, source-text parity between `factory.js` and the opencode port's docMap block, and driver wiring pins that both call sites actually invoke the repair (not just report it). |
| KI-E62 | **A mixed `group` batch (two or more disjoint similarity cliques) got NO batch-pattern stamp for ANY item, even when each clique on its own would have passed the strict all-pairs test — `batchPatternFor(picked)` is deliberately all-or-nothing (`similarity.mjs` § "STRICT all-pairs test"), so batching e.g. a doc-drift item alongside two pagination-clamp items silently stripped the sibling-convergence benefit from BOTH pairs, not just the item that broke the whole-batch clique.** Identified as a candidate in an internal parallelism/reuse analysis (never built until 2026-08-02). Fix: `similarity.mjs` adds `perCliqueBatchPatterns(items)` — clusters first via the pre-existing `clusterBySimilarity`, then calls `batchPatternFor` on each cluster independently, stamping only the clusters that qualify (≥2 members AND pass the strict all-pairs test within that sub-cluster). `driver.mjs`'s `cmdGroup` now builds a `Map<itemId, pattern>` via `perCliqueBatchPatterns(picked)` (replacing the single `batchPatternFor(picked)` call) and each item's `batchPattern` field is looked up per-item (`batchPatternById.get(wi.id)`) instead of forwarding one shared value; `--pattern`/`--no-pattern` operator overrides are unchanged (a forced pattern still stamps every item; `--no-pattern` still stamps none). Strictly additive by construction: when the WHOLE batch is one clique, `clusterBySimilarity` collapses to exactly one cluster (every pair being mutually similar makes them transitively connected), so `perCliqueBatchPatterns` reproduces the IDENTICAL stamp `batchPatternFor(items)` already produced — the pre-existing whole-batch-clique behavior cannot regress. Selftest-pinned: clique members get the same stamp text `batchPatternFor` would produce for that sub-clique alone, singletons and cross-clique items stay unstamped, the 4-item mixed-batch stamps exactly its one qualifying clique, and the whole-batch-is-one-clique parity case matches `batchPatternFor` byte-for-byte; driver wiring pins confirm `cmdGroup` calls `perCliqueBatchPatterns(picked)` and forwards via `batchPatternById.get(wi.id)`. **Follow-on caught in the same pass:** `cmdSuggest`'s own batch-pattern preview line (the advisory text shown before the `group --ids ...` command it prints) still called the retired whole-batch-only `batchPatternFor(batch)` and would print "none (mixed shapes — group will not stamp)" for a `pick(false)` fallback batch (file-disjoint, not all-pairs similar) even in cases where `group` would now actually apply a per-clique stamp to a qualifying sub-clique within that exact batch — the preview and the real behavior it exists to predict would have silently diverged the moment KI-E62 shipped. Fixed by switching the preview to `perCliqueBatchPatterns(batch)` and reporting three distinct states: no qualifying sub-clique ("none"), the whole batch qualifies ("AUTO, whole batch"), or a genuine partial ("AUTO, per-clique (KI-E62): N/M member(s) would be stamped"). This also left `batchPatternFor` itself with zero remaining call sites in `driver.mjs` (only `perCliqueBatchPatterns`, which lives in and calls it from `similarity.mjs`, is used directly) — the now-dead import was removed. **Second follow-on, more serious, live-caught:** the `batchPattern` → `batchPatternById` rename left ONE bare `batchPattern` reference far below the rename site, inside the `run_prepared` telemetry `temit(...)` call (`attrs: { ..., batchPattern: !!batchPattern, ... }`) — a `ReferenceError` that crashed EVERY real `driver.mjs group` invocation (dry or not) the instant it reached that line, i.e. the single most heavily-used command in the factory. Source-text selftest pins on the rename sites did NOT catch this — they assert substrings exist, they never execute `cmdGroup`. Caught only by an actual `driver.mjs group --max N --dry` smoke run performed as a final check before considering the change done — the lesson generalizes: any `driver.mjs` change that renames or removes a variable MUST be live-smoke-tested with a real (`--dry` is sufficient) invocation of the affected command, not just selftest-source-pinned, because Node throws at the FIRST executed reference to an undefined binding, and a purely textual pin cannot see execution order or reachability. Fixed to `batchPattern: batchPatternById.size > 0` (true when the batch produced at least one per-clique stamp) and selftest-pinned so this exact bare-reference shape cannot silently regress. |
| KI-E63 | **A `group` batch got zero benefit from the 167-and-growing CLOSED corpus: a fixer facing e.g. a doc-drift or pagination-clamp item had no way to discover that a gate-APPROVED instance of the exact same change-shape already shipped, so every item re-derived its approach from scratch even when a proven, reviewed template already existed.** Identified as a candidate in an internal parallelism/reuse analysis ("cheapest high-leverage reuse … immediately monetizes the CLOSED corpus", never built until 2026-08-02). Fix: `similarity.mjs` adds `bestClosedPrecedent(item, closedCandidates)` — a pure, one-item-vs-pool scan (no clustering needed) over already-CLOSED siblings, matched the same way batch-pattern matching is (same `theme` + `similarSigs`), preferring the MOST RECENTLY closed qualifying match. `driver.mjs`'s `cmdGroup` builds the candidate pool once per `group` call by cross-referencing `ledger.items` (`state === 'CLOSED'`) against `byId(graph)` for `theme`/`title`/`target`, looks up a precedent for each picked item, and stamps `item.precedent = {id, target, title, fixJson?, worktree?}` — **but only when at least one of `state/items/<id>/fix.json` or `state/worktrees/<id>/` is ACTUALLY present on disk right now** (`existsSync`-checked at stamp time). This existence gate is load-bearing, not defensive theatre: investigating this candidate found that NEITHER artifact class is durably guaranteed — worktree `gc` is explicit-opt-in (`driver.mjs gc --yes`), and on this very mount only 6 of 167 CLOSED items still had a `state/items/<id>/` directory at all (the rest were evidently cleared by an operator or process outside the factory's own code, which never deletes `state/items/`) — so a precedent stamp pointing at a dead path would be worse than no stamp (an unfollowable instruction). Silent-no-op when nothing qualifies, matching the `buildDocMap`/`solutionFor` convention (KI-E60): never an error, never required. Rendered into the prompt by `factory.js` and ported verbatim to `opencode/compose.mjs` (KI-O1 parity) as a `PRECEDENT` block, explicitly framed as READ-ONLY reference material in a *different* item's directory ("never Edit it, never touch its files") — the same ambiguity lesson KI-E61 already codified for the DOC MAP block, applied here even though the risk is lower (a precedent path can never coincide with the current item's own `files[]` touch-set). Selftest-pinned: the pure function's most-recent-match / cross-theme-exclusion / self-id-exclusion / empty-and-null-pool behavior; `cmdGroup` wiring (candidate-pool construction, the existence gate, per-item forwarding); source-text parity between `factory.js` and `compose.mjs` for the `PRECEDENT` block and its read-only framing. Independently verified against real ledger/graph data (166 of 167 CLOSED rows have a live graph entry; a synthetic item shaped like the real ITEM-H10 correctly resolved ITEM-H10 as its precedent with both `fix.json` and the worktree confirmed present) and via multiple live `driver.mjs group --dry` invocations (default, `--no-pattern`, `--pattern`, themed filters) with zero crashes. |
| KI-E64 | **`graph-audit`'s KI-E16 shared-file-gap lint has carried the wrong ledger filename since its introduction (2026-07-20): `LEDGER_PATH = '_bmad-output/tech-debt/STANDARDS-LEDGER.md'` — missing "DIVERGENCE" — a path that has NEVER existed on disk (only `STANDARDS-DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md` does). Every item whose acceptance names a ledger entry/anchor was flagged `SHARED-FILE-GAP` regardless of whether its `files[]` already correctly carried the real path, because `!(wi.files||[]).includes(LEDGER_PATH)` could never be false against a constant nothing could ever match — and `graph-audit --fix` would have silently APPENDED the nonexistent path into files[] instead of the real ledger, worsening rather than fixing the lint's own target.** Found live 2026-08-02 while merging a new batch of 45 standards-divergence-ledger-derived work items (a large batch-prep pass): a fresh `graph-audit` run reported 47 shared-file gaps — 45 of them on the brand-new items, whose `files[]` demonstrably ALREADY contained the correct `_bmad-output/tech-debt/STANDARDS-DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md` path (independently confirmed via direct JSON inspection: 45/45 items matching the lint's own trigger regex already had the real path in `files[]`), plus 2 more on pre-existing, unrelated items across several other target repos that had been silently mis-flagged since the day this lint shipped. Fix: corrected the constant to `_bmad-output/tech-debt/STANDARDS-DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md`; also widened the detection regex from the literal `STANDARDS-LEDGER` to `STANDARDS-(?:DIVERGENCE-)?LEDGER` (the OR'd `ledger entr(?:y\|ies)`/`ledger anchor` fallbacks already covered most real prose, which is why the bug went undetected for two weeks — the DETECTION side mostly worked via those fallbacks; only the PATH-COMPARISON side was structurally broken). Re-running `graph-audit` after the fix: shared-file gap count dropped from 47 to 0 (every real reference, old and new, already has the correct path — confirming this was a pure false-positive bug, not a real backlog of un-appended ledger references). Selftest-pinned: the corrected `LEDGER_PATH` constant's exact value. |
| KI-E65 | **A fold result's `toState` and the last element of its `transitions` array are two independent fields with no structural link — `foldResults` walks `transitions` as the primary source of truth and only falls back to `[toState]` when `transitions` is empty, so a result that sets `toState:'FAILED'` while leaving `transitions` ending in the skeleton's default success path (`'...,CLOSED'`) silently CLOSES the item instead of failing it.** Live-caught 2026-08-03 during the cycle-57 recovery pass for the `SDL-*` standards-divergence batch: `recoveryFoldSkeleton` pre-fills a new recovery's `transitions` array with the full `RED..CLOSED` success chain (correct for the common case where the fold-prep step only needs to fill placeholders when the recovery succeeds), and a fold-prep agent instructed to "set `toState: 'FAILED'`" on a still-failing item (ITEM-30 — one re-gate returned `CHANGES_REQUIRED`, `adjudicator: UPHELD`) correctly wrote `toState` but — never told to also truncate `transitions` — left it unchanged. Folding it walked the row straight through to `CLOSED`, silently recording a genuinely-still-broken item (a newly-introduced flaky test in `RefundCompletedConsumerTests.cs`) as done. Caught only because the operator independently cross-checked the folded ledger state against the recovery note's own prose (`grep`-visible contradiction: note said "toState is FAILED, not CLOSED" while `ledger.items['ITEM-30'].state === 'CLOSED'`) — nothing in the fold pipeline itself would have surfaced it. Corrected by hand (`canTransition` has no legal `CLOSED->FAILED` or `CLOSED->READY` edge for `reset`/a corrective fold to use — a narrow, disclosed, one-off ledger hand-edit was the only path back to a true state, logged as an `OPERATOR CORRECTION` history entry on the row). Fix: `lib/ledger.mjs` adds `reconcileToStateAndTransitions(r)` — a pure function called in `cmdFold` for every result BEFORE the deterministic-verify pass and `foldResults` (both of which also read `transitions`/`toState`). No-ops when the two already agree (or either is absent). When they disagree: if `toState` appears earlier in `transitions`, truncate there (the array over-ran its own stated ending); if `toState` does not appear at all (the live shape above), walk backwards from the end of `transitions` for the last state that is a legal jumping-off point for `toState` per `canTransition` (skipping `CLOSED`/`INTEGRATED`, neither of which can reach `FAILED`; landing on the last `ACTIVE` state, e.g. `REAUDITED`, which can), truncate there, and append `toState`. Every correction is reported loudly (`⚠ TOSTATE/TRANSITIONS MISMATCH ... AUTO-CORRECTED`, same posture as KI-E61's dirty-drift auto-repair) — never a silent fix-up.
