Selftest-pinned: all four no-op shapes (null result, no `transitions`, no `toState`, already-consistent), the mid-array-truncation case, the live ITEM-30 shape's exact backward-walk-and-append output, and an end-to-end regression pin that runs the exact live-bug shape through `reconcileToStateAndTransitions` + the real `foldResults` and asserts the row lands on `FAILED`, not `CLOSED`. |
| KI-E66 | **Cache-hit-rate telemetry did not exist ANYWHERE in the factory's own event stream — and its intended source (two Grafana panels, KI-E28) had been permanently empty on every host observed to date, including this one, with no cue why beyond a preflight message.** `claude_code_token_usage_tokens_total` (session-side OTLP) is the ONLY place cache-read/creation/input/output token typing exists at all; per KI-E33 it requires `OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER=otlp` + an OTLP endpoint, which this host had never actually set (`CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TELEMETRY=1` alone was present) — independently confirmed live by querying Prometheus directly: zero `claude_code_*` metric names registered despite the `telemetry/` compose stack (grafana/prometheus/exporter/otel-collector) running continuously for 25+ hours. Separately, true PER-ITEM token measurement was investigated and found not available at all: the Workflow runtime's `budget` global exposes only a whole-turn/whole-workflow output-token counter (`budget.spent()`, the existing KI-E23 `usage` event's basis) with no per-call or per-item split, and a direct empirical test (dispatching a marker-tagged subagent and inspecting the local session transcript) confirmed a Workflow-internal `agent()` call's own token cost is not independently recoverable from outside the sandboxed runtime either — corroborated by this factory's own multi-month development history never achieving better than a whole-run total despite KI-E23 explicitly wanting per-item granularity. Fix, two parts: (1) **cycle-scoped bridge** — `lib/token-usage.mjs` (`parseTokenUsageVector`, `cacheHitRate` — the SAME ratio already authored in the dashboard's "Prompt-cache hit ratio" panel, `cacheRead / (cacheRead + cacheCreation + input)`, reused rather than inventing a second definition — `tokenUsageSummary`, `buildTokenUsageQuery`) + `driver.mjs`'s `queryPrometheusTokenUsage` (best-effort `fetch`, zero new deps per AD-4, 5s timeout, never throws) wired into `telemetry-report` (not the hot fold path — AD-1's "runs to completion with the stack down" stays true): on success it emits a `token_usage_snapshot` event (`source:'driver'`) into `events.jsonl` so the durable AD-9 record carries it, not just a live Grafana view; on any failure (unreachable, empty result, malformed response) it resolves to nothing gathered — a bare 0%/0-token result is never fabricated (the exact KI-E40 silent-miscount lesson, applied here before it had the chance to repeat). (2) **disclosed per-item apportionment** — since real per-item measurement is unavailable, `apportionTokensByCallShare` divides each cycle's REAL, measured output-token total (KI-E23) across that cycle's items by their REAL, measured call-count share (`item_folded.attrs.cost`, already accurate) — rendered under a column/section explicitly labelled "estimate — NOT a measurement" in both the report and this row, never presented as measured data. Also fixed as a prerequisite, same session: this host's `setup/install.sh telemetry-up --shell-env --yes` had written the marker-guarded shell-profile block + merged the project `settings.local.json` env block at some earlier point, but the RUNNING session predated both (KI-E33's own documented "session predates the settings edit" class) — re-run to confirm both are now in place for the next session; `driver.mjs preflight`'s `cost telemetry: NOT gathered` message is exactly the correct, honest signal for a still-old session and needs no further fix. `renderTelemetryReport` gains "## Session token usage & cache hit rate" and "## Per-item apportioned tokens" sections; `aggregateEvents` gains `itemCallCounts` (cycle-keyed, so a retried item's later-cycle calls never dilute an earlier cycle's apportionment) and `tokenUsageSnapshots`. Selftest: 780 → 802 (`lib/token-usage.mjs` pure-function coverage incl. the exact dashboard formula match, the range-vector latest-sample case, and every zero/empty edge case; `aggregateEvents`/`renderTelemetryReport` integration incl. the cycle-keyed apportionment and the honest NOT-gathered fallback when no snapshot exists). |

| KI-E67 | **An item's own artifact prose can read as confidently DONE while the deterministic fold verdict is FAILED, with nothing surfacing the contradiction — a concrete instance of the factory's single largest recurring rejection driver (agent self-report vs machine-verifiable evidence, KI-E19/E38/E43/E51/E54) that cost real investigation time to disentangle.** Found live 2026-08-03 while auditing ITEM-22 (`FAILED — deterministic override: "integrate transcript: 406 new suite failure(s) beyond baseline 0"`): its `state/items/ITEM-22/` directory carries a non-canonical `VERIFICATION-REPORT.md` — a confident, 10-point "✅ COMPLETE — All acceptance criteria met, zero leftovers, build passes... Ready for merge... No additional changes required" — directly contradicting the FAILED state. (A prior hypothesis that this file was CONTAMINATION from an unrelated item, per a same-session research pass, was independently checked and disproven: `findings-graph.json` confirms ITEM-22's own real title/acceptance IS the admin-portal orphan-count-header feature this file describes — the file is genuine, if non-canonically-named, output for THIS item, not debris. Likewise, that same research pass's claim that a specific recent commit "accidentally committed real generated secrets into k8s" was checked directly against the commit's actual diff and found FALSE — the commit only edited placeholder text (`REPLACE_WITH_32_PLUS_RANDOM_CHARS` → `..._OR_FAIL_VALIDATION`) and a config key rename; HEAD still carries only placeholders. Both corrections are recorded here so neither false lead is chased again.) Fix: `lib/narrative-check.mjs`'s `detectNarrativeVerdictContradiction(toState, textsByFile)` — pure, deliberately conservative (fires ONLY on toState FAILED/ESCALATED, matching ONLY a small set of strong, unambiguous completion phrases: "✅ complete", "all acceptance criteria met", "ready for merge", "no additional changes required", "zero leftovers", a "Status: ... complete" line) — wired as a `cmdFold` WARN-only detection aid (same posture as F2/KI-D12/KI-E59: never a fold-blocker, the deterministic override remains the verdict) that scans EVERY `.md` file already sitting in a FAILED/ESCALATED result's `state/items/<id>/` directory, canonical or not — deliberately not limited to the canonical artifact list, since the live case was exactly a non-canonical file. Selftest-pinned: fires only on FAILED/ESCALATED (never CLOSED, where upbeat language is simply an accurate description), each marker phrase individually, a non-matching narrative producing no hit, and the live ITEM-22 shape end-to-end. Selftest: 802 → 816. |

| KI-E68 | **`cmdGroup`'s per-run agent-concurrency default was a bare hardcoded `2` in `driver.mjs`, completely disconnected from `config/factory.config.json`'s `concurrency` block — the block's `throttled`/`normal`/`max`/`default` tiers and its own guidance note ("2-3 under active throttle, 5-8 when quiet") were pure unread documentation, never consulted by any code path.** Found live 2026-08-03: the operator, watching cycle-58's 12-item batch stall in the review-gate band (11/12 items already through rootCause/red/fix/build, ~60+ independent review/gate agent calls queued across them) at a global concurrency of 2, asked why parallelism was so low and to raise it. `run-script.js`'s inlined `const CONC = A.concurrency || 2` traced straight back to `cmdGroup`'s `runArgs.concurrency: flags.conc ? parseInt(flags.conc,10) : 2` — a second, independent magic number never reconciled with the config's own documented tiers. Fix: `cmdGroup` now defaults to `cfg.concurrency.default` (raised 3→6, i.e. the config's own `normal` tier) with a `6` literal fallback only if the field is ever absent; `--conc` still overrides per-invocation for an explicit active-throttle/quiet judgment call. This closes the doc-vs-code gap in the SAME direction the operator asked for (6) rather than just picking a second disconnected number. In-flight run recovery: the ALREADY-CLAIMED cycle-58 batch's generated `state/run-script.js` had its inlined `"concurrency":2` hand-edited to `6` and relaunched via `Workflow({scriptPath, resumeFromRunId})` — the runtime's own cache-by-(prompt,opts) resume semantics replayed all 62 already-completed agent() calls for free and only the queued-but-not-started review-gate calls picked up the new ceiling; no worktree/claim/fold state was touched or lost. Selftest: 816 → 819 (3 source-text pins: `cmdGroup` reads `cfg.concurrency.default` rather than a bare literal, the old disconnected hardcoded-2 default is gone, and `config.concurrency.default` itself reads 6 — mirroring the KI-E14/KI-E29 cmdGroup source-pin style since `cmdGroup` itself has no pure-function harness to execute directly). |

| KI-E69 | **A killed Workflow run's per-stage artifacts (plan.md, test.json, fix.json) are already durably written to `state/items/<id>/` the moment each role completes — independent of the KI-L40 end-of-item `result.json` checkpoint, which only fires if the item's WHOLE lifecycle resolves — yet the sanctioned relaunch ("relaunch the same run-script verbatim", SKILL.md § Recovery) re-ran plan → test-author → fixer from scratch for every relaunch candidate regardless of how much of that already-expensive generative work was sitting on disk, complete and untouched, in the item's own worktree.** Witnessed live 2026-08-03 (the same cycle-58 batch KI-E68 raised concurrency for, now killed by a sudden host shutdown before ever reaching a checkpoint): `resume` reported 12 CLAIMED items with 50/42/32/19/13/11/10/10/8/7/7/0 changed files already sitting in their worktrees — for the items with real progress, the ONLY thing between them and a fold was re-running the downstream review/gate band, yet the existing recovery path would have discarded plan/test/fix entirely and paid full price again. Investigated and found a real correctness trap along the way: `state/items/ITEM-H6/verify.json` (a real on-disk artifact) carries rich `{result, errors, warnings, ...}` objects for `build`/`targetedTest`, but `runItem()`'s own code reads them as plain pass/fail STRINGS (`if (!/^pass/i.test(String(verify.build)))`) — `String({result:"pass",...})` evaluates to `"[object Object]"`, which would have silently misclassified a genuinely-passing build as FAILED. `test.json`/`fix.json`, by contrast, were directly verified to carry plain, schema-matching values for every field `runItem()` actually reads. Fix, scoped to what is provably safe: `lib/prior-attempt.mjs`'s `loadPriorAttempt(itemDir, sinceMs)` reads back `test.json`/`fix.json` (freshness-gated: mtime must be at or after the item's CURRENT claim timestamp from ledger history, never a stale prior cycle's leftovers) and reconstructs a safe stand-in for `plan` (`{recommendScopeStop:false, recommendEscalate:false}` — the ONLY two fields `runItem()` reads from it — correct by construction whenever `test.json` also exists, since either flag would have short-circuited the item before test-authoring ever ran). `verify` is deliberately NEVER reused — always re-run fresh, which is also the safety net that independently re-confirms the reused fix still builds/passes on this worktree. Everything from the editorial pass onward (editorial, edge-scan, acceptance-scan, the full gate band, refute, reaudit, integrate) is completely unchanged and always re-runs fresh, preserving the existing, explicitly-stated design principle that gates re-adjudicate the worktree on every relaunch. Wired behind an explicit opt-in: `driver.mjs resume --reuse` (a plain `resume` is byte-for-byte unchanged) regenerates the relaunch candidates' `run-args`/`run-script` with `item.priorAttempt` populated per item, then re-emits the launcher via the existing `emitLauncherScript` (no duplication of `cmdGroup`'s pick/worktree/dep-check pipeline — the ALREADY-CLAIMED items' fully-built objects already live in the original run-args.json; this only enriches and re-serializes them). `runItem()` in `factory.js` consults `item.priorAttempt.{plan,test,fix}` at exactly those three call sites and nowhere else. `res.priorAttemptReuse` is always present on the result (an empty array IS the "nothing reused" signal, never silently absent) and rides the `item_folded` telemetry event so reuse is measurable, never silent. Known, disclosed gap: the opencode runtime port (`_workflow/opencode/`, KI-O1) does not yet consult `item.priorAttempt` — out of scope here since the batch this fixes runs on the primary Workflow-based runtime; a future port pass should mirror the same three call sites. Selftest: 819 → 838 (`lib/prior-attempt.mjs` pure-function coverage incl. the fresh/stale mtime boundary, malformed-JSON isolation, and the plan stand-in's dependency on test.json also being present; source-text pins confirming `runItem()`'s three call sites, the `--reuse` opt-in gate, and the `item_folded` telemetry wiring). |

| KI-E70 | **`parseVerifyRaw`'s `lastMatch` on `FACTORY::TEST::FILTER::RESULT` assumes every occurrence in a transcript is a RETRY of the SAME test class (cycle-8's fail-then-pass precedent) — but a runner legitimately filtering SEVERAL DIFFERENT test classes in one verify pass (not a retry loop) hits the identical marker shape, so the deterministic override's "targeted regression test did not pass" message named no class at all, sending the reader hunting for the wrong test.** Live on ITEM-H1, 2026-08-07 (the cycle-58 relaunch batch): `verify-raw.txt` shows 5 sequential `FILTER::START`/`FILTER::RESULT` pairs for 5 DIFFERENT classes (`ClassATests`, `ClassBTests`, `ClassCTests`, `ClassDTests`, `ClassETests`) — the first 4 passed, the last (`ClassETests`) failed on a real `EntityFrameworkCore.Storage.RelationalConnection.OpenInternalAsync` exception against a `FACTORY::REALINFRA::Testcontainers-postgres` container. The agent's own schema-validated `targetedTest` return field genuinely said "pass" (truthfully describing the FIRST/nominally-"targeted" class), so the in-run check (factory.js) let it through to gates; only the fold-time deterministic override caught the LAST filter's exit=1 — correctly, but reported it as "targeted regression test did not pass" with no indication it wasn't even the same class the runner named as its target. Fix: `parseVerifyRaw` now also collects every `FACTORY::TEST::FILTER::START $target :: $filter` class name (build-test.sh's own emission format) in order and, when the LAST `FILTER::RESULT` fails, pairs it with the LAST captured class name (1:1 by emission order — build-test.sh always emits exactly one START before its RESULT/SUMMARY per filter invocation) into a new `targetedFailClass` field; `verdictFromParse`'s reason string appends `(<ClassName>)` when available, degrading to the old unqualified message when no START marker exists (legacy/bare transcripts). A same-class retry (the original cycle-8 shape) is unaffected — the class name is simply identical across attempts, so the message just becomes more specific, never wrong. Selftest: 838 → 844 (the ITEM-H1 multi-class shape end-to-end, a same-class-retry-with-name case, and the no-START-marker-present degrade-gracefully case). |

| KI-E71 | **`leftover-raw.txt` — the KI-D12 leftover-scan probe's OWN canonical artifact (`factory.js`: `res.artifacts['probe:leftover-scan'] = 'state/items/' + id + '/leftover-raw.txt'`) — was missing from `lib/telemetry.mjs`'s `STAGE_ARTIFACTS`, so `nonCanonicalArtifacts()`/`resume`'s KI-E42 debris detector false-positived "NON-CANONICAL artifacts from the dead attempt" on it for EVERY item that reached that stage, live run or dead, diluting the signal for genuinely-improvised files sitting alongside it.** Found live 2026-08-07 monitoring the cycle-58 relaunch batch: every `resume` check from the item's leftover-scan onward flagged `leftover-raw.txt`, even for items with fresh sub-2-minute-old writes elsewhere in the same directory (an actively-running item, not a dead one). Investigated `leftover-final.txt` (flagged alongside it on the same items) as a second candidate for the same fix — found ZERO references anywhere in `factory.js` or `agents/*.md`; it is genuine agent improvisation and correctly stays flagged, so only `leftover-raw.txt` was added to `STAGE_ARTIFACTS` (mapped to stage `probe:leftover-scan`, matching the label already used in `result.json`'s `artifacts` map). Selftest: 844 → 846 (`nonCanonicalArtifacts` no longer flags `leftover-raw.txt` while still flagging `leftover-final.txt`; `stageForArtifact('leftover-raw.txt')` returns `probe:leftover-scan`). |

| KI-E72 | **`preflight`'s `dotnetAvailable()` shells `dotnet --version` directly in the CONTROLLER session's own process env, but the REAL verify path every subagent uses (`verify/build-test.sh`) auto-delegates to a per-host `verify/build-test.local.sh` PATH shim (KI-E17 override seam, `SETUP.md § 6`) when one exists — so a controller session whose OWN shell never sources `~/.zshrc` (the non-interactive-shell class documented right in the shim's own header comment) reports `dotnet: ABSENT — build/test verify cannot run on this host` while every real verify call in the actual factory run succeeds via the shim.** Found live 2026-08-07: `driver.mjs preflight` reported ABSENT on a host that had already closed 180+ items that same session — independently confirmed the SDK/dotnet binary genuinely exists at `~/.dotnet/dotnet` (10.0.302) and `verify/build-test.local.sh` was present, executable, and correctly exporting `PATH`/`DOTNET_ROOT` before delegating. Fix: `lib/preflight.mjs` adds `shimAvailable(shimPath)` (pure, injectable — existence + executable-bit check) and `dotnetAvailable()` now returns true when EITHER the raw PATH probe succeeds OR the host shim resolves; `preflight()`'s return gains `dotnetViaShim` so `cmdPreflight`'s message distinguishes "available (on PATH)" from "available (via host shim — not on this session's own PATH, but every real verify call resolves it)" instead of collapsing both into a bare boolean. Live-verified against this host post-fix: `dotnet: available (via verify/build-test.local.sh host shim...)`. Selftest: 846 → 851 (`shimAvailable` pure coverage: executable/non-executable/nonexistent/empty-path cases via real temp fixtures, plus `dotnetAvailable(shimPath)` with an injected shim). |

| KI-E73 | **`cmdResume`'s checkpoint-visibility check hardcoded `cyc = ledger.cycle + 1`, even though its own comment said "same rule as reconstruct" — `cmdReconstruct` had ALREADY been fixed under KI-L63 to derive the relevant cycle from on-disk evidence (the MAX cycle among unfolded checkpoints), but that fix was never actually shared, so `cmdResume` silently drifted back to the naive +1 guess. A `resume --reuse` relaunch (KI-E69) keeps each item's OWN claim cycle rather than bumping to a new one, so a same-cycle checkpoint — the NORMAL shape for that recovery path — was invisible to `resume`'s in-flight listing.** Live-witnessed across the whole 2026-08-07 cycle-58 relaunch monitoring session: 7 separate items in that batch each finished with a complete, valid `result.json` and sat done-but-unfolded for tens of minutes apiece — `resume` never once flagged any of them as checkpointed (unlike a pre-existing, correctly-working case earlier the same session, where `resume` DID immediately flag one item's checkpoint because that checkpoint's cycle number happened to be strictly newer than the then-active cycle). Every occurrence required a manual `driver.mjs reconstruct` to discover. Fix: `deriveUnfoldedCycle(itemsRoot, ledger)` — the exact KI-L63 derivation logic — extracted out of `cmdReconstruct` into `lib/ledger.mjs` (pure, now unit-testable; it was previously inlined directly in `cmdReconstruct`'s body with zero test coverage of its own) and imported by BOTH `cmdResume` and `cmdReconstruct`, so the two call sites share one implementation and cannot drift apart again. Live-smoke-tested per the KI-E62 lesson (a driver.mjs change that moves/renames a binding must be exercised with a real invocation, not just selftest-pinned): `resume`, `reconstruct`, and `group --dry` all run cleanly post-change. Selftest: 851 → 858 (the exact same-cycle bug shape end-to-end, the already-folded exclusion, the KI-L63 multi-item MAX-wins case, a malformed-JSON item skipped gracefully, and 2 source-text pins confirming both call sites use the shared function with no re-inlined copy). |

| KI-E74 | **`decisions-digest`'s "Rule-together bundles" grouped parked owner-decisions ONLY by exact `target` (service) match, so the SAME underlying question recurring across DIFFERENT services was invisible to it — even though `cluster.mjs`'s `clusterBySimilarity` (theme + title-keyword overlap) already detects exactly this pattern for the SWEEP report.** Found live 2026-08-07: manual investigation of the 36-item parked queue found 7 items across 6 services (`ITEM-1`, `ITEM-20`, `ITEM-40`, `ITEM-41`, `ITEM-42`, `ITEM-44`, `ITEM-48`) were all the identical "build a shared `Shared.SharedTypes` assembly, or ratify permanent per-service duplication?" question — a single owner ruling would close all 7, but the digest scattered them as unbundled singleton rows because none of the 7 shared a `target`. Fix: `cmdDecisionsDigest` now ALSO runs the parked rows through the existing `clusterBySimilarity`/`sharedLabel` (already imported, already used for `suggest`/`group` batch-pattern stamping — no new classifier written), filtered to clusters spanning ≥2 DIFFERENT targets (same-target clusters stay owned by the pre-existing `byTarget` bundle, avoiding duplication). **Precision caveat surfaced during verification, not shipped silently**: live-testing against the real queue found the automated cluster (13 items / 10 services) is LARGER and more thorough than the 7-item manual count — it correctly also caught `ITEM-46` (a genuine match missed by hand) — but ALSO chain-merged 4 tangential items (`ITEM-6`, `ITEM-7`, `ITEM-10`, `ITEM-50`) that only share generic boilerplate phrasing ("owner ruling", "permanently unavailable" — common across this whole owner-decision batch's titles) with ONE other cluster member, not real subject-matter overlap; `clusterBySimilarity`'s union-find is chain-transitive (A~B~C merges even when A is not directly similar to C), which is an accepted tradeoff for its original SWEEP-triage purpose (a human reads every member individually before writing the canonical fix) but is a materially stronger, WRONG claim for decision-bundling ("one ruling closes all of these"). Rather than build a bespoke stricter clusterer, the section is worded as a candidate requiring verification — mirroring the existing KI-E36 "possibly already DELIVERED in HEAD — verify" posture — rather than an assertion; a partial ruling (some ids in the bundle, not all) is documented as the normal expected outcome. Selftest: 858 → 860 (2 source-text pins: the shared-clusterer wiring + cross-target filter, and the candidate/verify framing). |

| KI-E74B | **`VERIFY_SCHEMA` has always had a `note` field the runner MAY populate with an honest caveat, but `factory.js` captured the runner's structured return into a local `verify` variable and never read `verify.note` again anywhere — no gate, no review, no report. `review-pack.md` (the ONE artifact every review-role agent is told to read) is a pure git-diff snapshot with ZERO narrative capacity by design (its own generated header says "INPUT curation for reviewers, not an authored judgment"), so a runner's self-aware warning had NO channel to reach the review band at all.** Live on ITEM-H1, 2026-08-07 (the cycle-58 relaunch): the runner's own `verify.json` wrote, verbatim, "Build+targeted-test green here does NOT mean round 2's findings are resolved — they are not, and are unaddressed in this worktree as of this pass" — naming 5 specific unaddressed findings (a Development-boot crash, an unguarded malformed-BaseUrl DI-construction crash, an unbounded retry loop, retry-catch gaps, a timing-oracle amplification) from an EARLIER review round that no fixer round had ever touched. All 8 gates/reviews subsequently APPROVED anyway — none of them had any way to see that note, since it was never surfaced. The item would have folded CLOSED had an UNRELATED mechanism (KI-E70's deterministic transcript override, triggered by a completely different failing test class) not independently caught it. Fix: `factory.js` now sets `item.verifyNote = verify.note` immediately after the runner call (before any early return), and `compose()`'s review-role branch renders it prominently — "VERIFY-STAGE NOTE (from the runner's own verification pass — READ THIS, do not approve past it unverified)" — right after the REVIEW PACK instruction, framed as a hypothesis to independently re-verify (KI-L35 posture: never a conclusion to copy forward, since it may describe a stale/historical state). `agents/runner.md` gains an explicit instruction (mirroring the pre-existing fix-manifest-cross-check bullet's shape) to check `feedback.md`/`last-failure.md` for named findings on an unchanged worktree and say so plainly in `note` — "a green note that omits a known-unresolved finding you had evidence of is a worse failure than reporting a false fail". Partial parity: `opencode/compose.mjs`'s render-side is mirrored (the two `isReviewRole` branches are structurally identical, confirmed by the pre-existing KI-E60 parity precedent), but the SETTING half is a disclosed gap — `opencode/runtime.mjs` drives verification via external `mech verify` step-dispatch calls, not `factory.js`'s direct `await call('runner', ...)`, so there is no single insertion point to mirror without a deeper, untested-tonight architectural change; a future port pass should wire it (same disclosed-gap posture as KI-E69's opencode note). Selftest: 860 → 864 (source-text pins: the exact threading line runs before any early FAILED return, the render sits inside the review-role branch specifically — not the shared prefix every role sees — and the opencode render-side mirror). |

| KI-E74C | **KI-E67's narrative/verdict contradiction detector only fires in ONE direction — a FAILED/ESCALATED verdict contradicted by confident-DONE prose. The MIRROR direction (a CLOSED verdict contradicted by an artifact that ITSELF admits something is not resolved) had no detector at all, despite being the exact shape of the ITEM-H1 near-miss (KI-E74B): the runner's own verify.json admitted 5 findings from a prior review round were unaddressed, and all 8 gates approved anyway — only an UNRELATED mechanism (KI-E70's deterministic transcript override, on a completely different failing test class) happened to catch it.** This is a detective backstop for when KI-E74B's preventive fix (surfacing the runner's note directly in every review-role prompt) isn't enough on its own — an agent still misses the surfaced note, or a similar admission lands in some other artifact never explicitly prompted. Fix: `lib/narrative-check.mjs` gains `detectUnresolvedCaveatOnClose(toState, textsByFile)` — same pure, WARN-only, conservative shape as KI-E67 (mirrored exactly: fires ONLY on `toState === 'CLOSED'`, only on STRONG unambiguous non-resolution admissions modeled directly on the ITEM-H1 phrasing, never a bare appearance of "unresolved" alone — a legitimately-accepted LOW-severity deferral correctly does not trip it, verified against a crafted fixture). Wired into `cmdFold` right beside the existing KI-E67 loop, scanning every `.md` file PLUS `verify.json` (the proven real-world source of this admission) — deliberately broader than KI-E67's `.md`-only scan, which stays unchanged to avoid any risk to an already-shipped, working check. Live-verified against the exact ITEM-H1 text before wiring: the marker correctly fires on `toState:'CLOSED'` and correctly stays silent on `toState:'FAILED'` (the same admission on a FAILED result is an accurate description, not a contradiction) and on a clean control text. Selftest: 864 → 876 (all 4 marker phrases independently pinned, the live ITEM-H1 fixture end-to-end, the CLOSED-only gate, the legitimate-deferral non-false-positive case, and 3 driver.mjs wiring pins: the detector call + WARN tag, the CLOSED-only gate, and the verify.json inclusion). |
| KI-E81 | **Auto-generate the "died on exactly one late-pipeline stage, gates already APPROVED, nothing to remediate" recovery prompt — the manual work this session did 7 times by hand** (2026-08-16: four items each needed only a fresh `re-auditor`; seven items each needed only a fresh `integrator`) after a spend-limit outage killed the band mid-run. Every one required the controller to: read `last-failure.md`'s prose by eye to find the stage-reached line, manually pull the finding's title/acceptance/files from `state/findings-graph.json`, hand-write near-identical role-brief-inlined prompt boilerplate, and remember the exact `integrate-raw.txt` tee-path contract — real, repeatable, mechanical work with no tooling behind it. `driver.mjs recover <id>` already auto-generates a `regate-<role>.md` prompt for a structured DISSENT (a gate that said CHANGES_REQUIRED) but had NOTHING for a pure infra death, where `dissentersFrom` finds zero dissenters (the stage never ran at all — there's no verdict to disagree with) and the README fell back to a bare "no structured dissent found — read last-failure.md" pointer. Fix: `lib/recover.mjs` gains `missingStageFrom(transitions, gates)` (pure) — deliberately narrow, recognizing only the two cleanest, most common shapes: last transition `REAUDITED` -> only `integrator` missing; last transition `REFUTE_OK` -> only `re-auditor` missing, with the exact lens set read DIRECTLY off the already-recorded `gates.reaudit` string (e.g. `"code=NULL"` -> `['code']`) rather than re-deriving factory.js's theme/band-based `reauditLenses()` selection logic a second time (which the Workflow-runtime/driver.mjs split, KI-E2, makes awkward and risky to share safely) — any other shape (died before any gate ran, or at GATED with refuter/reaudit both still missing, or a multi-lens reaudit with no recorded string) returns `stage:null` and falls back to a normal re-group, matching the judgment calls this session made by hand for the harder cases. The data source is a NEW `state/items/<id>/last-failure.json` structured sidecar — `cmdFold`'s existing loop (which already writes `last-failure.md` for every FAILED result, purely from the in-memory `r` object) now ALSO dumps `{transitions, gates, infraSuspect, note}` as JSON right next to it; deliberately NOT `result.json` (KI-L40's end-of-lifecycle checkpoint), because the checkpoint-writer agent is exactly as likely to die to the SAME spend-limit outage as everything else in the band — most items in this session's live incident had no `result.json` at all for that reason, but `last-failure.md`/`.json` are written by the DRIVER from data it already holds, independent of any agent surviving. `cmdRecover` now calls `missingStageFrom` whenever `dissentersFrom` finds zero dissent, and when it recognizes a shape, writes `state/items/<id>/recovery/recover-stage-<role>.md` — the SAME role-brief-inlining machinery the dissent path already uses, reusing the pre-existing `solutionFor(target)` resolver (services.json + `<Target>/<Target>.sln` convention) for the integrator's build command rather than a second, divergent lookup, and ending with the same parseable `VERDICT: ...` line convention this session's hand-written prompts used throughout. Surfaced in both the generated README.md (a new step "1b") and the CLI summary output, never silently only on disk. Enrichment only, never a gate: a null/unrecognized shape changes nothing about the existing dissent-only behavior (confirmed via a live `cmdRecover` smoke test against a real pre-existing FAILED item with no `last-failure.json` yet — identical output to before this change). Deliberately does NOT auto-generate a `refuter` prompt or handle a multi-lens FULL-band re-audit death — this session judged those not worth the added complexity/risk for a single item and folded it into a normal re-group instead; a future pass could extend `missingStageFrom` to that shape using the same `gates.reaudit`-string-reading trick. **This host's KI-E461 (recovery-gate-freshness mtime check) has not been ported here** — a sibling fix, KI-E465, extends KI-E461 with a content-hash freshness fallback; since E76 isn't present in this repo, E80 is a disclosed gap here rather than ported (see the source project if/when E76 lands). `missingStageFrom`'s full decision table (single-lens, multi-lens, GATED-death, pre-gate-death, empty/undefined-input never-throws cases) plus wiring pins confirming `cmdFold` writes the sidecar in the same pass, `cmdRecover` gates the auto-detection behind zero-dissent, reuses `solutionFor`, and surfaces the generated path in both outputs are covered by the lib self-test. |
| KI-E82 | **`main-check <id>` (KI-E50) already existed but required the caller to already know which ids to suspect — and fold's own KI-L65 auto-repair only ever inspects items in the CURRENT fold batch, never a sweep of everything the factory has ever touched.** Live 2026-08-16, same session as KI-E81: a 17-item fold's auto-repair correctly caught and restored exactly 1 stray main-tree file — the one matching an item IN that fold's batch (`ITEM-H23`) — but a manual `git status` sweep of the REAL working tree immediately afterward found 6 MORE contaminated files (5 modified + 2 new untracked) sitting there, leaked by items from TWO EARLIER, already-folded rounds this same session (`ITEM-M23`; another service's item), each independently confirmed byte-near-identical to its own worktree's copy (an agent writing outside its worktree, the exact KI-L65 shape) before being restored to HEAD by hand. Neither that fold nor any later one ever re-checks an item once its OWN fold has happened — the contamination sat undetected in the actual application repo for two full rounds. Fix: `cmdMainCheck` gains an `--all` flag (and a bare `main-check` with no ids ALSO sweeps, matching the live incident where nobody thought to pass any ids at all) that lists every item directory carrying a claim-time `main-snapshot.json` — i.e. every id the factory has EVER claimed, any cycle, not just the current batch — and checks all of them in one read-only pass. Deliberately reuses the EXISTING `driftAgainstSnapshot`/`splitDriftByStatus` decision logic unchanged (committed-drift vs dirty-drift classification is untouched and already correct — live-verified against the real 97-item history: `ITEM-H21`/`ITEM-H22`/etc. correctly read as "committed drift (human delivery)" since the owner had already reviewed and committed those, while the 6 real contamination files, before this fix's manual cleanup, would have read as `⚠ MAIN-DRIFT`) — this is purely a wider id list, not new classification logic, so the well-tested KI-E50/KI-L65 semantics carry over exactly. Live-verified end-to-end on the real repo post-fix: `main-check --all` swept all 97 claimed items, zero `⚠ MAIN-DRIFT` remaining (confirming the manual cleanup was complete) alongside the expected `committed drift` rows for genuinely-already-delivered items. Operational note for future sessions: run `main-check --all` periodically during a multi-round session (not just relying on each fold's own narrower auto-repair) — cheap, read-only, and the only way to catch contamination from a round that already closed. Selftest: 1010 → 1016 (wiring pins: flags threaded through the dispatch, `--all`-or-bare-call triggers the sweep, the id set is derived from actual on-disk snapshots rather than guessed, and the sweep does not reimplement drift classification). |
| KI-O4 | **`_workflow/opencode/`'s binding is controller-agnostic, not OpenCode-specific — GitHub Copilot verified as a second controller with zero new code.** Nothing in `runtime.mjs`/`compose.mjs`/`routing.mjs`/`schemas.mjs`/`buildtest.mjs` actually depends on OpenCode; the CLI just emits a generic `{mechanical:"<step>"}` / `{agents:[...]}` protocol over stdout and reads/writes plain files (`state/items/<id>/opencode-progress.json` kept as the checkpoint's on-disk name for historical continuity with KI-O1 — cosmetic only, not OpenCode-specific behavior). VS Code Copilot Chat in agent mode and the GitHub Copilot coding agent both have a terminal/run tool, so the exact same `init`/`next`/`submit`/`mech`/`status`/`finalize` sequence documented in `_workflow/opencode/README.md`'s "Usage protocol" applies to them verbatim — see that README's new "Using this binding from GitHub Copilot" section. **New fidelity gap, specific to any subagent-less controller (Copilot included):** unlike OpenCode's `Task` tool, Copilot has no documented "spawn an independent subagent" primitive, so a Copilot controller session must play every role (`architect`/`developer`/`qa`/`security`/`po`, the review-flow roles, etc.) itself, sequentially, in one conversation — this weakens `PLAN.md`'s "the review stage is separate adversarial subagents, never nested in the doing agent" invariant, since the reviewer is no longer independent of the implementer's own reasoning trail. Disclosed and accepted, not fixed by this change — same posture as KI-O1's "no per-call model tiering" gap. Also added: `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (this repo, direct) and a host-installable `copilot-assets/copilot-instructions.md` template that `setup/init.mjs` copies to a host repo's `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (opt out with `--no-copilot-assets`), mirroring the existing `claude-assets/` → `.claude/skills/` install step exactly (same `copyTree()` helper, same `*.factory-new` no-clobber behavior on a locally-edited host file). |
| KI-E83 | **The `FACTORY::RED::` marker's disk-authoritative validity was checked in exactly ONE place: `deterministicVerifyOverride`'s P1 check at FOLD time — AFTER the entire expensive gate band (4-5 gates + refuter + reaudit + integrator) had already run and approved the item.** The test-author's OWN self-reported `test.red` field IS checked in-run, immediately after that call — but KI-E10 already proved, for the SAME shape of marker (`FACTORY::REALINFRA::`), that a self-reported field can diverge from what the agent's own artifact shows on disk (an earlier item, cycle 26). Nothing had applied that same lesson to the RED marker. Live 2026-08-16, same session as KI-E81/E82: `ITEM-H24` (9 gates APPROVED) and `ITEM-H26` (8 gates APPROVED) each burned a full band before being rejected at fold on `FACTORY::RED::0` — a vacuous regression test that passes on both old and new code, present since the ORIGINAL fixer round and caught by none of the 17 gate/review agents across both items combined, only by the fold-time grep neither of them ever saw. Fix: `factory.js` gains a `RED-PROOF MARKER PROBE` (KI-E83) — the KI-E10 realInfra-marker-probe SHAPE applied verbatim to this sibling marker: a cheap haiku agent runs `grep "FACTORY::RED::" <worktree-item-dir>/verify-red-raw.txt | tail -1` (mirroring `parseRedRaw`'s own "last match wins" semantics — a retry-appended transcript is handled the same way both places) and reports `markerFound`/`exitCode`/`line` back verbatim, no interpretation. Wired in immediately after `res.transitions.push('GREEN', 'BUILT', 'TESTED')` — the EARLIEST point the marker can exist — strictly BEFORE the edge-scan/acceptance-scan/leftover-scan/registration-drift-scan pre-band probes and the entire gate band. `markerFound:false` or an exit code that doesn't match the item's contract (`exitCode !== 0` for a normal item — vacuous; `exitCode === 0` for a `verificationOnly` item — KI-L55's INVERTED contract, the pinning/coverage test must PASS on the current tree) fails the item immediately, `finish('FAILED', ...)`, before a single gate-agent token is spent. The fold-time P1 check is explicitly RETAINED unchanged as the deterministic backstop — this is a fail-fast optimization layered in front of it, never a replacement (a null/unavailable probe fails OPEN, proceeding to the band exactly as every sibling pre-band probe already does, per the established fail-open posture). New `RED_PROOF_SCHEMA` also added byte-identical to `opencode/schemas.mjs` for the mechanical schema-parity selftest gate (Fix #20). *(Port status, corrected by KI-E112: this passage disclosed the probe as "NOT yet ported (a future port pass should wire it)" and cited a `KI-E460` that has never existed as a row in this registry — a dangling reference. The gap is now CLOSED: `afterVerify` enforces the RED-proof contract mechanically, and the KI-E103 runtime-parity gate makes a re-opened gap a selftest failure rather than a prose footnote.)* Selftest: 1016 → 1022 (three exec-smoke behavioral proofs, not just source pins: a vacuous `exitCode:0` on a normal `.cs` item fails BEFORE any gate/review/refuter/reaudit/integrator call — the actual token-savings claim, verified against the real `calls` list the harness records, not assumed; a missing marker fails identically; and the `verificationOnly` inversion, proven on a genuine `.cs` item since the obvious `SMOKE-VONLY` fixture turns out to be a config/YAML-only item — `codeChange:false` — so the probe correctly never even runs for it, a live discovery while writing the test, not a bug in the shipped code). |

| KI-O5 | **OpenCode was a first-class RUNTIME concern and a zero-class INSTALLATION concern: `setup/init.mjs` installed a host pointer for Claude Code (`.claude/skills/`) and, since KI-O4, for Copilot (`.github/copilot-instructions.md`), but an OpenCode controller sitting in a host repo had NO installed pointer at all** — `_workflow/opencode/` was reachable only by a session that already knew to go looking for it, and the only documents that mention the binding (`CLAUDE.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`) are either not loaded by OpenCode or describe a different controller's affordances. Fix, mirroring the KI-O4 precedent: a third asset dir `opencode-assets/` with two halves, because they have two ownership stories. (a) `opencode-assets/root/**` are factory-owned FILES that the existing `copyTree()` installs onto the HOST ROOT with the identical no-clobber contract (byte-identical → silent no-op, absent → created, locally-edited → `*.factory-new` sibling + warning): `AGENTS.md`, `.opencode/ai-factory.md` (the always-on rules), `.opencode/skill/ai-factory/SKILL.md` (the controller manual, rewritten around `runtime.mjs` rather than the `Workflow` tool, and mandating one independent `Task` subagent per role — the invariant KI-O4 records Copilot as structurally unable to honour). `AGENTS.md` is the one asset a host is genuinely likely to already own, so the shipped copy is deliberately a short POINTER and the substance lives at `.opencode/ai-factory.md`, a factory-owned path that cannot collide — a host that keeps its own `AGENTS.md` still gets the full rule set, via `instructions`. (b) `opencode-assets/opencode.config.json` is a FRAGMENT that is **merged** into the host's own `opencode.json`, not copied over it: that file carries the host's model/provider/MCP settings. `lib/hostinstall.mjs`'s `mergeOpencodeConfig` does it, and it is a real function rather than an object spread at the call site for one specific reason — **opencode evaluates the LAST matching permission pattern, and a JS spread keeps an existing key in its ORIGINAL insertion position on overwrite**, so `{...host, ...factory}` would leave a factory rule installed by a PREVIOUS run stranded behind a broad host rule declared after it (e.g. `git *: allow`) and the invariant would be silently off. `appendRulesLast` drops factory-owned keys from their old positions and re-appends the whole block at the tail; the reordering is REPORTED, never silent. A host config that is not strict JSON, or whose top-level `permission` is the bare-string form (which applies to EVERY tool, including ones the factory names no rules for — expanding it would silently narrow read/glob/grep/list/task posture), is REFUSED with the factory block written as `*.factory-new`, the same no-silent-damage posture as `install.sh`'s `settings.local.json` guard. **Policy shape, deliberately not a blanket deny:** `opencode.json` is repo-wide, not factory-session-scoped, so denying mutating git outright would also block ordinary OpenCode-assisted development in the host repo — which the factory's own rules explicitly permit ("implementing something yourself, outside the factory, is fine", `copilot-assets/copilot-instructions.md`). So bare `commit`/`add`/`checkout`/`restore`/`stash`/`reset`/`clean`/`push` (with and without `-C`) are **`ask`** — normal work costs one keystroke and "the human authors every commit" becomes a prompt that must be answered — while the same verbs aimed at a factory worktree (`git -C *worktrees* ...`, one pattern covering both `/` and `\` separators), deleting `state/STOP_REQUESTED.md` (`rm`/`del`/`Remove-Item`), and editing `state/ledger.json` are hard **`deny`**. `git worktree add` is deliberately NOT caught: `lib/worktree.mjs:58` invokes it WITHOUT `-C`, and every pattern is anchored on `git -C ` or a bare mutating verb. Also added: `setup/install.mjs`, the Node twin of `install.sh` for hosts with no usable bash (Windows without Git Bash/WSL, locked-down CI images) — same contracts (strict `vX.Y.Z` resolution via the shared `pickLatestReleaseTag`, selftest gate on install AND upgrade, rollback on a red upgrade, transactional removal of a partial install, vendored-mount refusal, explicit-`--host`-beats-self-location), plus a `controllers` command that re-installs host assets only. It delegates ALL host-side wiring to `init.mjs`, so the three controller seams have exactly one implementation and cannot drift; telemetry is explicitly NOT reimplemented (docker + shell profiles, no meaningful Windows story) and the gap is disclosed in `--help` and in `install`'s final line rather than silently omitted. **Live-caught during verification, before shipping:** `readFileSync(p,'utf8')` does not strip a UTF-8 BOM and `JSON.parse` rejects one, so a host `opencode.json` saved by PowerShell 5.1's `Set-Content -Encoding utf8` / older Visual Studio / Notepad read as "unparseable" and took the REFUSING-to-touch path — the factory's OpenCode policy would then have silently never installed on precisely the Windows hosts `install.mjs` exists to serve; `stripBom`/`parseJsonFile` fix it, and a malformed-JSON case is pinned alongside so the tolerance can never swallow a real parse failure into a clobber. **Accepted limitations:** the permission patterns are matched against the command string, so `git -C <path> worktree list` and similar read-only `-C` forms that happen to contain a mutating verb as a later word can prompt spuriously (safe direction, one keystroke); and an `ask` rule is only as strong as the human answering it — it is a visible decision point, not an enforcement boundary. Selftest: 898 → 972 (the reordering invariant stated directly, idempotency of a re-merge, per-tool string-shorthand promotion vs top-level bare-string refusal, host-setting preservation, BOM tolerance + malformed-JSON throw, the fragment's key set against opencode's `additionalProperties:false` Config, the ask/deny split per verb, the `git worktree add` exemption, all six asset paths, the OpenCode skill's required frontmatter and its `runtime.mjs`-not-`Workflow` framing, init.mjs's step-4c wiring, and install.mjs's parity contracts). |

| KI-E86 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-24): concurrent items run in SIBLING worktrees on the SAME host under `group --conc N`, and by default `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` leave persistent MSBuild worker processes running for reuse by the NEXT invocation — a well-documented source of intermittent cross-invocation contention/staleness under concurrent CI-style builds, independent of the code under test.** Fix: `export MSBUILDDISABLENODEREUSE=1` once, at the top of `verify/build-test.sh`, right after `set -uo pipefail` — covers every dotnet invocation in the script (present and future) without touching each call site individually. Zero-risk: only changes whether MSBuild worker processes are cached between invocations, no correctness impact. No selftest delta (a shell-script environment export has no pure-function surface to pin; verified by inspection that the export precedes every `dotnet` call in the file). No findings against this entry from the 2026-08-25 multi-lens review (see KI-E87's entry). |

| KI-E87 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-24) — PlanCommitmentScan: no stage in the pipeline cross-checked the diff against the PLANNER's OWN stated commitments — every stage checked the diff against the ITEM's external acceptance criteria (KI-E18) or general code quality, a different axis, so a plan/execution drift was invisible until the re-audit's holistic pass, at full band price.** Origin evidence: a host-mount session's own batch burned a FULL band (editorial x2, gate:architect, gate:developer, gate:qa, review:code/adversarial/edgecase/testreview, refuter) before the re-auditor — the LAST stage in the pipeline — caught that `plan.md` explicitly said "**MUST include the brownfield note**" while the delivered diff never included it. Fix, mirroring the proven KI-E18/KI-D12 shape: (1) hoist `plan` to function scope in `runItem()` (`let plan = null` before the `if (R.planner)`, assigned inside it) — `null` for a mechanical item (planner skipped), naturally gating the new probe off for that whole class at zero cost. (2) New pure deterministic pre-filter `hasPlanCommitmentLanguage(text)` (`lib/plan-commitment.mjs`, inlined byte-identically into factory.js per the KI-E2 split) detects explicit "MUST"/"must include/add/update/…"/"required to" language in `plan.approach`+`plan.blastRadius` — a coarse gate (not a clause splitter) since commitment language in free prose is sparse and irregular, unlike the acceptance field KI-E18 splits. Ships with the `[\s-]+` separator refinement already folded in (not `\s+`-only) — the origin session found live that a hyphenated title-case compound ("Must-cover checklist") defeats a whitespace-only pattern; proven against the exact specimen before widening. (3) New pre-band probe "PLAN-COMMITMENT SCAN" (`PLAN_COMMITMENT_SCHEMA`, cheap haiku), positioned AFTER the acceptance-scan and BEFORE the comment-scan: reads the plan's commitment language + `review-pack.md`, judges `honored`, with ONE bounded fixer amend + ONE re-probe on a confirmed gap (identical safety-valve shape to acceptance-scan) before a still-unhonored commitment fails the item PRE-BAND. Fail-open throughout: a null/malformed probe never sinks the item (verified: exactly one `finish('FAILED', ...)` call site in the whole block). `opencode/schemas.mjs` gets `PLAN_COMMITMENT_SCHEMA` for the mechanical schema-parity selftest gate, mirroring the KI-E83/`RED_PROOF_SCHEMA` precedent exactly — schema-parity only: the opencode runtime's own routing/dispatch of this probe is NOT yet ported. Selftest: 974 → 1020 (combined KI-E87+E88+E89 delta — see KI-E89 below): `hasPlanCommitmentLanguage`'s positive/negative/word-boundary cases incl. "mustard"/"mustache" false-fire guards and the "Must-cover" hyphen-separator case; the factory.js inline-copy byte-parity pin; full pipeline wiring pins (schema, gate key, the `plan` hoisting, the gate condition, the bounded-amend-then-reprobe shape, the single fail path). **Multi-lens review + fixes (2026-08-25, run against both this repo and the origin host-mount session, all findings cross-ported):** a 4-lens agent review (correctness, cross-repo consistency, test quality, doc accuracy) found 13 real, independently-verified issues across the KI-E86..E89 port (all 13 confirmed by an adversarial second pass). Four landed here: (1) **the "must + verb" pattern was a CLOSED 14-word verb allowlist** — tested against 14 realistic commitment sentences a planner would actually write, 13 of 14 were silently missed because their verbs weren't on the two-incident-derived list. Since this function is the SOLE gate deciding whether the probe runs at all, every miss meant zero plan-vs-diff cross-check. Widened to `\bmust[\s-]+\w+` (any word, not a fixed list). (2) **The KI-E69 cross-session plan-reuse stand-in carried NO text fields at all**, so on any relaunched item this scan silently, permanently never fired regardless of what the real on-disk `plan.md` promised. Fixed: the stand-in now also carries the raw `plan.md` text verbatim as `approach`. (3) **The PLAN-COMMITMENT AMEND note-only dead end** — see the KI-E18 entry above; the identical fix applied here too (and to this repo's own KI-E18 acceptance-scan, which had the same bug). (4) **`meta.description` was already accurate here** (this repo's copy of that field already named the plan-commitment scan stage — only the origin host-mount session's copy was missing it, fixed there). Also reconciled a cross-repo finding: the KI-E87 probe's LLM-facing prompt text had been independently rewritten in each repo rather than ported byte-for-byte, undocumented as an adaptation (unlike KI-E88's explicit one) — now documented in-line as an accepted adaptation (prompt prose has no byte-parity convention anywhere in this file; only deterministic logic does). Selftest: 1020 → 1046 (combined with the KI-E88/E89 fixes below — see those entries). |

| KI-E88 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-24), adapted — the picker (`driver.mjs suggest`) was item-COUNT-blind to item-COST: a FULL-band item runs the full 5-gate opus panel (architect/security/po + developer/qa) plus an opus-routed planner/testAuthor/fixer; LIGHT skips 3 of those 5 gates and the planner entirely, roughly a 4x gate-panel difference, and the printed item count gave zero signal for which a given batch would land.** Adaptation note: the origin session's fix targeted a mixed-batch severity-bucketed fallback section of `suggest` that does not exist in this repo — this repo's `suggest` is entirely similarity-cluster-based (`clusterBySimilarity` + clique/co-batch pick, owner directive 2026-07-04). Ported the reusable half verbatim (`lib/band.mjs`, a driver-side Node-importable port of factory.js's canonical `bandFor()`/`BAND_FULL_THEMES` — the Workflow-runtime definition itself cannot be imported, KI-E2 split; byte-identical to factory.js's copy, pinned) and re-implemented the DISPLAY half against the actual cluster-based shape: a whole-pool band-mix line right after `suggest`'s existing summary line, plus a per-cluster band-mix tally appended to each cluster's own header (`#n [theme] ... (band mix of the N-item pick: X LIGHT / Y FULL)`). Purely additive/read-only — the clustering, the clique/co-batch pick logic, and the `group --ids` recommendation are all unchanged. Selftest: see KI-E89 below for the combined final count — `bandFor`'s full priority-order unit tests, a `BAND_FULL_THEMES` byte-parity pin against factory.js, a `bandFor` function-body byte-parity pin, and driver wiring pins for both the whole-pool and per-cluster band-mix lines (targeting this repo's actual `cmdSuggest`, not the origin session's fallback shape). **Fix (multi-lens review, 2026-08-25 — see KI-E87's entry for the review itself):** two test-quality gaps. (1) No case combined a `BAND_FULL_THEMES` theme WITH `fixType:'mechanical'` in one call — `bandFor`'s own P5 safety comment names exactly this interaction ("a mechanical authz/HMAC/tenant-filter edit is still a security change whose load-bearing reviewer must NOT be dropped"), but nothing pinned it: a future edit swapping the order of the two `if`s would have silently downgraded a security-themed mechanical item from FULL to LIGHT with zero test failures. (2) The `BAND_FULL_THEMES` array-parity extraction had no null-guard before its `eq()` comparison, unlike the sibling `bandFor`-body check. Both closed with regression tests. |

| KI-E89 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-24) — `main-check`'s per-item snapshot loop (incl. its own KI-E82 `--all` widening, already present in this repo) could only detect drift on a path that was part of some CHECKED item's claim-time snapshot; a brand-new leaked path no item ever declared had no snapshot to diff against and was structurally invisible to it, even under the `--all` sweep.** Origin evidence: a host-mount session's batch validation found real leaked contamination (one tracked-file edit + two new untracked paths) sitting clean through both the closing item's own auto-repair and a fresh `main-check --all` sweep — a manual `git status --short` was the only thing that caught it, exactly the KI-E82 motivating shape one layer deeper (E82 widened WHICH ids get checked; this closes what happens when NO id's snapshot covers the leaked path at all). Fix: new pure helper `unclaimedMainDrift(dirty, mountRel, claimedPaths)` (`lib/mainguard.mjs`, alongside its KI-E14 sibling `dirtyMainPaths`) — given the raw dirty/untracked state of the main tree, the factory's own mount prefix (its bookkeeping — ledger/reports/queue/state — is ALWAYS legitimately dirty during active use and is excluded, never reported as noise), and the union of every path any CHECKED item has claimed (accumulated from every checked id's snapshot `files[]` keys, regardless of drift status — the exact ceiling of what KI-E82's own widened loop is even capable of seeing), returns whatever main-tree dirt remains unaccounted for. `cmdMainCheck` calls it once after its existing per-id loop (unmodified from KI-E82: still `flags?.all` triggers the full-history id sweep) and prints a distinctly-labeled `⚠ MAIN-DRIFT unclaimed (KI-E89)` warning — still read-only/warn-only, no auto-repair (an unclaimed path has no snapshot to prove what "repair" would mean). Ported here as an UNMODIFIED port (not an adaptation) — this repo's `cmdMainCheck` already had the exact `(rest, flags)` + `--all`-sweep shape the origin session built KI-E89 against. Verified via a unit-level real-git-repo fixture (mirroring the origin session's own CLI-level live repro of the identical incident shape) — a synthetic leaked directory under an already-tracked sibling path correctly surfaces, while a claimed file and the mount's own bookkeeping churn both stay silent; `main-check --all` also smoke-run live against this repo's own (currently empty) ledger — confirmed it reaches and exits the new code path cleanly (no claimed items exist here yet to demonstrate a live positive-fire the way the origin session did on its own populated ledger). Selftest: 974 → 1020 (KI-E87 + KI-E88 + KI-E89 combined delta: `unclaimedMainDrift`'s pure predicate cases, the live-repo-fixture proof, and driver wiring pins for `cmdMainCheck(rest, flags)`'s actual KI-E82-widened shape; `bandFor`'s coverage per KI-E88; `hasPlanCommitmentLanguage`'s coverage per KI-E87). **Fix (multi-lens review, 2026-08-25 — see KI-E87's entry for the review itself):** three real correctness gaps, all zero-test-coverage before this pass. (1) **`claimedPaths` was scoped to only the explicitly-requested id(s)** on a targeted (non-`--all`) call — correct under `--all` but wrong on the far more common single-id call every item's own mid-band Verify stage makes: any OTHER item's legitimately snapshot-backed but not-yet-committed change sitting in main was misreported as unclaimed/leaked contamination. Fixed by computing the full claimed-id inventory unconditionally and seeding `claimedPaths` from THAT, decoupled from which id(s) this invocation is targeting for its own drift re-hash. (2) **`unclaimedMainDrift`'s `dirs` branch never consulted `claimedPaths` at all** — a directory an item legitimately declared in its own files[] was unconditionally, permanently reported as unclaimed, directly contradicting this function's own header comment. Fixed by mirroring `filesOverlapDirty`'s reversed-direction check. (3) **`dirtyMainPaths` stripped only the OUTER quotes** git wraps around a path containing a quote/backslash/non-ASCII byte, leaving the C-style `\NNN` octal escapes literally in the string — verified live against real git output (a backslash, an embedded quote, and a UTF-8 filename all previously came out mangled). Fixed with a small, narrowly-scoped decoder that only activates on an already-quoted path. Selftest: 1020 → 1046 (combined with KI-E87/E88's fixes above — see those entries). |
| KI-E90 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-28) — `group` had no signal for same-`target` batch-pairing risk.** The KI-E14 file-lock check only compares DECLARED `files[]`, but a fixer may legitimately touch a file OUTSIDE its declared lock-set when "strictly required" (fix.json's own documented escape hatch). Origin evidence: two same-`target` items in one batch had ZERO declared `files[]` overlap and both cleared the KI-E14 check, yet one item's fixer discovered mid-run it also needed a file the OTHER item held, and FAILED on a pure batch-scheduling collision neither item's own content caused. Fix: new pure helper `sameTargetPairs(picked)` (`lib/ledger.mjs`, alongside its KI-E29 sibling `closedDepsWithLiveWorktree`) returns `[{target, ids}]` for every target with 2+ picked items; `cmdGroup` prints a `KI-E90 WARN` after the existing file-lock/KI-E29 checks. WARN, don't exclude (KI-E29 posture) — same-target pairing is often fine, nothing is clobbered by it; the operator judges whether to split the batch. Ported here as an UNMODIFIED port (this repo's `cmdGroup` already had the exact same file-lock + KI-E29 shape the origin session built KI-E90 against). Selftest: 1046 → 1052 (pure-function cases + a `sameTargetPairs(picked)`/`KI-E90 WARN` wiring pin). |
| KI-E91 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-28) — no check mechanized `standards-evolution.md §3.4`'s own documented "ripple check"** (grep for the tag, confirm the ledger anchor exists, confirm the entry isn't self-contradictory) — left to a human/reviewer's memory, caught (if at all) only by a full expensive adversarial review + adjudication. Origin evidence: a doc-drift-themed item authored the SAME anchor slug as a top-level entry in TWO sibling ledger files (a root ledger + a subrepo-scoped ledger) with materially different Created dates / Standard citations / Legacy-sites content for what is presented as the SAME divergence, and separately asserted a `standards-evolution:` call-site tag was present in five files that grep proved did NOT carry it — both caught only after a full band + adjudication, and the same duplicate-entry content later leaked into an UNRELATED item's main-tree contamination the same cycle (a stale claim-time snapshot happened to expose it). *(This repo has a single `_bmad-output/tech-debt/STANDARDS-DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md`, not the origin session's root+subrepo pair — the duplicate-anchor check still applies to any two ledger files a host configures, and degrades to a no-op when only one exists.)* Fix: new pre-band haiku probe `ledger-anchor-probe` (`factory.js`, mirrors the `leftover-probe`/KI-D12 STEP-1-mechanical/STEP-2-classify shape — this repo has no `registration-drift-probe`/KI-E462 at all, so that origin-session precedent doesn't exist here to mirror) — gated on declared `item.files` naming a `*STANDARDS-DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md` path; new engine-owned `build-test.sh ledger-anchor` subcommand (joining the `leftovers|comments` dispatch case) backed by pure module `lib/ledger-anchor.mjs` + CLI wrapper `ledger-anchor-lint.mjs` finds candidates deterministically (added/edited anchor headings, cross-checked against every configured ledger file for the same slug at the same heading level, plus any claimed `standards-evolution:` call-site tag cross-checked against the actual file); the haiku probe judges each candidate for material disagreement. `LEDGER_PATHS` in `ledger-anchor-lint.mjs` currently holds this repo's SINGLE ledger path (`_bmad-output/tech-debt/STANDARDS-DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md` — no subrepo-scoped sibling), so the duplicate-anchor half always finds zero candidates here (correct degrade, not a bug — verified by a dedicated selftest case); the false-tag-claim half stays fully active. Fail-open (only acts on an explicit boolean), FAILS the item pre-band on a hit. **Port status**: `LEDGER_ANCHOR_SCHEMA` is exported from `opencode/schemas.mjs` and, since KI-E112, the opencode runtime DISPATCHES this probe (mechanical `build-test.sh ledger-anchor` STEP-1 in `mech leftover`, gated on the item's declared files naming a `*STANDARDS-DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md` path, then a `ledger_anchor_classify` agent step mirroring `leftover_classify`). *(Correction, KI-E112: this row previously claimed "this repo has no `schema-parity` selftest gate forcing opencode runtime parity". That was FALSE — a mechanical schema-parity gate does exist in `opencode/_selftest.mjs` and is run by the main suite. The accurate statement was that no RUNTIME-parity gate existed; KI-E103 then built one, and KI-E112 closed the gap this row was disclosing.)* Selftest: 1052 → 1079 (pure-function coverage for all six exported `lib/ledger-anchor.mjs` functions incl. a CPS-H-15-shaped duplicate-anchor repro and the single-ledger no-op-degrade case, plus `build-test.sh`/factory.js pipeline wiring pins; two pre-existing KI-D12/KI-E59 literal-string wiring pins updated for the extended `leftovers|comments|ledger-anchor)` case pattern). |
| KI-E92 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-28) — mechanical fixer/test-author routing (`fixer.mechanical`/`test_author.mechanical`) re-routed `claude-sonnet-5` → `claude-sonnet-4-6`** (owner directive, in response to the origin session's own batch close-rate regression). Origin ledger cost-bucket evidence confirmed `claude-sonnet-4-6` was the mechanical fixer/test-author default before a routine current-gen swap to `claude-sonnet-5` — never shown worse; the only documented revert on that route was an unrelated `claude-haiku-4-5` experiment. Updated in all three places routing is defined in this repo: `config/model-routing.json` (`fixer.mechanical`/`test_author.mechanical`), `factory.js`'s inline `RT` table (`fixerMech`/`testMech`), the pinned selftest assertion, AND `_workflow/opencode/routing.mjs`'s mirror copy. |
| KI-E93 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-28) — `test-author.md` had no explicit self-check for MULTI-target acceptance criteria.** When a finding's acceptance names several controllers/methods/files needing coverage, nothing forced the test-author to enumerate and cover every one rather than a representative sample. Origin evidence: an item failed the identical pre-band acceptance-scan twice in a row — its acceptance named three controllers needing HTTP auth-matrix test coverage, and both attempts covered only one of the three. **Fix**: `agents/test-author.md` gains a new numbered "MULTI-TARGET COVERAGE SELF-CHECK" step — enumerate every named target explicitly and check the acceptance text off target-by-target against `testFiles` before returning. Prevention, not detection. |
| KI-E94 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-28) — `fixer.md` had no self-check for a fix that touches one instance of a repeated pattern (a switch arm, a method overload, a near-identical sibling) while leaving other instances of the identical defect untouched, nor for verifying a fix's own writes are actually consumed by anything.** Origin evidence: one item fixed one arm of a remediation switch while the next arm — the same defect class, a write nothing reads — shipped unfixed; a separate item's actual money-correctness fix was inert dead code, papered over with an unescalated "known limitation" comment, caught only by a unanimous multi-reviewer rejection. **Fix**: `agents/fixer.md` gains a new numbered "SIBLING-PATTERN SWEEP" step (grep for every other instance of the touched pattern before finishing, fix each one carrying the identical defect) plus a "DEAD-CODE SELF-CHECK" (before finishing, name the specific downstream reader of every write/increment/cache-set the fix adds or relies on). |
| KI-E95 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-28) — `fixer.md`'s existing DOC-CLAIM (KI-E11) and COUNT-CLAIM (KI-E51) self-checks catch a phantom path or a wrong count, but nothing caught a fully plausible-sounding, wholly INVENTED config key / default / behavior, nor a stale claim left standing elsewhere in the same document being corrected.** Origin evidence: a new AGENTS.md-style doc invented a config key name, its default (backwards), and its behavioral semantics (nonexistent) — asserted confidently in five places, shipped with a fully green test run because nothing in the diff tested the documentation's own accuracy against source; a separate item left a stale claim standing elsewhere in the same doc file it was correcting. **Fix**: `agents/fixer.md` gains a "NO-INVENTION SELF-CHECK" (every factual prose claim must trace to an actual grep/read in this worktree, never recalled from a plausible-sounding convention; if you can't find where a behavior lives, say so instead of guessing) and an "ADJACENT-CLAIM RE-CHECK" (re-read the whole surrounding section — not just the edited line — for other claims about the same subject that may also now be wrong; re-verify any exact line-number/text citation against current file content immediately before finishing). |
| KI-E96 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-28) — `code-style.md`'s CancellationToken rule ("ALWAYS pass CancellationToken through the entire async call chain") had no self-check in `fixer.md` at all** — the violation compiles cleanly, which is exactly why it keeps shipping. Origin evidence: a fix accepted a `CancellationToken` parameter but never forwarded it at multiple call sites — and the SAME gap, at the SAME call sites, survived from one review round into the next uncorrected. **Fix**: `agents/fixer.md` gains a "CANCELLATIONTOKEN CHAIN SELF-CHECK" — when a touched method accepts a `CancellationToken`, grep its body for every downstream `Async(...)` call and verify the token is threaded to every one, not just the first/obvious call. |
| KI-E97 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-29) — `REALINFRA_SIGNAL`'s bare `concurren` catch-all (the safety net that forces `needsRealInfra:true` even when the graph declares `realInfra:false`) matched plain-English uses of "concurrent" with zero relation to a real concurrency defect, forcing an unnecessary Testcontainers demand onto items that never needed one.** Origin evidence — 5 live false-positive incidents: "~10 concurrent 100MB downloads cause OOM" (a volume/OOM concern, not a race), two "do not group **concurrently** with X" file-lock batch-scheduling notes (matched purely because the word appears), "file-lock will serialize against any **concurrent** … work" (scheduling prose), and one item with TWO separate occurrences — "…or that they are **concurrent**)" and, missed by the first-pass fix since `.test()` only needs one survivor, a bare throttle-cap aside "…SemaphoreSlim cap (e.g. max 10 **concurrent**)." with no following noun. **Fix**: narrowed the catch-all with a preceding are/is lookbehind, a following-noun exclusion list (downloads/requests/clients/users/sessions/connections/tabs/calls/work), a with/to/against-preposition exclusion, and a close-paren exclusion (`(?!\))`) for the dangling-adjective throttle-cap shape — WITHOUT losing any of 11 confirmed-genuine concurrency-defect matches, several of which depend ENTIRELY on this regex since their own graph entry declares `realInfra:false`. Also carries forward two earlier, never-before-ported refinements found alongside this fix: hyphen-vs-space normalization (`optimistic[\s-]+concurren`, `unique[\s-]+(constraint|index)`) and an explicit `concurrencyexception` alternative. Verified against a full findings-graph sweep on the origin session (not just the curated incident list) — the rest of the realInfra-matching population all matched via genuine, non-catch-all alternatives. One latent, unconfirmed risk NOT fixed on the origin session (no live incident, and fixing it risks a new false negative): the identical "concurrently (parenthetical)" scheduling-note idiom, phrased with a parenthetical instead of "with X" — noted, not chased without evidence. |
| KI-E98 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-29) — neither `detectNarrativeVerdictContradiction` (KI-E67) nor `detectUnresolvedCaveatOnClose` (KI-E74C) understood negation** — a phrase preceded by "No"/"Not"/"Zero"/"None"/"Never"/"Nothing" asserts the OPPOSITE of what the bare marker text implies, but neither detector's match logic ever looked at what preceded the match. Origin evidence: an item's own `verify.json` wrote "No findings from the prior round remain unaddressed." — a genuine, correct completion claim on a `CLOSED` result — and `detectUnresolvedCaveatOnClose`'s `findings…remain unaddressed` marker fired anyway, reading a correct claim as an admission of failure. **Fix**: `narrative-check.mjs` gains a shared `isNegated(text, matchIndex)` helper (a 25-char lookbehind window tested against `/\b(no|not|zero|none|never|nothing)\s*$/i`), applied to BOTH detectors' match loops — symmetrically, even though only the `detectUnresolvedCaveatOnClose` direction has a confirmed origin-session live incident, since it is the identical underlying weakness. Care was taken NOT to over-suppress: the real ITEM-H1 admission this detector exists to catch ("Build+targeted-test green here does NOT mean round 2's findings are resolved — they are not, and are unaddressed…", the KI-E74B/KI-E74C citation) still fires — its outer "does NOT mean X" negates a different clause than the inner "findings…remain unaddressed" marker the regex actually matches on, so a naive blanket negation scan could have silently broken the very detector KI-E74C was built to add. |
| KI-E99 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-29) — `doclint.mjs`'s `findMissingClaims` (F2) flagged a line HONESTLY stating a path does not exist YET — a common, legitimate "deferred/planned/Wave N" prose pattern — as if it were a fabricated-existence claim, the opposite of the false-existence claim the linter exists to catch.** Origin evidence: a doc-drift fix's own added `CONTEXT.md` line — "**No K8s manifest yet**: `k8s/base/services/a-service.yaml` is Wave 5 (deploy gates the full sprint)." — a true statement about the current tree, flagged anyway because the linter only checked "does this path resolve", never whether the surrounding prose asserts existence or absence. Non-blocking (WARN-only), but exactly the kind of noise that degrades a WARN channel's trustworthiness (the same rationale KI-E67/KI-E74C were built on). **Fix**: `findMissingClaims` gains a `NOT_YET_EXISTS_RE` line-level skip (`no…yet`, `not yet`, `does(n't/ not) exist`, `yet to be/exist`, `to be built/created/added/implemented`, `not (yet) built/created/implemented`, `is wave \d`) checked BEFORE claim extraction on each added line — deliberately line-granular (a genuine phantom-path claim on a DIFFERENT line in the same call still catches; only the matching line itself is skipped). |
| KI-E100 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-08-29) — `removeWorktree` (worktree.mjs) drops the worktree directory + git's worktree-admin entry but leaves the `factory/<id>` branch ref standing at its stale creation commit** — `addWorktree`'s own fallback path (`git worktree add -b` failing "already exists" → falls back to plain `git worktree add <path> <existing-branch>`) then silently attaches the NEXT worktree for that id to the OLD branch tip instead of cutting a fresh one from current HEAD. Origin evidence, confirmed via direct operational experience: refreshing worktrees that had drifted far behind HEAD required a MANUAL `git branch -D factory/<id>` after `worktree-remove` before a subsequent `group` created a genuinely fresh worktree — silent staleness, not a crash, so it would otherwise go unnoticed until a fix landed on top of months-old code. **Fix**: new `pruneStaleBranch(branch, repoRoot)` in `worktree.mjs`, called immediately after `removeWorktree` at BOTH call sites (the CLI `worktree-remove` subcommand — branch resolved via `listWorktrees()` before removal, since the info is gone once the worktree is gone — and `gc`'s CLOSED-item sweep, which already has `r.branch` from the ledger). Deletion is gated on `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch> HEAD`: per the factory's own "never commits" invariant, a `factory/<id>` branch has, by construction, zero commits beyond its base, so one that IS an ancestor of HEAD carries no unique value and is safe to discard; one that is NOT (an unexpected commit landed on it, which the hard rule forbids but this function must not blindly trust) is left standing with a reason string, never silently discarded. |
| KI-E104 | **The fold's P9 root-cause check ("a diff that touched ONLY tests greened the test, it did not fix the bug") ran ONLY at fold — after the entire gate band had been spent.** This is the identical economics KI-E83 fixed for its sibling P1, and P9 is strictly cheaper to hoist: no marker, no transcript, no agent judgment, just the worktree's own `git status`. **Fix**: new `verify/build-test.sh rootcause` subcommand (joining the engine-owned `leftovers\|comments\|ledger-anchor` dispatch, before the host-override seam) backed by `_workflow/rootcause-lint.mjs`, which IMPORTS `lib/worktree.mjs`'s `changedFiles` and `lib/verify.mjs`'s predicate rather than re-deriving either — there is no second definition to drift from what the fold applies. A cheap haiku relay probe (`ROOTCAUSE_SCHEMA`, no classify step, mirroring KI-E59's comment probe) reports the markers verbatim; `nonTestCount === 0` FAILS the item pre-band. Gated exactly as the fold gates P9: code items, never `verificationOnly` (KI-L55), only when the item predicted a non-test touch-set. Fail-open on a null/malformed probe, and a probe that could not run records `SKIPPED` on the gates map — silence never reads as clean (KI-E20/KI-E41 posture). **Two real defects found while building it, both fixed here.** (1) `ROOTCAUSE_SCHEMA` initially used a `count` field, which collided with `COMMENT_SCHEMA`'s `count` in `_execsmoke.mjs`'s schema-property-sniffing stub — where zero means GOOD and here it means FAILURE. The exec-smoke caught it by driving every lane to FAILED. Renamed to `nonTestCount`: an identically-named field with inverted polarity is a footgun for any shape-keyed consumer, not just the harness. (2) The shared `isTestPath` predicate (hoisted here out of two byte-identical copies in `touchedRootCause` and `debrisFiles`) carried `/i` on its FILENAME arm, so `[^/]*Tests?\.cs$` matched any source file merely ENDING in those letters — `Contests.cs`, `Manifests.cs`, `Protests.cs` were all classified as tests. That is a live false-positive in two shipped deterministic checks: P9 would read a fix touching only such a file as "tests-only" and FAIL a correct fix, and `debrisFiles` (which never flags a test file) would let a same-named scratch file through. The filename arm is now case-SENSITIVE on `Test` (.NET test types are PascalCase by universal convention); the DIRECTORY arm stays `/i` as the backstop for repos not using PascalCase filenames, and both directions are pinned.
