Also fixed: three selftest pins on the `build-test.sh` case pattern were pinned to the exact alternation literal and broke on every lint added to it — re-pinned on MEMBERSHIP, because a pin that fails for a purely additive correct change trains readers to edit pins reflexively. One of them (`indexOf('leftovers\|comments)')`) had been silently VACUOUS since KI-E91 extended the pattern: it returned -1, and `-1 < indexOf(...)` is trivially true, so the "lints dispatch before the host override" assertion had been passing for the wrong reason and would no longer have caught a regression. |
| KI-E105 | **KI-L41's retry economics were one-sided: they could only EXTEND the budget, never shorten it.** `isStrictlyNarrower` grants a bonus round when the blocking-finding set shrinks; nothing anywhere detected the opposite. An item whose findings were flat or GROWING round over round still consumed its full `maxItemRetries` allowance — and a band is the most expensive unit of work the factory has (a FULL band is ~5 opus gates + review.code + review.adversarial + refuter + multi-lens re-audit; KI-E88 measures the gate panel alone at ~4x LIGHT). Spending band three on a trajectory that has twice failed to make ANY numerical progress is close to pure waste, and it delays the human review the item evidently needs. The comparison data already existed and was simply unused: `applyConvergenceBonus` persists `row.convergence` on EVERY fold, not only on rounds it grants a bonus for. **Fix**: `isNotConverging` + `applyStallDetection` + `isStalled` in `lib/convergence.mjs` (reading the same field the bonus path writes, so the two directions cannot disagree about what "the prior round" was), a new `maxStallRounds` config knob (default 2, `0` disables), and a single parking decision — `escalateExhausted` now parks on EITHER stop condition, with the transition note naming WHICH one, so scheduling and parking can never diverge. Ordering subtlety handled explicitly: `applyConvergenceBonus` overwrites `row.convergence` as it goes, so the driver snapshots the prior summary BEFORE the bonus pass and hands it to the stall pass. **Deliberately conservative in three ways**, because a false stall parks work that would have closed: it is the strict complement of PROGRESS rather than of `isStrictlyNarrower` (a round that reduces findings while max severity worsens earns no bonus yet is NOT a stall — real progress was made); it requires both rounds to carry comparable gate data, so a pre-gate failure never counts; and it needs N CONSECUTIVE stalled rounds, with any progress resetting the streak to zero. |
| KI-E106 | **`acceptance` is the contract every downstream stage grades the diff against — and nothing BLOCKED on its quality.** The acceptance-scan splits it into clauses, `gate-po` signs off against it, the re-auditor confirms it, KI-E22 lints its named surfaces — yet it is hand-authored and the only check was KI-E22's advisory WARN. That asymmetry is expensive in a specific way: a badly-scoped item does not fail cheaply, it buys ~15-25 agent calls of expensive argument, fails, and re-bands up to `maxItemRetries + maxBonusRounds` times. KI-E18's own row records acceptance-clause coverage gaps as 4/4 of one cycle's band FAILs, and KI-E27 concedes outright that a raw ingested issue "rarely states a checkable acceptance + regressionTest". **Fix**: `lib/readiness.mjs` — a deterministic pre-`group` gate checking three things an author can satisfy unambiguously: the acceptance yields >= 1 clause the KI-E18 splitter would actually probe (reusing that SAME splitter, so the gate and the probe cannot disagree about what "checkable" means), a `regressionTest` description exists and is not a placeholder (`n/a`/`TBD`/`?` are ABSENT, not stated), and `files[]` is non-empty (an empty lock set silently disables the KI-E14 lock, makes the KI-E22 lint vacuous, AND short-circuits the fold's P9 to PASS). `cmdGroup` REFUSES to schedule unready items, printing every problem at once in stable order, with an explicit `--force-unready` override. **Blocking, unlike the KI-E29/KI-E90 scheduling warns, and the distinction is deliberate**: those flag risks that may not bite and that a different batch composition could avoid, whereas an unready item is defective in ITSELF and no scheduling luck rescues it. **Scope bound, deliberately narrow**: it judges only what can be decided MECHANICALLY about the item's own text — never whether the acceptance is *correct* or *well-scoped*, which is an authoring judgment. A gate that guessed at that would reject real work and train operators to reach for the override reflexively, which is how a blocking gate degrades into noise. `blocked`-tier items are skipped (they await an owner ruling and are not scheduled anyway). |
| KI-E107 | **The report could say which models were called and how items ended, but not the one number that governs cost: what share of the work went to items that never closed.** `agg.models` and `agg.outcomes` were both derived from the SAME `item_folded` event and never crossed. Given six prior entries about this factory's own numbers being wrong or absent (KI-E40 rendered a 100%-passing gate as 0%; KI-E46 read a 45% recovery rate as 14%; KI-E48 had two structurally dead sections; KI-E44 lost usage on a kill; KI-E66 found cache telemetry never existed; KI-E33 found session telemetry silently off), the absence of a yield figure meant no cost argument could be settled with evidence. **Fix**: `agg.callsByOutcome` + `renderCallsByOutcome` (pure, selftest-pinned arithmetic) render a per-outcome table with items, calls, share AND calls-per-item — that last column is what exposes a FAILED item costing more than a CLOSED one — plus an explicit yield line and a FAILED-spend line. Call counts are the unit because KI-E66 established per-item TOKENS are structurally unrecoverable inside the Workflow runtime; this is a call-weighted proxy, labelled directional, never a bill. **"Wasted" deliberately means FAILED only**: an ESCALATED item reached a human with its analysis intact and a BLOCKED one surfaced a real scope question — both are the system working, and the footnote says so rather than letting a reader infer a harsher number. Report-side only; no emit path touched (KI-E7 — telemetry is observational, never evidentiary). |
| KI-E108 | **Nothing distinguished a genuinely-failing test from an intermittently-failing one**, and the cost of that confusion is a whole band: a flaky failure re-bands the item, while a flaky PASS is worse — KI-E65 records an item folded CLOSED while carrying a newly-introduced flaky test, caught only because the operator cross-checked the note against the ledger by hand. **Fix**: `flakeSuspects` in `lib/verify.mjs` uses the one flake signal that is deterministic and free — the SAME test class appearing with BOTH a passing and a failing filter result in one transcript, re-paired START->RESULT by the identical 1:1 emission contract KI-E70 already relies on. Wired into the fold as a named WARN over both the verify and integrate transcripts. **Deliberately ADVISORY, and this bound is the design**: a fail-then-pass sequence is ALSO the legitimate cycle-8 retry shape (fix, re-run, green) that `lastMatch` intentionally resolves as last-wins, so promoting this to a blocking signal would fail the very shape the engine explicitly supports. It only NAMES the class, letting a human tell "retried after a fix" from "this test is unstable" — pinned in both directions, including an assertion that a retry-shaped transcript still PASSES `verdictFromParse` while still being named. |
| KI-E109 | **Main-tree contamination had SEVEN detection passes (KI-L65/E41/E45/E50/E35/E82/E89) and ZERO prevention** — because KI-E50 explicitly parked the choice: "prevention (sandboxing main read-only vs failing the lane fast) remains a host-owner policy decision the engine deliberately does not make." That left the decision permanently unmade rather than available. **Fix**: `failLaneOnMainDrift`, a third entry in the existing `lib/policy.mjs` seam (the same KI-E57/KI-E58 mechanism, not a bespoke flag), OFF in the shipped engine. When ON, the `⚠ MAIN-DRIFT` marker the KI-E50 verifyHint ALREADY has the runner paste verbatim into its own note fails the lane immediately — before the gate band, and long before the fold's KI-L65 check — instead of only warning. Adds no new detection: it only decides what the factory DOES with a signal it already collects. Default OFF is byte-for-byte the historical warn-only behaviour, so no host's posture changes without an explicit opt-in. |
| KI-E134 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-09-04) — three fixes shipped together in direct response to that session's own KI-E133 incident review: a real PREVENTION mechanism for main-tree contamination (not just another detector), a real fix for plan-drift (not just another downstream scan), and a real correctness bug found while investigating both.** **(i) `build-test.sh` worktree-boundary guard.** Every contamination incident that session traced to the same root cause: `build`/`red`/`filter`/`suite` resolve their `$target` against whatever the CALLING shell's cwd happens to be, and because every factory worktree AND the main tree are full checkouts of the SAME repo, a wrong-cwd invocation frequently does NOT error (no MSB1009) — it silently SUCCEEDS against the wrong tree's copy of that path, giving the caller no signal anything is wrong. A new `_guard_worktree_path` helper refuses (exit 65, loud stderr `FACTORY::WORKTREE-GUARD::REFUSED`) unless the resolved target lives under `state/worktrees/<id>/`. Deliberately scoped to ONLY the `dotnet`-invoking subcommands (`build`/`red`/`filter`/`suite`) — the read-only git-diff lints do not share the silent-wrong-tree failure mode and a synthetic/placeholder path is a legitimate way to exercise their wiring, confirmed live when the first unscoped draft of this guard broke exactly such a fixture in `opencode/_selftest.mjs`'s own mechanized "mech leftover" simulation. Ported here for the `build`/`filter`/`suite` cases and the `red` case, matching this repo's `verify/build-test.sh` shape as of the port (this repo does not yet carry the KI-E482/KI-E132(ii) self-tee `_redout` mechanism the origin session's `red` case also touched, so only the guard call — not the redout positional-arg fix — applies here). **(ii) `pathToken`/`phantomManifest` object-shape bug.** NOT ported: the origin fix patches an object-shape bug in `pathToken`/`phantomManifest` (KI-E471), and neither exists yet in this repo's `lib/verify.mjs` — recorded here only so a future KI-E471 port carries this fix from the start rather than reintroducing the same bug. **(iii) Plan-drift prevention at the source, ported to BOTH runtimes.** Two items in that session's own batch died in `KI-E87` PROSE mode with "no evidence in the diff after one bounded amend" — PROSE mode's `hasPlanCommitmentLanguage` prefilter is a coarse keyword heuristic, weaker than STEP mode's per-step evidence check, and `steps` stayed intentionally optional in `PLAN_SCHEMA` (KI-E101), so a planner could describe substantive commitment-language work in prose and never decompose it, silently routing into the weaker check. Fix: when a FRESH plan's own approach/blastRadius carries commitment language (`MUST`/`required to`) but `steps` came back under a 2-entry floor, ONE cheap bounded follow-up call (`PLAN_STEPS_NUDGE_SCHEMA`) asks specifically for the missing decomposition — the same "one bounded amend" shape used everywhere else in this pipeline, never a second full plan, and skipped entirely for the common case at zero extra cost. Ported to `_workflow/opencode/runtime.mjs` as its own phase (`plan-steps-nudge`) per this repo's own Fix #20 (`opencode/_selftest.mjs` mechanically deep-equals every `factory.js` `*_SCHEMA` against a same-named `schemas.mjs` export), so shipping the schema without the runtime wiring would have been a mechanically-caught partial state. **Verification (origin session):** real behavioral tests via the origin repo's `exec-smoke` harness, not source-text pins; origin selftest 1626 → 1654 passed, 0 failed. This repo's own selftest is re-verified green after the port (see below). |
| KI-E133 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-09-04) — SEVERITY: CRITICAL, a subagent violated KI-E1 ("NO mutating git, ever") and ran a destructive `git reset`/`clean` against the MAIN repository, not a worktree, mid-Workflow.** During a 13-item batch on the origin host, `git reflog` on the HOST repo showed a `git reset --hard` with NO corresponding `driver.mjs` code path (audited: every git invocation in `_workflow/*.mjs` is either read-only or `-C <worktree>`-scoped), combined with an unscoped `git clean -fd`. **Blast radius:** every uncommitted, tracked, main-tree change standing at that moment was wiped back to HEAD — that session's own in-flight tooling fixes (silently reverted mid-batch, so the latter half of the same batch ran against the re-broken tooling, reproducing a KI-E132(ii)-class litter-file bug live a second time), the ledger (silently un-recording prior recoveries), and every regenerated report. **Attribution: circumstantial, not proven** — one runner agent had file activity in the reset's exact window and a repeat wrong-cwd fingerprint, but no agent's own self-reported artifacts across all 13 items mention running `git reset`/`clean`; the full transcript set was not read in full to avoid a context-overflow, so this is not a closed investigation. **Recovery (origin session, verified complete):** `state/items/` is `.gitignore`d and untouched by `reset`/`clean` (respects the ignore by default) — every per-item checkpoint, recovery-fold JSON, and gate verdict survived intact; the tooling fixes were re-applied, `driver.mjs reconstruct` + `fold` rebuilt the ledger identically to its pre-incident state, and the origin selftest reconfirmed green after the redo. **No prevention fix shipped in the incident itself** — the existing contamination-DETECTION net (KI-E61 auto-repair, KI-E89 unclaimed-drift, KI-E41/KI-E45 main-guards, and this repo's own KI-E109 `failLaneOnMainDrift`) is what made it recoverable, but all of it is detect-after-the-fact; nothing stopped a subagent from running the command in the first place. KI-E134(i) (above) is the prevention fix this incident produced. **Process mitigation adopted (not a code fix):** commit controller-authored factory-tooling changes immediately after verifying them (selftest green), BEFORE launching a Workflow batch — `git reset --hard`/`clean -fd` cannot touch what is already committed. A durable technical fix (sandboxing/restricting what a subagent's Bash tool can execute against the HOST repo root) is a real gap this incident exposes but is outside what a prompt-level or driver.mjs-level change can close — flagged for the operator, not resolved. |
| KI-E132 | **Ported from a host-mount session (2026-09-04) — two RED-evidence-integrity bugs found investigating a batch fold: a data-staleness bug that had ALREADY wrongly FAILED several fully-approved closes, and an off-by-one in the KI-E482 self-tee fix.** **(i) `fold`'s P1 `verificationOnly` check trusted the EMBEDDED `r.verificationOnly` flag** (a copy captured whenever the checkpoint/result object was produced) while re-deriving the RED evidence itself fresh from disk (`verify-red-raw.txt`) — a freshness mismatch. When an item is re-claimed for a later, redundant re-verification pass, that pass's fresh `test.json`/`verify-red-raw.txt` overwrite the very files P1 reads, but nothing updates the embedded flag, which came from an earlier (often `reconstruct`-recovered) checkpoint. Fold then reads: embedded flag = stale `false`, disk evidence = a genuine verificationOnly `exit=0` re-confirmation — and misreads the second as a vacuous test on the first. Live on the origin host: three items with a COMPLETE, fully-gate-APPROVED (or fully re-verified) closure each folded to FAILED by this bug alone. **Fix:** P1 now re-reads `test.json` fresh (`readIf`, same freshness tier as `verify-red-raw.txt`) and recomputes `verificationOnly` with the exact formula `factory.js` uses to set it (`test.verificationOnly===true && !test.red`); on disagreement with the embedded flag, the on-disk value wins (it is the live attestation) and a diagnostic line names the mismatch. The corrected value is written back onto `r.verificationOnly` so later checks that also read it (P9 root-cause-touch, the `filesChanged` guard) inherit the fix automatically. **This repo's own `fold` already carried this exact re-derivation pattern independently** (its `KI-L55`/`KI-L66` handling, immediately below the ported code) — the port slots in alongside it without conflict, since the two check different freshness windows (embedded-vs-disk here; contradicted-flag-vs-genuine-red there). **(ii) `build-test.sh red`'s KI-E482 self-tee off-by-one:** on the origin host, `_redout="${3:-}"` read the SAME positional slot already assigned to `filter` two lines above, so the "optional 3rd arg" was never actually optional — it silently equalled the dotnet filter STRING on every `red` invocation, and since a filter never contains `/`, the transcript got `cp`'d to a file literally named after the test filter in whatever directory happened to be current: harmless litter inside a worktree, real main-tree contamination when cwd was the repo root. **NOT ported**: this repo's `verify/build-test.sh` does not yet carry the KI-E482 self-tee `_redout` mechanism at all (see KI-E134(i) above), so there is no target for this specific off-by-one here — recorded so a future KI-E482 port carries the fix from the start. |
| KI-E113 | **`.claude/` was never gitignored in the engine repo, so a routine `git add -A` would have committed per-host session settings, a full checkout of the repo inside itself, and a generated copy of tracked content.** Found 2026-09-02 while preparing a commit: `git status` showed `?? .claude/` — 124 files, 1.3 MB, untracked and NOT ignored. This is the KI-E26 class ("host data must never enter this engine repo"), and the original sweep enumerated every sibling (`state/`, `reports/`, `queue/`, `config/factory.config.local.json`, `verify/build-test.local.sh`, `agents/repo-profiles/*`, `telemetry/`) but missed this one. **Why it was missed is the interesting part, and is recorded so the distinction is not re-lost:** `.claude/` is the ONE path that IS legitimately tracked in a *host* repo — its `.claude/rules/*.md` are the acceptance rules the factory enforces, and `worktree.mjs`'s KI-L67 sparse-checkout exists specifically to strip that tracked copy out of each worktree so the ~55k-token rules baseline is not paid twice. So the path does not read as runtime state at a glance. In the ENGINE repo it is never engine content, for three independent reasons: (1) `.claude/settings.local.json` is per-host session config — KI-E33 and KI-E52 both write the OTLP env block, including endpoints, into precisely that file; (2) `.claude/worktrees/<branch>/` are REGISTERED git worktrees (confirmed via `git worktree list`: one sat on `feature/recovery-auto-gen-mainsweep-redprobe` with its own `.git`), so committing one is actively BROKEN rather than merely noisy, and the Claude Code worktree feature recreates them — ignoring the current contents once would not have held; (3) `setup/init.mjs` INSTALLS the controller skill into `.claude/skills/`, so running `init` against this repo (dogfooding) generates a copy of the tracked `claude-assets/` source, and committing the copy would let it silently drift from the original it was generated from. **Fix**: `.claude/` added to `.gitignore` with the engine-vs-host distinction spelled out at the ignore line itself (that distinction is the whole reason for the miss, so it belongs where the next reader will be). Purely preventive — nothing under `.claude/` was tracked, so no `git rm --cached` was needed. Selftest pins BOTH directions, by intent rather than by current directory contents: `.claude/` is ignored, AND `claude-assets/` — the shipped source `init.mjs` installs FROM — is NOT, so a future over-broad pattern that swallowed the engine's own assets fails loudly. |
| KI-E112 | **Every remaining disclosed-but-unfixed gap, closed — the `UNPORTED` set in `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` is now EMPTY.** KI-E103 built the runtime-parity gate and honestly recorded four stages the port did not implement; KI-E104 added a fifth. A disclosed gap is better than a hidden one, but it is still a gap: on every one of them an item could pass in the port that canon would have failed. **Stages ported.** (1) **RED-proof (KI-E83)** — `afterVerify` now re-reads `verify-red-raw.txt` from DISK via `parseRedRaw(decodeTranscript(...))`, applying canon's exact contract including KI-L55's INVERTED `verificationOnly` polarity (a pinning test must PASS, exit 0). The port previously trusted the test-author's self-reported `test.red` until fold time — the precise divergence that let ITEM-H24 and ITEM-H26 each burn a FULL band with 9 and 8 gates APPROVED. `parseRedRaw` had been imported and unused since the port was written. (2) **Root-cause touch (KI-E104)** — `nonTestChanged(changedFiles(worktree))` through the SAME `lib/verify.mjs` derivation the fold's P9 uses, gated identically, recording `mech:rootcause-touch` and announcing `SKIPPED` on an unreadable worktree. (3) **Debris (KI-D1)** — `debrisFiles` was not even imported; a teed factory artifact or scratch file reached the gate band. (4) **Plan-commitment / plan-step (KI-E87 + KI-E101)** — the full trio (probe, ONE bounded amend, one final re-probe) mirroring the acceptance trio, with both STEP and PROSE modes, sharing canon's existing `probe:plan-commitment-scan` gate key and stamping the mode into the headline. This required retaining the planner's output on `progress` (the port's analogue of KI-E87 hoisting `plan` to function scope in canon) — without it the stage would have dispatched and silently found nothing to check, which is why the selftest pins the retention AND pins that the acceptance phase actually advances INTO the new stage: a stage nothing routes to is dead code that looks implemented. (5) **Ledger-anchor (KI-E91)** — mechanical STEP-1 via the engine-owned `build-test.sh ledger-anchor`, gated exactly as canon gates it (only when the item declares a `*STANDARDS-DIVERGENCE-LEDGER.md` path, so a free no-op for hosts without one), then a `ledger_anchor_classify` agent step mirroring `leftover_classify`. **Non-stage gaps closed in the same pass**, each individually small and each a real divergence: the KI-E74B `verifyNote` channel is now POPULATED rather than being a dead render (canon fills it from the runner AGENT's self-caveat; this port has no runner agent, so the faithful analogue is the caveats its own DETERMINISTIC pass produced — a check that could not run, a KI-E108 flake-suspect class — which unlike agent prose cannot be self-serving); `integ.branch` now reaches `res.branch`, so the folded row carries the branch the human is meant to review and commit, the one hand-off the factory exists to produce (KI-E1); the host's configured `gateSet` is finally READ (both `gateRolesFor` call sites passed `null`, silently ignoring a host that customised `config/factory.config.json` — harmless only while that config happens to equal the hardcoded default, which is exactly what made it a latent trap rather than a visible bug); and the dead `touchedRootCause`/`appendRaw` imports are removed. **Three false statements in this registry and its docs, corrected rather than left standing.** KI-E91's port-status note claimed "this repo has no `schema-parity` selftest gate" — false; that gate exists in `opencode/_selftest.mjs` and is run by the main suite. The accurate claim was that no RUNTIME-parity gate existed, which KI-E103 then built. KI-E60 claimed the port scopes repo profiles "to non-probe roles only exactly like its own brief-reading block" — factually wrong; the profile block sits OUTSIDE `compose.mjs`'s `!/-probe$/` branch, and both the port's own comment and its selftest assert the opposite. `CLAUDE.md` still said "currently 972 asserts". Also refreshed: `factory.js`'s own `meta.description` (it omitted comment-scan, ledger-anchor and root-cause — the file was describing a pipeline it no longer ran) and `claude-assets/skills/ai-factory/SKILL.md`'s stage list (it omitted six stages, so the Claude-Code controller's own manual understated what a run does). Selftest: 1347 → 1368; opencode 187/0. **Remaining gaps are structural only, and all three are inherent rather than unimplemented**: per-call model tiering (KI-O1 — `Task` accepts no model parameter), Copilot's lack of independent subagent dispatch (KI-O4), and `infraSuspect`/KI-L53 (the submit protocol has no null-agent state to express — every key requires a real submit). |
| KI-E111 | **The three residual failures KI-E102 disclosed as "host-capability gaps, not engine defects" were re-examined on the owner's instruction to fix them — and TWO of the three turned out to be genuine engine defects that the Windows environment merely made visible.** Assuming they were environmental was the wrong call, and this row records the correction. **(1) `shimAvailable` could never return true on Windows — a byte-for-byte recurrence of the KI-E72 false-negative it was WRITTEN to fix, one platform over.** It tested `statSync(f).mode & 0o111`, but NTFS has no execute attribute and Node synthesises `mode` from the read-only flag alone, so that expression is 0 for EVERY file — verified against this repo's own shipped `verify/build-test.sh`, which reports mode `0o666`. Consequence: `dotnetAvailable()` and `preflight()` reported "dotnet: ABSENT — build/test verify cannot run on this host" on ANY Windows host relying on the KI-E17 shim, which is exactly the live 2026-08-07 symptom KI-E72 exists to prevent. It matters concretely because the shim is never executed via its mode bit on Windows anyway: `_workflow/opencode/buildtest.mjs` spawns `build-test.sh` through Git Bash, which runs a `.sh` regardless of NTFS attributes. **Fix**: on win32 the honest test is existence-of-a-FILE (an `isFile()` guard keeps the relaxation from degrading into "always true", and a directory is still rejected); the POSIX path keeps the mode check byte-for-byte unchanged. The selftest now asserts the REAL contract per platform rather than skipping. **(2) `writeJsonAtomic`'s `renameSync` had no Windows retry — and it is the engine's ONLY durable-write primitive.** `rename` over an existing target is atomic-and-always-succeeds on POSIX, but on Windows it is a `MoveFileExW` that fails with a transient sharing violation (EPERM/EACCES/EBUSY) whenever anything momentarily holds the destination open — antivirus, the search indexer, a concurrent reader. KI-E102 recorded the live symptom (an intermittent `EPERM … opencode-progress.json.tmp -> opencode-progress.json` that passed on re-run) and speculated it "may be a real portability defect"; it is. Because every ledger update, report, run-args write and opencode checkpoint funnels through this one function, a dropped write strands real state — the observed case loses an item's in-flight progress. **Fix**: bounded retry (5 attempts, 10/20/30/40ms backoff, ~100ms worst case) gated on win32 AND on those three codes only, so a genuine error still throws immediately and POSIX takes the pre-existing single-attempt path byte-for-byte. The backoff is a real synchronous sleep via zero-dep `Atomics.wait`, never a CPU-burning busy-wait, and is wrapped so a runtime that disallows it degrades to an immediate retry rather than throwing. A FAILED write now also removes its temp file — a stray `<path>.tmp.<pid>` is precisely the non-canonical debris KI-E42's relaunch scanner flags, so a failed write would otherwise have poisoned the next resume. **(3) The `bash -n` check was the only genuine host-capability gap — but it was reporting it dishonestly.** All three `bash -n` calls sat inside one try/catch whose failure message was "install.sh / release.sh / _e2e.sh all parse", so a host with no usable bash produced a scary FALSE failure implying the shipped release tooling has a syntax error. An errno check would not have separated the two either: on Windows `bash` typically resolves to the WSL launcher stub, which does not throw ENOENT but runs and exits non-zero. **Fix**: probe `bash -c "exit 0"` separately first; a usable bash runs the real syntax gate (now surfacing the actual stderr on failure instead of a bare boolean), an unusable one prints an announced SKIP naming the assert count — the same posture as this file's git-unavailable fixtures and KI-E102's own filename skip. CI runs `ubuntu-latest`, so the shipped scripts remain syntax-gated on every push. **Result: `node _workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs` now runs 1347 passed / 0 failed on Windows — green on that platform for the first time**, alongside the opencode suite's 187/0. |
| KI-E110 | **KI-E88's band-mix line told the operator the SHAPE of a batch but never its SIZE**, leaving its own "roughly a 4x gate-panel difference" as prose to be re-derived mentally each time. **Fix**: `lib/band-cost.mjs` projects a batch in AGENT CALLS — the exact unit `item_folded`'s cost map records and KI-E107's new yield table aggregates — so a projection can be compared against last cycle's real calls/item and calibrated, instead of quietly becoming folklore. A projection in a different unit from the measurement is uncheckable by construction; a currency or token figure would be strictly worse, since KI-E66 established per-item tokens are not recoverable and any such number would be fabricated. The per-stage counts are DERIVED from what `runItem()` actually dispatches (base stages + the band's role-gate panel + its review/refute/lens set + the planner only for non-mechanical items), and the rendered line states explicitly that it is a FLOOR — retries, amends, adjudication and re-gates all add calls. Under-promising is the safe direction for a planning aid: an operator surprised by a cheaper batch changes nothing, one surprised by an expensive batch has already spent it. `suggest` renders it against the ready pool using the SAME `bandFor` the scheduler uses, so projection and scheduling cannot disagree about a band. |
| KI-E103 | **The opencode/Copilot binding (KI-O1/KI-O4) had silently FORKED from `factory.js`: one drifted routing constant that made a class of items structurally unclosable in the port but closable in canon, five result-shape divergences that fed downstream consumers `undefined`, and no mechanism anywhere capable of detecting any of it.** Found 2026-09-02 by a directed parity audit (owner ask: "make sure copilot and opencode are on par"). **Root cause of the whole class: there was no RUNTIME-parity gate.** The only mechanical cross-check was SCHEMA parity, and its own documented workaround — export the schema, omit it from the `SCHEMAS` registry, add a `NOT yet ported` comment — is exactly how KI-E83/KI-E87/KI-E91 shipped unported while green. Anything with no schema surface (a guard, a phase re-order, a prompt hint, a routing constant) was structurally invisible. **The proof case is KI-E97**: it narrowed `REALINFRA_SIGNAL`'s bare `concurren` catch-all after five live false-positive incidents and added hyphen-normalised separators + `concurrencyexception` — in `factory.js` only. KI-E97's own row lists no opencode mirror (unlike KI-E92's, which explicitly lists all four sites), so the drift was unnoticed, not accepted. Re-verified live before fixing: the stale copy false-fired on all four documented incident shapes ("~10 concurrent 100MB downloads", "do not group concurrently with X", "serialize against any concurrent work", "max 10 concurrent)") — each then DEMANDS a Testcontainers marker the item's fix cannot produce, i.e. **unclosable in the port, closable in canon** — and false-negatived the hyphenated `unique-constraint` shape, losing its real-infra floor. **Fixes, six behavioural + one mechanism.** (1) `REALINFRA_SIGNAL` re-ported byte-identically. (2) `runtime.mjs`'s plan phase no longer overwrites `res.rootCauseFiles` with the planner's self-reported `plan.files` — it contradicted the port's OWN cmdInit comment and made the fold's P9 grade the diff against the planner's opinion of scope instead of the work item's, defeating the self-certification check P9 exists to be. (3) `gateDetails['probe:acceptance-scan']` now uses canon's `{verdict, headline, findings}` instead of the probe's raw `{covered, gaps}`: `lib/feedback.mjs` reads `d.verdict` and iterates `d.findings`, so the port had been rendering `## probe:acceptance-scan - undefined` and **silently dropping every un-evidenced clause** from feedback.md — the KI-L31 authoritative channel a reFix reads FIRST. The gate is also now recorded on the covered:true first pass (canon writes it unconditionally; the port only wrote it on the reprobe path, so a passing scan left no evidence it ran). (4) `res.gates['reaudit']` (the `code=ok edge-case=no` roll-up) is now recorded — its absence broke two consumers: `lib/recover.mjs missingStageFrom()` parses that exact string to derive which lens set a recovery must re-run, and KI-E40's telemetry classifier counts the all-`key=ok` family as ok, so a missing key read as 0%. (5) `gateDetails['adjudicator']` is recorded, and a P8 re-gate now writes `<key>:re-gate` instead of OVERWRITING the original dissent's detail — `lib/recover.mjs` filters `:re-gate` rows precisely because it expects both to exist. (6) `REFUTE_OK` is pushed when the refuter passes, not only after all lenses converge, so a refuter-passed/lens-failed item no longer ends its transitions at `GATED` — the shape `missingStageFrom()` keys on to auto-scaffold a re-auditor-only recovery. (7) **The mechanism, and the actual point of this entry**: new pure `lib/port-parity.mjs` (`extractFactoryRoles`/`extractPortRoles`/`namedConstantSource`/`parityGaps`) + a machine-checked manifest `_workflow/opencode/stage-parity.mjs` declaring every canonical stage as DISPATCHED, MECHANICAL (deterministic Node instead of an agent — `runner`, `marker-probe`, `comment-probe`, each of which is arguably STRONGER than the agent it replaces, having no self-report that can diverge from disk) or UNPORTED-with-a-reason (`red-proof-probe`, `plan-commitment-probe`, `ledger-anchor-probe`, and since KI-E104 `rootcause-probe`). The selftest fails on an undeclared stage, a stale declaration (declared-but-actually-dispatched), a dead one (declared-but-gone-from-canon), a contradictory one, a sub-40-char placeholder reason, and on any byte-drift in the shared constants — proven non-vacuous by asserting the extractor finds >=15 real roles and by an injected-stage simulation. Following KI-E59's own lesson (the owner's words: "MECHANISMS that prevent … not just more prompt rules"), this is a forcing gate, not a reminder — and it proved itself immediately: the very next stage added to canon (KI-E104's `rootcause-probe`) tripped it on the first run. `_workflow/opencode/README.md` also corrected: it had called `routing.mjs`/`compose.mjs` "**Verbatim** port" (both genuinely drifted) and claimed "the SAME contract (schemas, fold rules, artifact shapes)" while artifact shapes and gate keys differ (`reaudit-<lens>.md` vs `reaudit.md`, `mech:comment-scan` vs `probe:comment-scan`) — it now points at the machine-checked manifest instead of carrying a prose list nothing verified. **Left as disclosed, NOT fixed**: per-call model tiering (KI-O1 — `Task` has no model parameter) and Copilot's lack of independent subagent dispatch (KI-O4) are structural; `infraSuspect`/KI-L53 is structurally inexpressible here (the submit protocol has no null-agent state — every key requires a real submit); budget pre-stop (KI-C2) has no Workflow `budget` global to read. Selftest: 1181 → 1217. |
| KI-E101 | **The plan-vs-diff axis (KI-E87) could only fire when the planner happened to phrase its intent with "MUST"/"required to" — a plan describing the same work as ordinary narrative prose was structurally invisible to it, so on those items the axis silently did not run at all.** `hasPlanCommitmentLanguage` is a COARSE prose gate by design (its own header says so): it decides only "does this text contain SOME checkable promise", and the haiku probe then has to FIND the commitments in free text before it can judge them. That works — it caught the origin session's brownfield-note drift, and the KI-E87 review already had to widen a closed 14-verb allowlist after finding 13 of 14 realistic commitment sentences silently missed — but every widening is chasing the same root shape: the check's reach depends on the planner's word choice, not on the work. **Fix — structured STEP mode, additive, execution UNCHANGED:** `PLAN_SCHEMA` gains an OPTIONAL `steps` (string array; `agents/planner.md` asks for 2-8 ordered, individually checkable one-sentence steps, and warns the planner the list is machine-checked so an aspirational or deferred step is a self-inflicted failure — deferred work is routed to `ruleRisks`). New pure `normalizePlanSteps(steps, cap)` (`lib/plan-commitment.mjs`, inlined byte-identically into `factory.js` per the KI-E2 split, parity selftest-pinned) trims/collapses, strips a hand-written leading ordinal or bullet so the probe's numbering does not double up, dedupes case-insensitively, and merges the over-cap tail into the last entry exactly as `splitAcceptanceClauses` does — nothing authored is ever silently sliced away, since an unchecked step is the precise failure class this entry closes. Its length floor is **12, deliberately BELOW `splitAcceptanceClauses`' 20**: that splitter's floor discards punctuation noise from mechanically splitting a sentence, whereas an entry here is an explicitly authored unit of work, and 20 would silently drop genuine short steps ("Wire the DI seam" is 16, "Add the null guard" is 18). The 4c-bis block now runs **two modes through ONE probe/amend/re-probe/gate tail**: STEP when `normalizePlanSteps(plan.steps).length >= 2` (probes coverage step-by-step, the proven KI-E18 AcceptanceScan shape on the plan axis, with no phrasing dependency), PROSE otherwise (the unchanged KI-E87 prefilter + its verbatim original prompt). A single `axis` noun renders both modes' prompts/headline/findings/failure note; the mode is stamped into the headline (`[STEP mode]`/`[PROSE mode]`) so `gateDetails`/`feedback.md` is self-describing; both modes share the EXISTING `probe:plan-commitment-scan` gate key, because a second key would silently orphan the telemetry/recover/feedback consumers already keyed off it. **Backward compatibility is the load-bearing property**: `steps` is NOT in `required`, so a planner that omits it, a mechanical item (no planner at all), and a KI-E69 reused prior-attempt plan authored before this field existed all fall through to byte-identical pre-change behaviour — the silent-no-op posture `buildDocMap`/`solutionFor`/`precedent` already use. **SCOPE BOUND, recorded because the originating request was the opposite:** steps decompose CHECKING, never EXECUTION. The owner's initial proposal was a cheap-model worker implementing the plan in parts; that was rejected on this repo's own evidence and the rejection is why the bound exists — `agents/fixer.md`'s SIBLING-PATTERN SWEEP + DEAD-CODE SELF-CHECK (KI-E94), ADJACENT-CLAIM RE-CHECK (KI-E95) and CANCELLATIONTOKEN CHAIN SELF-CHECK (KI-E96) are all whole-item, cross-file consistency checks that a worker holding only its own slice structurally cannot perform, and they guard "fix-introduced defects" (KI-E51), the #1 rejection class; separately, the fixer is ~1 of 15-25 agent calls per item while a FAILED item re-runs the WHOLE band up to `maxItemRetries + maxBonusRounds` times, so raising the fail rate to cheapen one call is net-negative arithmetic (KI-E92 already re-routed that same route in the cautious direction after a close-rate regression), and haiku specifically is pinned OUT of the heavy roles for measured context-ceiling deaths (KI-L64/KI-L58/KI-L56; `worktree.mjs`'s KI-L67 header records the ~120k first-turn baseline against a 200k window). The one-fixer-per-item bound is therefore selftest-pinned BEHAVIOURALLY (an exec-smoke assertion counting fixer calls), not just by comment. Selftest: 1121 → 1181 on this Windows host (+60), including a second `execSmoke` run that executes the STEP-mode FAILURE path end to end — an un-evidenced step must fail the item pre-band after exactly ONE bounded amend and ONE re-probe, with the step text in the note, the gate band provably never dispatched, and sibling lanes unaffected. |
| KI-E102 | **`_workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs` crashed UNCAUGHT on Windows at ~line 987 of 2953, so roughly two thirds of the suite never ran on that platform and `node _workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs` — the command `CLAUDE.md` mandates be green after EVERY change — could not pass there at all.** The KI-E89 quote-path fixture wrote three filenames unconditionally: `café.cs` (legal everywhere), `weird"quote.cs` and `back\slash.cs`. `"` and `\` are RESERVED characters in a Win32 filename, so `writeFileSync` threw `ENOENT` at the second one and aborted the whole run — no assertion failed, nothing was reported, the process simply died mid-file. Found 2026-09-02 while running the mandated selftest before a KI-E101 change on a Windows host; the working tree was clean at HEAD, confirming it as pre-existing rather than change-induced. Notably this is the same platform KI-E59 already calls out as "the very platform the [comment] gate was born on", and the same class as KI-E59's own finding (d) — an `execFileSync('cat')` that was a silent no-op on Windows — so this is the second time a POSIX assumption in engine tooling has degraded silently on Windows specifically. **Fix**: the UTF-8 case stays unconditional (it exercises the octal-escape decode this fixture exists for and is legal on every platform); the two POSIX-only filenames are platform-gated behind `process.platform !== 'win32'` and ANNOUNCE their skip with an explicit count (`SKIP KI-E89 quote/backslash filename decode (2 asserts …)`), so silence never reads as "passed" — the same announce-never-skip-silently posture as KI-E41/E42 and the same `try/catch` + printed-SKIP convention the git-unavailable fixtures in this file already use. POSIX coverage is byte-for-byte unchanged and CI runs `ubuntu-latest`, so the decode assertions still gate every push. **Disclosed, NOT fixed here** (they were unreachable behind the crash and were BELIEVED to be host-capability gaps, not engine defects): with the suite now running to completion on Windows, 2 further failures surface there — `KI-E72 shimAvailable` (asserts a POSIX executable bit, which NTFS does not have) and `KI-E52 bash -n` (no `bash` on the host). A third, `PR#9: the opencode suite runs green`, was observed FAILING once and passing on subsequent runs, with `EPERM: operation not permitted, rename … opencode-progress.json.tmp -> opencode-progress.json` on the failing run — an intermittent Windows atomic-rename contention in the port's checkpoint writer that may be a real portability defect and is recorded here for whoever reproduces it. **CORRECTION (KI-E111, same day): that "host-capability gaps, not engine defects" judgment was WRONG for two of the three.** `shimAvailable` was a genuine engine defect — a recurrence of the very KI-E72 false-negative it was written to fix — and the EPERM rename was a genuine portability defect in `writeJsonAtomic`, the engine's only durable-write primitive. Only the `bash -n` case was a true host gap, and even that was being reported dishonestly. See KI-E111; all three are now fixed and the suite is green on Windows.** | further failures surface there — `KI-E72 shimAvailable` (asserts a POSIX executable bit, which NTFS does not have) and `KI-E52 bash -n` (no `bash` on the host). A third, `PR#9: the opencode suite runs green`, was observed FAILING once and passing on subsequent runs, with `EPERM: operation not permitted, rename … opencode-progress.json.tmp -> opencode-progress.json` on the failing run — an intermittent Windows atomic-rename contention in the port's checkpoint writer that may be a real portability defect and is recorded here for whoever reproduces it. |

| KI-E135 | **Ported from a host-mount session — documentation only, no code.** `standards-evolution.md § 3.1`'s rule-file-update requirement is structurally unsatisfiable from inside a work-item worktree whenever the cited standard lives under a target repo's own rules directory: every factory worktree sparse-checks out that path (KI-L67, "so the rules baseline is not paid twice"), so a fixer cycle scoped to the item's own branch can never stage the required companion rule-file edit — the Architect gate would fail it again for the identical reason every time. Not itself a code fix (the sparse-checkout exclusion is correct and intentional), but a process gap: nothing currently tells the controller/operator "this CHANGES_REQUIRED needs a MAIN-TREE edit, not a worktree recovery." Interim resolution on the origin host: the controller applied the rule-file edit directly in the main tree, then re-dispatched the delta re-gate. Durable fix, not yet built (there or here): an architect-gate finding whose `file` is a target-repo rules path should be mechanically distinguishable (e.g. a structured `routing: "main-tree"` field) so a recovery scaffold can surface it as a distinct step instead of leaving the operator to infer the routing from prose. Recorded here purely so a future engine session recognizes the shape if it recurs. |
| KI-E144 | **Ported from a host-mount session — MAIN-DRIFT FALSE-POSITIVE CROSS-CONTAMINATION, and this repo carried the exact same latent bug.** Live on the origin host, cycle 93: a 6-item batch spanning five UNRELATED services all FAILED identically on "main-tree contamination (KI-E109)". Root cause: `driver.mjs main-check <id>` prints up to TWO independent things — a per-item drift line scoped to that item's own declared files, and a SEPARATE, ALWAYS-appended, repo-wide sweep (KI-E89's "unclaimed" line) that fires on ANY stray path anywhere in the repo regardless of which id was asked about. The KI-E50 runner brief instructs pasting the whole `main-check` output verbatim into the item's own note; `factory.js`'s KI-E109 fail-gate then matched the bare substring `/⚠\s*MAIN-DRIFT/` — unable to distinguish "MY declared files drifted" from "an unrelated path exists somewhere else that nobody has claimed." **Confirmed this repo has the identical vulnerable regex at the identical call site** (`factory.js`'s `failLaneOnMainDrift` gate, and this repo's own `cmdMainCheck` prints the same two-line shape including the literal `MAIN-DRIFT unclaimed (KI-E89)` sweep) — a real, live, not-yet-triggered latent defect here too, only reachable when the OFF-by-default `failLaneOnMainDrift` policy is turned on, same as on the origin host. Fixed identically: the regex is anchored to the item's own id in the exact format `cmdMainCheck` only ever prints for a real per-item hit; the literal token `unclaimed` can never match a real item id. The KI-E50 runner hint was rewritten to explain the two-line shape explicitly. Verification: two new exec-smoke scenarios (an innocent item whose own note carries an unrelated item's real drift line AND the unclaimed sweep — must NOT fail; a guilty item whose own id's line is present — must still fail). Superseded in the same pass by KI-E144B below — see that entry for the final mechanism. Selftest 1405 → 1410 (E144); folded into KI-E144B's own count below since E144B replaces this fix's detection mechanism in the same file region. |
| KI-E144B | **Ported from a host-mount session — the KI-E144 id-anchored regex above was STILL prose-parsing, and broke a different way on the very next retry (same day, origin host).** Live there: a runner, re-run under the KI-E144 fix, correctly determined its own main-check was clean and wrote into its note something like `"No ⚠ MAIN-DRIFT <id> ( line... printed"` — quoting the exact marker format specifically to PROVE its absence. The anchored regex has no concept of negation: it matched the runner's own quoted disproof as if it were the real thing, and failed an item whose runner had explicitly, correctly said nothing was wrong. Owner feedback on seeing this pattern recur, verbatim: *"is that a good idea to do the regex find? this sounds unreliable. try to find a better way."* **Durable fix: stop parsing prose for a verdict at all.** `VERIFY_SCHEMA` gains `mainDriftOwnFiles` (boolean) — the runner's own closed yes/no answer to "did main-check print a real per-item drift line for MY OWN id" — set directly, never inferred from wording. `factory.js`'s KI-E109 gate now reads `verify.mainDriftOwnFiles === true` and nothing else; the id-anchored regex is retired, not layered on top. **General lesson, applied here as it was on the origin host**: grepping a schema-CONSTRAINED enum field (`build`/`targetedTest`, pattern `^(pass|fail)`) is safe — the model cannot express ambiguity in a field the structured-output validator already forces to one of two literal values. Grepping a FREE-TEXT field (`note`, `evidence`) for a marker is never safe, because the same vocabulary can express both "X happened" and "X did NOT happen," and a sufficiently careful, transparent runner is exactly the one MOST likely to write the negation form — the failure mode is triggered BY thoroughness, not carelessness. **NOT ported**: the origin fix's sibling `workreeGuardRefused` boolean (the same fix applied to a post-amend-verify hint) — that mechanism (KI-E478/KI-E485's post-amend re-verify) does not exist in this repo yet, so there is nothing to wire the field into; `opencode/schemas.mjs`'s `VERIFY_SCHEMA` still gains `mainDriftOwnFiles` for schema-parity (canon-only in behavior, per the established KI-E139/E141 schema-parity-only convention). Verification: three exec-smoke scenarios covering the full evolution — cross-contamination with `mainDriftOwnFiles=false` in a note that still contains unrelated drift text (must NOT fail), a genuine own-drift with the field `true` (must fail), and the exact negation shape reproduced directly (prose disproving drift, field correctly `false` — must NOT fail, unlike the live incident). Selftest 1405 → 1410 passed, 0 failed (KI-E144 + KI-E144B together); opencode 189/0 unaffected. **Superseded by KI-E145 below**: `mainDriftOwnFiles` was a real improvement over free-text regex-matching, but still an unverified agent self-report — KI-E145 replaces it with an independent probe that re-derives the fact from disk instead of asking the runner to characterize it at all. |

| KI-E146 | **Ported from a host-mount session.** The KI-E89 unclaimed-drift sweep only ran when an operator remembered to invoke `main-check` by hand — `fold`, which runs every single cycle without fail, never looked for this class of contamination at all. Root cause of the origin host's KI-E144 cascade: a stray test file left by an earlier item sat undetected through that cycle's own fold — the per-item KI-L65 snapshot check only ever validates paths the CLAIMING item's own `files[]` declared, so a file nobody's snapshot claims is structurally invisible to it — then poisoned an entire separate, unrelated batch's main-check sweep the NEXT cycle. A full cycle of silence sat in between, during which nobody had a reason to run `main-check` by hand. **Fix:** `cmdFold` now also runs the KI-E89 `unclaimedMainDrift()` sweep on every fold, aggregating `claimedPaths` fresh from every item's own `main-snapshot.json` (not just the one item a manual `main-check <id>` call would have been scoped to) — read-only, WARN-only, wrapped in try/catch so a missing/corrupt items dir can never fail or half-apply a fold, same posture as every other fold-time detection aid already in this function. This surfaces the gap at the ONE checkpoint that always runs instead of the one that sometimes does. Deliberately still NEVER auto-repaired: `mainguard.mjs`'s own header comment is explicit about why — an unclaimed path could be agent contamination OR an operator's own unrelated work-in-progress sitting in the same tree, and there is no snapshot to prove which; auto-deleting the wrong guess would destroy real, uncommitted human work. Selftest 1410 → 1421 (11 new source-text-pin assertions, matching the same rigor already established for `cmdMainCheck`'s own KI-E89 wiring — a filesystem-fixture integration test was judged disproportionate for this class of never-blocking diagnostic aid). Confirmed KI-E89/`unclaimedMainDrift`/`dirtyMainPaths`/`cmdMainCheck` all already exist in this repo (ported earlier, KI-E86..E89) — no prerequisite gap. |
| KI-E147 | **Ported from a host-mount session — an operational finding, not a code change; recorded so a future contamination triage on THIS repo doesn't fall into the same trap.** On the origin host, KI-E146 confirmed its own value within minutes of shipping — and its FIRST live catch exposed a diagnostic trap: raw file mtime is NOT a reliable signal for which copy (main vs. worktree) is authoritative when contamination strikes an INTERMEDIATE pipeline step. A stray test file's mtime looked, by mtime alone, like the OLDER of two copies (main and worktree both fell "today", main's slightly earlier) — the natural assumption ("main gets an earlier draft, worktree gets the later, correct one") would have discarded it as safely superseded. Cross-referencing the item's OWN checkpoint artifacts proved the opposite: an earlier-stage checkpoint (e.g. `test.json`) was stale from an early attempt, while later-stage checkpoints (`verify.json`/gate-review files) were fresh from today — meaning the worktree's file had not been touched since the early attempt, and the real, later edit had landed in the main tree instead and never reached the worktree at all. A concrete instance of the still-architecturally-unsolved main-tree/worktree contamination mechanism (the origin repo's KI-E133/E136), striking ONE file at a mid-pipeline step while a LATER step in the same attempt correctly wrote the item's other touched file to the worktree — so the worktree ended up internally inconsistent: one file legitimately fresh, the other silently stale, with nothing about the worktree's own state flagging the split. **Lesson, applicable here too:** diagnosing which copy is authoritative requires cross-referencing the item's own checkpoint-artifact timestamps (`plan.json`/`test.json`/`fix.json`/`verify.json`/gate-review files), never a raw main-vs-worktree mtime comparison alone. No selftest applies — this is an investigative technique for a human/operator session, not a code path. |

| KI-E148 | **Ported from a host-mount session — documentation only, no code; a real, deliberately UNFIXED gap in KI-E146's claimed-path check, disclosed here rather than silently absorbed by porting E146 as if it were complete.** KI-E146's "claimed" check treats a path as accounted for if ANY item, ever, once snapshotted it — not whether the item that ACTUALLY dirtied it just now is among the claimants. Live on the origin host: one item cross-contaminated 3 shared k8s secrets files with its own config; the sweep caught the first (correctly unclaimed) but silently passed the other two, because FIVE unrelated, older items had legitimately claimed those exact same shared-secrets paths in their OWN past snapshots — a plausible situation any time several graph items share a config file across the whole ledger's lifetime. `claimedPaths` is a UNION with no provenance check: it answers "has anyone ever had a reason to touch this path", not "does today's dirty content belong to one of its claimants". **Not fixed there, and not fixed here — flagged, not patched.** A real fix needs per-path provenance (e.g., hash the claiming item's OWN copy of the path at ITS claim time and compare against the CURRENT dirty content, not just presence-in-any-snapshot), which is a real design task, not a one-line tweak — and is exactly the class of problem `isolation: 'worktree'` (KI-E149, ported below) would make structurally moot rather than requiring an ever-more-precise claim ledger. Recorded as a known, narrower gap in KI-E146 rather than deepened into a bigger detection mechanism. |

| KI-E149 | **Ported from a host-mount session — the single most significant standing gap this registry named (KI-E133/E136's own words there, "sandboxing what a subagent's own tool calls can reach is outside what a prompt-level or driver.mjs-level change can close") turned out to be wrong, for one class of the problem.** Every main-tree-contamination fix through KI-E148 above was DETECTION after the fact (main-check, the KI-E89/E146 unclaimed sweep) because nothing could PREVENT a subagent's Edit/Write call from landing at the wrong absolute path in the first place. On the origin host, prompted by the owner asking whether Claude Code hooks could help, research established the Agent/Workflow tool's `isolation:'worktree'` option does exactly this at the TOOL layer, with a scope narrower and stranger than hoped, confirmed via four independent LIVE tests against a real isolated agent: (1) an Edit/Write call targeting the shared main checkout — the EXACT failure mode behind the origin host's own KI-E133/E136/E144/E147/E148 incidents — is mechanically REJECTED before any write happens, with an explicit error naming "the shared-checkout path"; (2) an Edit/Write call targeting a DIFFERENT, unrelated git worktree of the same repo — including the very worktree the isolated agent is supposed to treat as its own workspace, `state/worktrees/<id>/`, which is a real, separate `git worktree` and therefore NOT "the shared checkout" — succeeds completely normally, so the factory's existing per-item worktree model needs ZERO redesign; (3) Read/Grep/ls anywhere, including the main checkout, are completely unaffected — full cross-service investigative context-gathering survives untouched; (4) Bash-tool writes (`tee`, `>` redirect, heredocs) to ANY path, including the main checkout, are ALSO completely unaffected — the guard is specific to the Edit/Write TOOLS, not a filesystem sandbox. (4) is a real, disclosed limitation, not a false sense of completeness: the origin host's own KI-E133 incident was a Bash `git reset`/`clean`, a vector this fix does nothing for. **Fix:** a new `needsWriteIsolation(role)` helper in `factory.js` scopes `opts.isolation = 'worktree'` to exactly the two roles that mutate a worktree's tracked SOURCE — `fixer` (and every bounded-amend call that reuses that role literal from acceptance-scan/plan-commitment-scan/docsync-scan/registration-drift-scan/leftover-scan/ledger-anchor-scan) and `test-author` — wired into BOTH the primary and the KI-D10 fallback-model `opts` objects in `call()` (a fix that only isolated the primary path would silently stop protecting the fallback the moment a primary model degraded). A new role-conditional prompt block (mirroring the existing KI-D7 pattern) tells the isolated agent what changed: Edit/Write now only reach its own worktree; the ARTIFACTS DIR instruction in the shared cache-strategic prefix (write your state/items artifacts EXACTLY here) names a path OUTSIDE that worktree, so anything written there now needs Bash (tee/redirect) instead of Edit/Write, which is a deliberate use of finding (4) as the SOLUTION rather than a gap. An explicit host-policy escape hatch (`A.policies.isolateWorktreeWrites === false`) is the only way OFF; unset/missing defaults ON, matching a safety feature's correct default. **This repo's own `_execsmoke.mjs` did not yet capture composed-prompt text at all (the origin host's KI-E470 feature was never ported here)** — extended it to do so (a small, purely-additive, zero-existing-test-risk change) so the ported behavioral tests below can actually verify the isolated agent SEES the hint, rather than silently checking `undefined`. Verification: 5 source-text pins (helper exists, correctly scoped, escape-hatch condition present, both opts sites wired, hint text present) plus 9 real exec-smoke behavioral checks against the live pipeline (every real fixer/test-author call in a codeChange smoke lane carries `isolation:'worktree'` AND sees the hint; every planner/gate/review call in the SAME lane carries neither; the escape-hatch policy actually turns both off). Selftest 1421 → 1435. |

| KI-E150 | **Ported from a host-mount session — precise per-item token attribution.** Only a run-wide token total existed (`budget.spent()`, KI-E23) at cycle end; answering "how many tokens did THIS item cost" was impossible below the whole cycle. Adds an atomic-claim design (a shared `lastSpentGlobal` checkpoint, advanced synchronously with no `await` between read and write, safe under `Promise.all` concurrency) that attributes `budget.spent()`'s marginal increase to the item whose call produced it. Two real bugs found on the origin host via a deliberately discriminating exec-smoke test (every mocked call contributes an identical, known token amount, so each item's own total is exactly verifiable rather than "the grand total roughly adds up"): claiming only on eventual `call()` success leaked a retry-exhausted call's real spend onto whichever call claimed next (fixed via a new `tryAgent` `onAttempt` callback, fired on both the success and the KI-D9 throw/catch paths); the checkpoint-writer calls (deliberately excluded from `tokensUsed` as bookkeeping overhead) never claimed at all, leaking their spend the same way (fixed with `claimTokensSilently()` — advances the shared checkpoint without attributing it to any item). A third, smaller mismatch on the origin host turned out to be a test-harness artifact: the exec-smoke mock resolved every call synchronously, letting `makeLimiter`'s `pump()` batch a whole concurrency-window's calls before any of its claims fired — fixed there by awaiting `agentOverride` in `_execsmoke.mjs` and giving the test's own override a real macrotask-crossing delay. **Ported with one confirmed adaptation, not a blind copy — since resolved.** At the time of this port, this repo had only `checkpointResult`/KI-L40 — `checkpointProgress`/KI-E137 had not been ported yet, so `claimTokensSilently` was wired into the ONE checkpoint call site that existed rather than two, and the ported test's checkpoint-label exclusion predicate checked for both the `:progress:` and `:checkpoint` label shapes even though nothing produced the `:progress:` one here. **KI-E137 has since landed** (see its own entry below) and its `checkpointProgress` call site was wired with `claimTokensSilently` from the moment it was added — the gap this note originally disclosed is closed; the `:progress:` half of the exclusion predicate is no longer dead weight. `ledger.mjs` accumulates `tokensUsed` across relaunch attempts (same posture as `cost`); `driver.mjs` threads it through `item_folded` telemetry and adds a tokens-per-closed-item stat plus a top-10-by-tokens report table. Verification: real behavioral exec-smoke coverage (not source-text pins alone) against this repo's own `smokeBatch()` fixture, ported byte-for-byte from the origin's already-debugged test and passing on the FIRST run here (no adaptation needed for the core token-accounting assertions, only for the checkpoint-site count noted above). Selftest 1435 → 1456 passed, 0 failed (re-run 3× to confirm the timer-based harness fix isn't flaky in this repo either). Opencode 189/0 unaffected. |
| KI-E493 / KI-E495 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a write that lands inside an agent's OWN isolation sandbox (KI-E149) instead of its assigned worktree is NOT rejected by the tool; it succeeds silently in the wrong place, indistinguishable from a correct write until a LATER stage discovers the claimed change is not where it should be.** Live incident on the origin host: a stepwise fixer's per-step `filesChanged` claims resolved against a series of disconnected, throwaway isolation sandboxes instead of the item's real tracked worktree, so a downstream step (and eventually the fold) found none of the claimed work actually present — misreported as a false "owner scope decision" BLOCKED rather than the infrastructure bug it actually was. **Ported here to the two call sites this repo actually has** (this repo has no KI-E470 stepwise fixer — see the not-ported note below — so the origin's other 3 call sites, all stepwise-specific, do not apply): the test-author call and the single-shot fixer call. **Fix, identical mechanism at both sites**: a plain, zero-agent-call, zero-cost string-prefix check — `outOfTreePaths(paths, wtPath)` — filters for any ABSOLUTE claimed path (`testFiles` / `filesChanged`) not rooted at the item's own assigned worktree; a bare relative path is deliberately left unpoliced (an orthogonal question). On a hit, ONE bounded retry (`recoverOutOfTree`) re-issues the SAME call with an explicit path-correction instruction; a still-bad or unavailable retry fails the item immediately and cheaply, before any further pipeline stage spends effort on data that was never real. Skipped entirely when the value came from `item.priorAttempt` (KI-E69 reuse) — nothing NEW ran that round to retry. **Verification**: real exec-smoke behavioral coverage via a dedicated rendering pass (a correct in-tree absolute path never flagged; an out-of-tree path at either call site triggers the retry; a corrected retry succeeds; an uncorrected retry fails the item with a message naming the exact call site and offending path) plus `node --check` and a full apostrophe-escaping scan on the new prompt text. This repo's own selftest stayed green throughout (see the aggregate count on the closing entries of this batch below). |
| KI-E175 | **Ported from a host-mount session — KI-E83's RED-proof marker check only proves SOME marker exists somewhere in verify-red-raw.txt; it never proves that marker belongs to the test CLASS the final diff actually ships.** Live incident on the origin host: test-author's own red-proof covered ONLY an inherited convention test (a legitimate `verificationOnly` judgment); the plan separately mandated a NEW test file, which the fixer created afterward with no red-proof of its own; the final `verify.json` targeted THAT unproven file, whose facts never actually exercised the code path the fix changed, and its own `evidence` string falsely claimed otherwise — three opus-class gate reviewers each independently rediscovered this only after the full gate band ran. **Fix**: a new pre-band `red-coverage-probe` (haiku, cheap), positioned immediately after the (already-present) KI-E83 RED-proof marker probe: cross-references which test class `test.json`'s red-proof (`testFiles`/`runCmd`) covered against which class `verify.json` actually targeted, and only on an apparent mismatch reads the real test source (never the self-reported evidence prose) to judge whether it genuinely reaches the fix's code path. Deliberately read-only — no live revert, consistent with every other pre-band probe in this pipeline. New `RED_COVERAGE_SCHEMA` (`{covered, gap}`), added immediately after `RED_PROOF_SCHEMA`. **Ported verbatim** — this repo's KI-E83 position and `test.json`/`verify.json` shapes matched the origin closely enough to need no adaptation beyond the surrounding call-site context. **`opencode/` binding**: `RED_COVERAGE_SCHEMA` mirrored into `opencode/schemas.mjs` for schema-parity; the stage itself is recorded `UNPORTED` in `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` (this port's `planNext`/`applyPhaseResults` have no equivalent test.json-vs-verify.json class cross-check phase) — purely economic gap, never a correctness hole, since a vacuous test here still gets caught downstream at full gate-band cost. Verification: exec-smoke confirmed the probe dispatches and renders correctly (see the rendering pass under KI-E493/172 above, which exercised this same batch). |
| KI-E176 | **Owner-requested: "log all runs that were continued like this one" — a pause-and-relaunch (`mark-launched --continued`) had no durable record anywhere.** Fix: `cmdMarkLaunched` now detects a `--continued` flag, reads the item's prior `launch-meta.json` before overwriting it, and — only when the new taskId genuinely differs from the recorded prior one — emits a `run_continued` telemetry event via the existing append-only stream and regenerates `reports/continued-runs.md` (new `continuedRunsMd(events)`, appended to `lib/telemetry.mjs`). Ported verbatim; the function and its wiring matched this repo's `telemetry.mjs`/`driver.mjs` shapes with no adaptation needed. Verification: this repo's own selftest exercises `continuedRunsMd` directly plus the `cmdMarkLaunched` wiring; both green. |
| KI-E177 | **Ported from a host-mount session — two related fixes shipped together after a real main-tree contamination incident during this same porting effort: a detection mechanism for leaked write-isolation debris, and a tooling-inconsistency mitigation for the write-isolation hint itself.** **(i) `matchWorktreeDebris`.** An unclaimed dirty path in the main tree that is BYTE-IDENTICAL to a file already present in one of the batch's OWN item worktrees is overwhelming evidence of leaked write-isolation contamination (KI-E149's sandbox writing to the wrong place, the SAME failure class KI-E493/172 above closes at the agent-claim level) — as opposed to genuine, unrelated operator work-in-progress, which the pre-existing KI-E89 unclaimed-drift sweep could not distinguish. New `matchWorktreeDebris(repoRoot, unclaimedPaths, itemIds, worktreesRoot)` (in `lib/mainguard.mjs`, alongside the existing `unclaimedMainDrift`) content-hashes every unclaimed path against every claimed item's own worktree copy of files at the same relative path; a match is reported as HIGH-CONFIDENCE contamination (still never auto-repaired — a human eyeballs and deletes), a non-match falls through to the existing, more conservative KI-E89 "could be either" framing. Wired into both `driver.mjs`'s fold-time unclaimed-drift block and the standalone `main-check` command. **(ii) FILE-WRITE ISOLATION hint Bash/Python fallback.** Live during this same session: an agent in a write-isolated role hit Edit, Write, AND a Bash heredoc all refused for both its assigned worktree and its artifacts dir — a genuine tooling-level inconsistency, not agent error. The KI-E149 hint text gains an addendum naming two confirmed-working fallbacks (a plain single-line Bash redirect instead of a heredoc; a short Bash-invoked Python one-liner) plus an instruction to Read the file back and confirm before trusting a clean exit code, and to note the occurrence rather than stall on it. **Ported with names adapted, logic identical**: `matchWorktreeDebris`/`unclaimedMainDrift` wiring matches the origin's shape at both call sites; the hint addendum text is adapted to NOT reference this repo's own KI-E493 (a different, unrelated mechanism here) since the origin's phrasing cross-referenced its own KI-E493 sibling incident, which does not correspond 1:1 to this repo's KI-E493/172 port above. Also sent as product feedback upstream (a tooling defect, not a factory-logic defect). Verification: pure-function coverage for `matchWorktreeDebris` (byte-identical match / non-match / unreadable-file cases) plus the driver.mjs wiring pins; green. |
| KI-E179 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a completeness-proving regression test is only as good as its own search methodology, and nothing verified a breadth-claiming test's actual scan boundary against what its acceptance criteria claimed to prove.** Live incident on the origin host: an item's acceptance text claimed universal coverage ("no consumer re-persists a plaintext body", "across all ... writers"); its own regression test's regex matched one call-site shape but not a dotted-member-access variant of the exact same call, and its scan root covered one consumer directory while a sibling directory (same interface, same service) held both the unswept offender and several already-converted consumers — three opus-class gate reviewers (developer/qa/security) each independently rediscovered the same gap only after the full band ran, because the sweep and its own proof test shared the identical blind spot. **Fix**: a new pre-band `breadth-claim-probe`, positioned immediately after the (already-present) KI-E18 acceptance-scan, gated on a cheap, zero-agent-call trigger — `BREADTH_CLAIM_RE`, a plain regex over the item's OWN acceptance text for universal-quantifier language (`every`, `all`, `no participating`, `no consumer`, `no handler`, `no site`, `across all`, `platform-?wide`) — so ordinary, bounded-scope items never pay for it. When triggered, the probe independently reads the claim, the new test's actual implementation, and searches the real worktree itself for anything inside the claimed universal set but outside the test's own boundary, reusing the existing `ACCEPT_SCHEMA` shape (`covered`/`gaps`) rather than adding a new one. Ported verbatim, including the regex and the placement relative to the acceptance-scan. **`opencode/` binding**: no schema-parity entry needed (reuses `ACCEPT_SCHEMA`, already mirrored); the stage itself is recorded `UNPORTED` in `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` for the same reason as `red-coverage-probe` above — purely economic gap. Verification: exec-smoke confirmed the probe correctly triggers on breadth-claiming acceptance text and renders the claim/scan-boundary instructions correctly (see the KI-E493/172 rendering pass, which included a dedicated breadth-claiming fixture item). |
| KI-E151 | **Ported from a host-mount session — `cmdDecisionsDigest`'s `question` field rendered the literal text `"[object Object]"` for a row whose `wi.ownerDecision` is a structured `{date, ruling, rationale, source}` object (a batch owner-ruling item) rather than a plain string, the shape every other item in this field uses.** Root cause: `let question = wi.ownerDecision \|\| ''` assigned the object unchanged, then stringified it unguarded downstream. Fix: branch on `typeof wi.ownerDecision` — a string passes through unchanged (zero behavior change for the common case), an object extracts `.ruling` (falling back to `.decision`) as the real display text. Ported verbatim. Verification: behavioral, not a source-text pin — the actual shipped expression is extracted from the file and executed via `new Function` against fixtures covering both real shapes plus an explicit regression guard asserting the exact observed bug string never reappears. |
| KI-E152 | **Ported from a host-mount session — no review-role prompt (edge-scan, the technical gates, method-flow reviews, PO, refuter, re-auditor) ever quoted the runner's OWN deterministic verify result; a reviewer judging code quality had zero visibility into whether the fix even builds, unless it happened to independently re-run the build itself.** Motivating incident on the origin host: a diff collected unanimous APPROVED verdicts across an entire gate band on a runner's confident summary, then failed the deterministic integrate step with hundreds of new test regressions — the compose() REVIEW PACK hint points at a diff snapshot only, and no gate brief anywhere mentions `verify.json`'s `build`/`targetedTest`/`suite` fields. **Fix**: a `verifyEvidenceHint` string, built once from the runner's own already-in-scope `verify` result immediately after `res.transitions.push('GREEN', 'BUILT', 'TESTED')`, appended to EVERY review-role dispatch: edge-scan, all technical gates, the method-flow reviews, PO, refuter, and re-auditor — explicit that a fail verdict or nonzero `suite.failed` means CHANGES_REQUIRED unless it is a known, already-ledgered baseline, never silently overridden by how clean the diff reads. Ported verbatim to all 5 call sites, which matched the origin's pre-fix shape closely enough (byte-identical `extra: null` / bare-string arguments) to need no structural adaptation. Verification: real exec-smoke behavioral coverage, not source-text pins — every hinted role's actual rendered prompt is captured and asserted to contain the evidence line with the real build/targetedTest verdicts interpolated. |
| KI-E154 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a Workflow can survive a laptop sleep in the harness's OWN task tracker in name only: `TaskOutput` kept reporting status "running" for 12+ hours on the origin host while the item's on-disk state sat completely frozen, and `driver.mjs` cannot check this itself (only the controller session holds a `TaskOutput` handle, per KI-E140's own admission).** Fix: new `stallSuspected(itemDir, launchedAtIso, graceMinutes)` — pure, disk-only — compares `launch-meta.json`'s recorded `launchedAt` against the newest mtime of any OTHER file in the item's directory (excluding `launch-meta.json` itself, which is the controller's own bookkeeping write and would otherwise always read as ~launchedAt, masking the exact staleness this exists to catch). A 30-minute grace period avoids flagging a run that is legitimately just quiet inside one long call. Wired additively into `cmdResume`'s existing KI-E140 TASK-LIVENESS line, explicit that it is evidence, not proof — resume stays read-only and advisory. Ported verbatim. Verification: direct-import pure-function tests against real temp-dir fixtures (no launchedAt; unparseable launchedAt; within grace; past grace with zero activity; past grace with real activity; the `launch-meta.json`-exclusion case; an unreadable item dir) plus wiring pins confirming `cmdResume` computes and surfaces the hint. |
| KI-E156 | **Ported from a host-mount session, adapted — a plan's explicit NEGATIVE statements ("no X edits needed", "Y is unchanged", "do NOT touch Z") are just as binding as its positive commitments, and nothing checked a diff against what the plan said NOT to do.** Live incident on the origin host: a plan explicitly stated a certain `ProjectReference` edit was not needed because an existing shared dependency already gave the required transitive compile visibility; the shipped diff added exactly that unneeded reference anyway, breaking a pre-existing architecture-fitness test for a change the plan had already ruled out — and the consolidation pass's own whole-diff sweep judged the result "COHERENT" without ever checking it against the plan's stated exclusion. **Adapted, not ported verbatim**: the origin fix lives in a stepwise-consolidation-only prompt (this repo has no KI-E470 stepwise fixer — see the not-ported note below), so the check is instead spliced into this repo's single monolithic fixer prompt as a `PLAN-EXCLUSION ADHERENCE` instruction, applied before every `applied:true` return rather than at a separate whole-diff pass. `agents/fixer.md` gains a new numbered item 12 with the equivalent guidance, ported near-verbatim. Verification: source-text pins confirming both the prompt text and the fixer.md item exist with the expected content. |
| KI-E157 | **Ported from a host-mount session — two fixes from the same investigation: an instruction-conflict resolution, and a debris-attribution gap.** **(i) PEER-OWNED SURFACES precedence.** Live incident on the origin host: a fixer was caught between two genuinely conflicting instructions — the PEER-OWNED SURFACES lock (do not touch a sibling item's files) and a same-PR doc-sync mandate that would otherwise require touching one of those same files — and picked the mandate, crossing the lock.
