The PEER-OWNED SURFACES prompt now states explicitly that the lock takes PRECEDENCE over any other rule that would otherwise require touching a locked file in the same change; the correct response is to name the gap in the result note, not cross the lock. **(ii) fixer.md scratch-file discipline.** `agents/runner.md` already had a DEBRIS-GUARD instruction (never drop a build/test transcript inside the worktree — redirect to the ARTIFACTS DIR); `agents/fixer.md` had no equivalent, and on the origin host multiple independent reviewers attributed real worktree debris to the fixer's own self-verification, triggering a deterministic FAILED override. `fixer.md` item 8 gains the same discipline. Both ported verbatim. Verification: source-text pins on both the factory.js prompt text and the fixer.md addition. |
| KI-E158 | **Ported from a host-mount session, adapted — cross-session prior-attempt reuse (KI-E69) treated a killed-mid-flight run and a REJECTED-by-real-review attempt identically, and never fenced test/fix reuse against a later replan.** **(i) A rejected attempt is not a crash to recover from.** Live incident on the origin host: a test rejected by real review (3 of N new tests judged vacuous) was reused verbatim on the very next attempt because KI-E69's reuse never distinguished "died mid-flight" from "review already ruled on this" — the rejected test was never rewritten, and a later independent review pass rediscovered and widened the same finding, burning a full gate battery twice for a defect nobody got a chance to actually fix. Fix: `loadPriorAttempt` gains an `isReFix` parameter (the caller's `['FAILED','CONFLICT'].includes(state)` test, the SAME flag that drives `factory.js`'s `item.reFix`/`reFixNote`) and unconditionally disables ALL reuse when true — the fixer/test-author MUST run fresh, with feedback.md in hand. **(ii) A replan supersedes pre-replan artifacts.** A fresh `plan.md` written after `test.json`/`fix.json` already exist supersedes whatever was authored against the older plan; a test/fix predating the current `plan.md` can pass KI-E69's freshness/well-formedness checks and still describe an approach the plan no longer takes. Fix: a `planMs`/`predatesReplan` mtime fence blocks `test.json`/`fix.json` reuse when either predates the current `plan.md`. **Adapted, not ported verbatim**: this repo's `loadPriorAttempt`/`freshFile` never carried the origin's KI-E474 content-proof (`changed`/`workTreeProves`/`proven` parameter) — that mechanism does not exist here — so the port carries forward only the `isReFix` and `planMs` halves, adapted to this repo's simpler 2-parameter `freshFile(dir, name, sinceMs)` shape; `loadPriorAttempt`'s new signature here is `(itemDir, sinceMs, isReFix)`, not the origin's 4-parameter form. The sole `cmdResume` call site is updated to pass `it.reFix` through (this repo's `cmdGroup` has no `loadPriorAttempt` call site of its own to update, unlike the origin). Verification: direct-import pure-function tests against real temp-dir fixtures (isReFix disables reuse even with fresh, well-formed artifacts; isReFix=false/omitted preserves pre-existing behavior; a test/fix predating a later plan.md is not reused) plus the `cmdResume` wiring pin. |
| KI-E159 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a BLOCKED/ESCALATED item's `decision.md` is generated ONCE, from whatever probe/gate flagged it at that moment, but a LATER pass in the SAME pipeline run can independently REVERSE the finding it was built from, with nothing regenerating `decision.md` or telling the human reading the queue.** Live incident on the origin host: an item's BLOCKED verdict rested on a HIGH finding that its own later review pass had already reversed on disk, steering the human toward ruling on a finding that no longer existed on the record. Fix: new `staleDecisionHint(itemDir)` — pure, disk-only — compares `decision.md`'s mtime against every sibling `review-*.md`/`gate-*.md` file's mtime; any newer file names itself in the hint. Deliberately a SURFACING fix only, never auto-unblocking — a false positive on an auto-unblock would be worse than the staleness itself. Wired into both human-facing queue surfaces: `cmdEscalationsSync`'s per-item line (`queue/decisions.md`) and `cmdDecisionsDigest`'s per-row flag plus a new dedicated "Possibly stale decisions" section (`reports/decisions-digest.md`), mirroring the existing KI-E36 delivered-in-HEAD hint's own two-surface wiring exactly. Ported verbatim. Verification: direct-import pure-function tests (no decision.md; decision.md alone; an older sibling file; a newer sibling file, correctly named in the hint) plus wiring pins on both call sites and the new digest section. |
| KI-E160 | **Ported from a host-mount session, live incident — `cmdResume`'s relaunch line trusted a recorded launcher script PATH, never whether that path's CURRENT on-disk content still corresponds to the ids being relaunched.** The shared, unlabeled default launcher path (`state/run-script.js`/`run-args.json`) can be, and on the origin host was, overwritten by a LATER, completely unrelated `group` call before the original item was ever relaunched — the printed relaunch line looked identical whether it was fresh or stale, and a copied-and-launched stale line silently re-ran WHATEVER items the script currently held instead of the intended one (hours of real cost for zero new work, on the origin host). Fix: before printing each relaunch line, read the script's sibling `run-args.json` (the SAME derivation `--reuse` already uses) and verify every id being relaunched is still actually present in its `items[]`; a mismatch prints a loud STALE LAUNCHER warning naming exactly which ids are missing and the concrete recovery (`resume --reset-stale` then `group --ids ... --label ...`), and annotates the relaunch line itself so it cannot be mistaken for a trustworthy one. Fails OPEN (UNVERIFIED, never silently "clean") on an unreadable `run-args.json` — a warning layer, never a hard block, since resume must never refuse to print anything. Ported verbatim. Verification: source-text pins confirming the check, the run-args derivation, the staleIds computation, the named recovery, and the fail-open posture on an unreadable file. |
| KI-E487 / KI-E488 / KI-E491 / KI-E496 / KI-E497 / KI-E498 | **Confirmed NOT PORTABLE, recorded so a future KI-E470 port carries these forward instead of reintroducing the same gaps.** All six origin-host fixes are stepwise-fixer-specific mechanisms (KI-E470's per-step loop, cross-step continuity, and consolidation pass) with no analog to attach to in this repo, which runs its fixer as a single monolithic call. **KI-E487**: a cheaper `fixerConsolidateSolo` route for a 1-step stepwise plan — no stepwise route table exists here. **KI-E488**: a per-step absolute-worktree-path grounding hint, per-step numbered artifact naming (`fix-step-N.json`), and a phantom-manifest cross-check on a step's negative "did not apply" claim — none of these have a per-step call site to attach to; the phantom-manifest half additionally depends on KI-E471/E141 (confirmed absent: zero references to `PHANTOM_SCHEMA`/`phantom-manifest-probe`/`workTreeProves` anywhere in this repo). KI-E156's plan-exclusion-adherence half of this same origin commit WAS adapted separately (see KI-E156 above) since it attaches cleanly to the single-shot prompt; only the stepwise-specific two-thirds of that commit are recorded here. **KI-E491**: a self-introduced build error being misclassified as a `scopeStop` — the fix distinguishes the two INSIDE the stepwise per-step loop's error handling, which does not exist here. **KI-E496/KI-E497/KI-E498**: this session's own prior work (stepwise partial-progress resume, freehand step-file schema drift, and per-step phantom checks respectively) — all three are, by their own nature, mechanisms that only make sense once a per-step loop exists to have partial progress, freehand step files, or per-step phantom checks in the first place. |
| KI-E161 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a reFix verificationOnly claim got NO instruction to refresh `verify-red-raw.txt`, only a prose "document the full re-verification" mandate — invisible to the KI-E83 RED-proof marker probe, which reads ONLY the marker file, whatever attempt produced it (KI-L37's own documented design).** On a multi-round item, that file is very likely written during an EARLIER, non-verificationOnly round — correctly showing that round's `exit!=0` failure on the THEN-unfixed code — and a later round's verificationOnly claim, if it never re-runs and re-tees the test, leaves that stale failing capture in place, where the probe reads it as current evidence and fails an item whose fix is actually already correct. Live incident on the origin host: a genuinely correct, independently re-confirmed fix still FAILED because `verify-red-raw.txt` was untouched since the ORIGINAL red-authoring round several cycles earlier. **Fix:** `testVoHint`'s reFix branch (previously hard-blanked to `''`) now carries the SAME disk-proof mandate the first-round verificationOnly shapes already have — RUN the pinning/coverage test now, TEE its output + a fresh `FACTORY::RED::<exitcode>` marker (0 expected) to `verify-red-raw.txt`, OVERWRITING whatever earlier-round capture is already there. Ported verbatim. Verification: exec-smoke confirms the existing verification-only reFix lane's test-author prompt now carries the KI-E161 mandate and its explicit overwrite instruction. |
| KI-E489 / KI-E490 | **Ported from a host-mount session — two fixes chained from the same root-cause investigation: the RE-FIX EXCEPTION told a reFix test-author to defer to "the FIRST round's baseline-raw.txt (already on disk)" with no check that any such file existed, and `effectiveBaseline`'s `Math.max(0, 0)` cannot distinguish a REAL zero (a baseline run genuinely found nothing pre-existing) from a PHANTOM zero (nothing was ever captured or reported).** Live incident on the origin host: an item ran multiple reFix rounds with NO `baseline-raw.txt` ever on disk (Docker was reachable when the test was first authored, so capture was correctly skipped then; it stopped being reachable later), and its integrate stage's real, reproducible, environment-unrelated failures — independently confirmed to ALSO fail on a completely clean, unmodified main checkout with zero relation to the item's diff — were folded as "N new suite failures beyond baseline 0" and deterministically FAILED an otherwise fully-APPROVED item repeatedly, until the retry bound exhausted into a human escalation for a question a captured baseline would have answered for free. **KI-E489 fix (upstream, prompt-side):** the RE-FIX EXCEPTION now opens with an existence check — `test -f .../baseline-raw.txt` — and only defers to the existing file when one is actually present; when none exists, the exception does NOT apply and the test-author must capture one now regardless of reFix status. **KI-E490 fix (downstream, defense-in-depth):** since a prompt instruction is not a guarantee, new pure functions `isPhantomBaseline`/`annotateBaselineReason` in `lib/verify.mjs` let `deterministicVerifyOverride` distinguish the two zeros and append an explicit "this 0 is a DEFAULT, not a measurement" caveat to the fail reason whenever a "beyond baseline 0" failure rests on a phantom baseline — a message-quality improvement, not a verdict change; a phantom baseline still fails the item exactly as before, but the human reading the escalation queue sees the caveat immediately instead of needing a from-scratch investigation. Both ported verbatim; this repo had no pre-existing byte-for-byte-duplicate dead-code lines to clean up (unlike the origin's own incidental find). Verification: direct-import pure-function tests for `isPhantomBaseline`/`annotateBaselineReason` (all four baseline shapes; a reason that doesn't even mention "beyond baseline 0" is never touched) plus wiring pins confirming both the verify- and integrate-transcript fail paths route through the annotator. |
| KI-E164 | **Ported from a host-mount session, owner directive ("update the factory to prevent such failures") — editorial (KI-L34) deliberately never self-edits FACTUAL content (a design boundary: a prose pass correcting a fact it cannot independently verify against code is exactly the overreach that separation avoids), but until now that report went nowhere within the SAME attempt: a HIGH-severity, grep-verified factual finding with an exact replacement already written sat as advisory prose while the diff proceeded to the hard gate band UNCHANGED.** Live incident on the origin host: two HIGH `editorial:prose` findings (fabricated event/field names in a planning doc, a false "already covered" claim) shipped completely unapplied all the way to the gate band, which independently rediscovered the identical defects at full opus-gate-band price. **Fix:** after the editorial loop, collect every HIGH-severity finding across all editorial-band flows; if any exist (and the item isn't verificationOnly, mirroring edge-scan's own guard), dispatch ONE bounded fixer amend carrying the exact findings, then re-scan only the specific flow(s) that found something so the recorded verdict reflects the corrected diff — the exact "one bounded amend" idiom edge-scan/acceptance-scan/plan-commitment-scan already have, preserving editorial's discovery/application separation of roles (the FIXER applies, never editorial itself). Scoped to HIGH only — MEDIUM/LOW stays purely advisory so a style opinion never forces an amend on every doc-touching item. Ported verbatim. Verification: real exec-smoke behavioral coverage — a HIGH finding triggers exactly one amend + a targeted re-scan with the verdict flipping to APPROVED and the item still CLOSED (editorial never itself blocks); a MEDIUM-only finding never triggers anything. |
| KI-E166 | **Ported from a host-mount session — `feedback.md` is regenerated on every fold by design, meaning a finding raised by an EARLIER cycle is silently LOST the moment a LATER cycle's own, different rejection reason overwrites the file, even though the earlier finding was never actually fixed.** Live incident on the origin host: a later cycle's `gate:qa` named the EXACT gap an earlier `gate:qa` pass on the SAME item had already flagged, never fixed because the intervening round only addressed a different, unrelated finding — that intervening round's own regenerated `feedback.md` carried no trace of the earlier finding at all, so a reFix round faithfully reading `feedback.md` (exactly as its own briefing instructs) had zero visibility into it. **Fix:** new `mergeFeedbackHistory(fresh, prior)` in `lib/feedback.mjs` — a pure function keeping the current cycle's content first/authoritative but appending the prior file's content below a clearly-labeled "PRIOR CYCLE(S)' FEEDBACK" re-verify header instead of discarding it, capped at 6000 chars (re-truncated every cycle before re-appending, mirroring KI-E474's `PLAN_REUSE_CAP` posture) so a chronically-failing item's file converges to a bounded size instead of growing forever. `driver.mjs`'s fold now reads the existing `feedback.md` before overwriting it and routes both through the merge. Ported verbatim. Verification: direct-import pure-function tests (no/empty/whitespace-only prior -> byte-identical to fresh; a real prior finding survives the merge under the labeled header; an oversized prior file is capped) plus the `driver.mjs` wiring pin. This is also the prerequisite KI-E168 (below) builds its "skip already-covered older content" step on. |
| KI-E167 | **Ported from a host-mount session — the KI-E105 stall detector (the circuit-breaker meant to cut off a non-converging item before it burns its full retry budget) only ever reads `result.gateDetails`, which is EMPTY for roughly half of all real exhaustion causes: a build failure, a missing RED-proof/realInfra marker, worktree debris, main-tree contamination, a refuted fix, a non-converged re-audit lens, a failed integrate.** Live audit on the origin host: of the items that burned their entire retry budget before landing ESCALATED/BLOCKED, over half exhausted on exactly one of these invisible failure classes, with `stallRounds` frozen at 0 the whole time — one item's final two attempts failed with a byte-identical realInfra-marker note and the detector never noticed, burning a third full band (including the opus gate panel) before the flat retry bound finally stopped it. **Fix:** new `failSignature(note)` in `lib/convergence.mjs` — a coarse, deterministic fingerprint of a FAILED result's `note` (cut at the first ": " or " — " separator, or a 60-char cap), identifying WHICH CHECK failed without needing `gateDetails` at all. Wired into `applyStallDetection` as a fallback used ONLY when `gateFindingsSummary` returns null (the richer, gate-band comparison always wins when it has data — the fallback never second-guesses it); two consecutive rounds with an identical signature feed the SAME `stallRounds` counter and `maxStallRounds` bound as the gate-band path, reusing the existing config knob. `driver.mjs`'s escalation note and the fold-time stall log line both now branch on the persisted `row.stallReason`/`s.reason` so the reported evidence always matches what actually fired, instead of always phrasing the note as if gate-band findings existed. Ported verbatim. Verification: direct-import pure-function tests for `failSignature` (both separator shapes, the 60-char fallback, null/empty) plus behavioral tests of the fallback path itself (a first pre-band failure sets no stall; two consecutive identical signatures accumulate the SAME counter the findings path uses and get reported at the bound; a DIFFERENT signature the next round is not a stall; gate-band data always wins over a coincidentally-matching stale signature) plus the two `driver.mjs` wiring pins. |
| KI-E168 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a reFix round is TOLD in prose (`reFixNote`/`staleGuard`) to read `feedback.md` and address every finding that rejected the prior attempt, but nothing MACHINE-VERIFIES it actually did, on a THIRD axis distinct from acceptance-scan (KI-E18: the item's own acceptance criteria) and plan-commitment-scan (KI-E87/E101: the planner's OWN stated intent) — the SPECIFIC review findings that caused the LAST rejection.** Live incident on the origin host: three separate reFix items each burned a FULL, expensive gate band on a reFix round before failing again on essentially the same class of finding the prior round had already surfaced — the most expensive possible point to learn "you didn't fix what broke last time". **Fix:** new pre-band `prior-finding-probe`, gated on `item.reFix` only, positioned right before the editorial pass (this repo has no KI-E486 phantom-manifest probe to sit after, unlike the origin — the relative placement before editorial is preserved instead): reads `feedback.md` (skipping any "PRIOR CYCLE(S)' FEEDBACK" section already covered by an earlier round's own pass, per KI-E166 above — this repo needed KI-E166 ported FIRST for this divider to exist, which it now is), extracts every bulleted finding, and judges against the freshly-regenerated `review-pack.md` whether each has concrete diff evidence. Mirrors acceptance-scan/plan-commitment-scan's exact probe -> ONE bounded amend -> re-probe -> pre-band-fail-or-continue shape, and — per KI-E481's lesson for plan-commitment-scan — ALSO runs for verificationOnly reFix rounds, with a KI-E481-style prompt variant grading the standing diff rather than new work. Deliberately scoped to reFix items only. Ported verbatim. **`opencode/` binding:** recorded `UNPORTED` in `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` (this port's `planNext`/`applyPhaseResults` have no notion of reading back the driver-written `feedback.md` digest at all) — purely economic gap. Verification: real exec-smoke behavioral coverage — a gap on the first probe call resolved by the bounded amend proceeds to CLOSED with the actual finding text reaching the fixer; a gap that SURVIVES the amend FAILS pre-band with the finding text in the operator-visible note and exactly one amend (no retry loop); a non-reFix item never dispatches the probe at all; the verification-only reFix lane dispatches the KI-E481-style variant and still records APPROVED. |
| KI-E492 / KI-E494 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a model-fit question ("can haiku manage a proposed consolidation of acceptance-scan + plan-commitment-scan + prior-finding-scan into one call?") deserved empirical evidence before anything shipped on the strength of it.** On the origin host, 9 haiku-4-5 worker calls against realistic scenarios with known ground truth, graded by 3 independently-launched opus evaluators, found zero measured degradation and no cross-axis anchoring from merging the three probes into one call — but explicitly n=1, with two structural risks named regardless of sample size: atomic failure (one call is a single point of failure for N verdicts) and an untested "legitimate true mixed with subtle false" direction. **KI-E492 fix:** rather than ship the merge cold or shelve it, a HOST-POLICY-GATED (`policies.shadowConsolidatedScan`, off by default), purely observational shadow probe — `consolidated-scan-shadow`, new `SHADOW_SCAN_SCHEMA` with three INDEPENDENTLY-required booleans so a truncated response fails schema validation rather than silently reading as three passes. Fires ONE additional non-blocking haiku call, ONLY when acceptance-scan/plan-commitment-scan/prior-finding-scan all already ran and returned APPROVED this round, and records AGREE/DISAGREE against them in `gateDetails['probe:consolidated-scan-shadow']` WITHOUT EVER touching `res.toState` — the three real scans remain the unmodified, sole source of truth; an unavailable probe is recorded `SKIPPED`, never misread as a disagreement (KI-L53 fail-open posture). **KI-E494 fix (self-caught dependency, ported alongside per the research fork's explicit recommendation):** `lib/policy.mjs`'s `loadPolicies()` has its OWN independent `DEFAULTS` allowlist, and its merge only ever copies config keys already present in it — `shadowConsolidatedScan` would otherwise be set in `config/factory.config.json` and still be silently dropped on every real `group`/`sweep` launch, exactly reproducing the bug KI-E494 fixed on the origin host (where the flag shipped inert for a full day because exec-smoke's direct `batch.policies` injection bypasses this loader entirely and structurally could not catch the gap). Both ported verbatim; `DEFAULTS` gains `shadowConsolidatedScan: false`, and the shipped `config/factory.config.json` documents the new key at its default (OFF — a public engine must not ship an experimental data-collection probe on by default, unlike the origin host's own local-overlay opt-in). **`opencode/` binding:** `SHADOW_SCAN_SCHEMA` mirrored into `opencode/schemas.mjs` for schema-parity; the stage itself recorded `UNPORTED` in `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` (pure data-collection infrastructure with no correctness effect on any item — its absence changes nothing about what this port accepts or rejects). Verification: real exec-smoke behavioral coverage — off by default (never dispatches even when every real gating condition is met); on + agreement (recorded AGREE, item still closes); on + a FORCED disagreement (recorded DISAGREE naming the diverging axis, item STILL closes normally — the critical safety property); the policy on but prior-finding-scan never ran on a non-reFix item (never treats a missing real axis as satisfied); an unavailable probe recorded SKIPPED — plus the KI-E494 round-trip assertion (write a local-overlay config, call the real `loadPolicies()`, confirm the flag actually comes back true, the exact integration gap the origin bug slipped through). |

| KI-E137 | **Ported from a host-mount session — INCREMENTAL PROGRESS CHECKPOINTING.** `checkpointResult` (KI-L40) persists only the TERMINAL outcome, chained via `.then()` strictly AFTER `runItem()` fully resolves, so a Workflow killed anywhere in the pipeline between `fix` and `integrate` (10-25+ agent calls: the whole pre-band scan chain, then the entire opus gate band, then refute+re-audit) left `reconstruct` with NOTHING — discarding every already-paid-for stage no matter how close to done it was. **Fix:** `factory.js` gains `checkpointProgress(res, stage, extra)`, a sibling of `checkpointResult` (same KI-D8 provenance framing, same `CHECKPOINT_SCHEMA` write contract, a SEPARATE `state/items/<id>/progress.json` file so the terminal-checkpoint contract `reconstruct`/`fold` depend on is untouched), called at 4 phase boundaries: `post-verify` (before the pre-band scan chain begins), `post-preband` (after it, before the gate band), `post-gates` (after `GATED`, before refute+re-audit), `post-reaudit` (after `REAUDITED`, before integrate). `driver.mjs`'s `cmdResume` (per-item inflight line) and `cmdReconstruct` (both the zero-results and partial-results missing-item branches) now read it via the new `lib/progress-checkpoint.mjs` (`readProgressCheckpoint` + `summarizeProgress`, resultId-fenced exactly like `result.json`'s KI-B4 fold-idempotency check) and print the reached stage + gate tally + what remains, instead of a bare "no checkpoint — must re-run". Deliberately does NOT (yet) feed relaunch reuse — `progress.json` is advisory/forensic only in this change, same posture `lib/prior-attempt.mjs` already documents for why `verify` onward stays unreused. **Scoped narrower than the origin commit, deliberately:** the origin repo shipped E137 and KI-E139 (content-hash-fenced gate-band reuse) together in one commit, since E139's `post-preband` checkpoint carries a `reviewPackHash` E137 alone has no use for. This port lands E137 ALONE — the `post-preband` call here is the plain 2-arg form, with no hash argument, since there is no `PACK_HASH_SCHEMA`/pack-hash probe here yet to produce one; KI-E139 is a deliberately separate, later port. The `post-preband` checkpoint is also placed one scan earlier than the origin's exact spot, since this repo's own pre-band scan chain currently ends at `ledger-anchor-scan` (KI-E91) — it has no root-cause-touch-probe (KI-E104's PROBE form) yet to checkpoint after; this remains the correct "last thing before the gate band" position for this repo's actual pipeline. `claimTokensSilently` (KI-E150, already present in this repo) is wired into the new `checkpointProgress` call site from the moment it was added — no separate follow-up needed. Verification: real exec-smoke behavioral coverage over this repo's own 6-item `smokeBatch()` fixture — each of the 4 checkpoints fires exactly once per lane, AND, for one representative FULL-band lane, the four checkpoint calls interleave with the real pipeline calls in true RUNTIME order (post-verify < post-preband < gate band < po gate < post-gates < refute/re-audit < post-reaudit < integrator), passing on the FIRST run with no role-name adjustments needed. Plus full pure-function coverage of the read side (absent/fresh/stale-cycle/id-mismatch/malformed/unrecognized-stage-tag, all never-throw). Selftest 1456 → 1482 passed, 0 failed; opencode 189/0 unaffected (this change touches only `factory.js`/`driver.mjs`/a new `lib/` file, not `opencode/*`). |

| KI-E138 | **Initially recorded N/A (see the session's own note below), then actually ported once KI-E143C needed it.** On the origin host, KI-E138 fixed a self-contradiction that KI-E485's own commit introduced: it added an `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` `UNPORTED` entry (`probe:post-amend-verify`, a synthetic gate-KEY label, never a literal `call(...)` role argument) without updating the sibling KI-E112 assertion that hard-pinned the set to `[]`, AND that entry was structurally undetectable by the existing port-parity extractor (`extractFactoryRoles` can only ever find literal `call('roleName', ...)` arguments, never a hand-assigned `res.gates['...']` string). | KI-E139 | **Ported from a host-mount session — GATE-BAND REUSE, content-hash-fenced.** Building on KI-E137's checkpoints: on a relaunch, if a PRIOR attempt's `progress.json` proves the gate band already reached a fully-resolved `GATED` state (`post-gates` or `post-reaudit`) AND a FRESH hash of the JUST-regenerated `review-pack.md` matches the hash recorded at that prior `post-preband` checkpoint, the reviewable diff is provably byte-identical to what a full gate band already vetted — the recorded verdicts are adopted (`res.gateBandReused = true`, a visible telemetry flag) and the expensive opus gate band is skipped entirely. On ANY mismatch (a genuinely changed diff, a stale/corrupt checkpoint, or no eligible checkpoint at all) it falls through to a full, independent re-gate — byte-identical to pre-KI-E139 behaviour. The hash is never trusted from the prior attempt's own say-so; it is always re-derived, right now, against the live worktree via a new `pack-hash-probe` role (haiku, `PACK_HASH_SCHEMA`) — this is what lets the mechanism NOT weaken the "gates re-adjudicate the worktree on every relaunch" trust boundary: a skip only ever happens on mechanical proof, never an assumption. `factory.js`'s ENTIRE gate-band dispatch (role gates, method-flow reviews, dissent adjudication, PO gate) is wrapped in `if (!gatesReused) { ... }`; `methodFlows` is hoisted OUT of that block since the later Refute+Re-audit section also reads it, so it must exist regardless of which path ran. Wired into `cmdResume --reuse`, which now ALSO attaches a `progress.json` to `it.priorProgress` (id+cycle-fenced via `readProgressCheckpoint`, same convention as `result.json`'s KI-B4 fold-idempotency), alongside the pre-existing KI-E69 plan/test/fix attach. **Confirmed adaptation:** the origin repo ALSO wires this into a `cmdGroup`-based `KI-E474 CONTINUING` automatic re-claim-and-relaunch mechanism — that mechanism does not exist in this repo at all (this repo's `cmdGroup` only prints relaunch candidates for the operator to manually copy a command; there is no automatic re-claim to extend). `cmdResume --reuse` is the ONLY relaunch-reuse entry point this repo has, and it now carries the complete, correct KI-E139 wiring. New `pack-hash-probe` role declared `UNPORTED` in `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` (genuinely absent — this runtime's phase-driven state machine has no "prior attempt" concept to fast-forward from at all) with `PACK_HASH_SCHEMA` added to `opencode/schemas.mjs` for schema-parity; the KI-E112 hard-pin assertion is repinned from `eq(..., [])` to `eq(..., ['pack-hash-probe'])` in the SAME commit, per that assertion's own documented discipline (see KI-E138 above for why the fuller E138 mechanism wasn't needed for this repin). Verification: source-text pins (schema exists, reuse-eligibility condition, hash-comparison condition) plus 4 real exec-smoke behavioral scenarios proving ALL directions — a HIT (matching hash + eligible stage: zero gate-role calls dispatched, `gateBandReused===true`, the reused verdict lands on the final result, the item still reaches `CLOSED`), a hash MISMATCH (falls through to a full re-gate), a stage-too-early miss (`post-preband` only — falls through), and the pre-existing no-`priorProgress` baseline (already proven by KI-E137's own coverage, cited rather than re-proven). Selftest 1483 → 1496 passed, 0 failed (13 new assertions, matching the shape of the origin's own coverage); opencode 189 → 191 (schema-parity + UNPORTED-declaration checks). |

| KI-E140 | **Ported from a host-mount session — TASK-LIVENESS REMINDER.** A process mitigation for the still-unproven root-cause hypothesis behind main-tree contamination on the origin host: a stray/duplicate `runItem()` for the same id, from an incompletely-terminated PRIOR attempt whose task the controller never confirmed dead before relaunching into the SAME worktree path. The origin host verified live that a Workflow task does NOT appear to survive the session that spawned it — reassuring for the cross-session case, but the WITHIN-session case (a relaunch issued before a just-killed attempt's task is confirmed torn down) remains exactly the window that class of incident could not rule out, and the driver cannot check task liveness itself — only the controller session holds a `TaskOutput`/`TaskStop` handle. **Fix: a recording + surfacing mechanism, not a detector.** New `driver.mjs mark-launched --ids a,b,c --taskId <id> --runId <id>` writes `state/items/<id>/launch-meta.json` (atomic write, per-id skip-not-throw for a non-claimed id) — the controller runs this immediately after every real `Workflow` launch. `cmdResume`'s relaunch-candidate listing reads it back (mirroring KI-E41's MAIN-GUARD loop's exact shape and posture: warn loudly, per-id try/catch, never block) and prints the exact command to run (`TaskOutput <taskId> block:false`) before the relaunch line is copied. Best-effort by design: a missing `launch-meta.json` is silent, never a warning. **A real, live bug this port ALSO carried, caught and fixed in the SAME commit**: `launch-meta.json` (and, in the same pass, this repo's OWN `progress.json` from KI-E137 — a gap that had slipped through that earlier port uncaught) would otherwise be flagged by the pre-existing KI-E42 "non-canonical artifact" quarantine detector (`lib/telemetry.mjs`'s `CONTROL_ARTIFACTS` allowlist), meaning a real `resume --quarantine` run would sweep away the ONE file each mechanism exists to surface. Fixed by adding both filenames to `CONTROL_ARTIFACTS`. **Confirmed adaptation:** the origin host's reminder message also cites its own KI-E485 as "the leading, still-unproven, root-cause hypothesis" for the worktree-drift class this reminder guards against; that citation is dropped here since KI-E485 doesn't exist in this repo — the mechanism and its reasoning are otherwise unchanged. Verification: source-text coverage (matching this file's established convention for `driver.mjs`: it has no exports and a top-level `main()`, so no test spawns it as a subprocess) — `cmdMarkLaunched`'s no-op/skip/atomic-write/timestamp behavior, and the reader's silent-on-missing/exact-check-named/never-blocks posture, both pinned directly against the real source. Selftest 1496 → 1507 passed, 0 failed; opencode 191/0 unaffected (canon-only, no opencode relaunch-reuse concept to wire into). |

| KI-E142 | **Ported from a host-mount session — TWO changes shipped together: moving the plan-commitment failure point EARLIER (KI-E142A, before test-author/fixer even run) and making the EXISTING failure point less binary (KI-E142B, so a deliberate, justified deviation from the plan is no longer indistinguishable from a genuine gap).** Origin-host owner framing: "it is very expensive to fail the work in the very end if the plan was bad... deviations from the plan should be explained and evaluated as some deviations are required and not a signal of the failure." **KI-E142A — PLAN-REVIEW, a new pre-implementation stage.** Inserted right after the planner call (and KI-E134's existing step-decomposition nudge) but BEFORE test-author ever runs, gated `if (plan && freshPlanCall)` (never for a KI-E69-reused plan). Two lenses: a MECHANICAL `plan-feasibility-probe` (haiku; does every file the plan names actually exist, or is it declared new; is every step concrete enough to implement and verify) and an LLM `plan-quality-probe` (routed at the planner's OWN model tier; does the plan cover every acceptance criterion and the edge-case categories that plainly apply). Any gap drives ONE bounded planner revision. The two lenses are NOT symmetric: a feasibility gap that survives the revision is an OBJECTIVELY broken premise and hard-FAILS the item pre-band — the cheapest possible fail; a quality/edge-case gap NEVER hard-fails on its own, it only steers the one revision and rides along as `probe:plan-review` gate telemetry. **KI-E142B — plan-deviation adjudication, extending `plan-commitment-scan`'s existing failure path.** `FIX_SCHEMA` gains `deviations: {commitment, reason}[]` — distinct from the pre-existing `divergence` (target-codebase standards-evolution.md conventions) — `deviations` is about knowingly not honoring one of THIS ITEM'S OWN plan commitments. When the bounded-amend-and-reprobe cycle still finds a commitment unhonored, the fold now first checks whether the fixer's last response declared a `deviations` entry with a real `reason`. A SILENT gap (nothing declared) fails exactly as before. A DECLARED, explained gap is routed to the `adjudicator` role (the same role that already breaks gate-review dissent ties) rather than re-asked of the same cheap probe that just found the gap. `adjudicator.md`'s brief gained a second documented invocation scenario, sharing the same UPHELD/OVERRULED schema and fail-safe "default to UPHELD when genuinely uncertain" posture; OVERRULED requires EVERY remaining gap to be legitimately explained. **Verified prerequisites before porting, not assumed:** `PLAN_COMMITMENT_SCHEMA`, `PLAN_SCHEMA`, `ADJUDICATE_SCHEMA`, `R.adjudicator`, the existing `adjudicator` gate-dissent call site, and the plan-commitment-scan block's exact structure were all confirmed present and byte-identical to the origin's pre-change state before applying either hunk. **New `agents/adjudicator.md` second scenario and `agents/fixer.md` `deviations` documentation transplanted verbatim** from the origin's real brief text, not recomposed. `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` gains `plan-feasibility-probe`/`plan-quality-probe` as UNPORTED (a materially larger, separately-scoped addition — a new stage with its own bounded-revision cycle — deferred, unlike E142B which extends the already-present plan-commitment-scan and IS fully ported here); `opencode/schemas.mjs`'s `FIX_SCHEMA` gains `deviations` for schema-parity (structurally inert there since the adjudication routing isn't ported). Two PRE-EXISTING selftest pins (KI-E87's and KI-E101's own "exactly one `finish('FAILED')` call site" invariants) needed updating to "exactly two" in the SAME commit, matching the origin's own updates — a fix that only ported the new behavior without repinning these would have left them silently wrong (or, if run first, would have caught the gap immediately). Verification: 4 real exec-smoke behavioral scenarios (a feasibility gap that survives revision hard-fails before test-author/fixer/verify ever run; a quality-only gap never hard-fails and still reaches APPROVED; a declared deviation the adjudicator OVERRULES proceeds with the ORIGINAL gate key flipping to APPROVED; a declared deviation the adjudicator UPHOLDS still fails with a note distinguishing it from silence) — all passed on the FIRST run. Selftest 1509 → 1532 passed, 0 failed; opencode 191/0 (schema-parity + 2 new UNPORTED declarations only). |

| KI-E143 | **Ported from a host-mount session — THREE changes, each from investigating a real failure batch on the origin host; a fourth (KI-E143D) confirmed correctly N/A and skipped.** **KI-E143A — plan-feasibility-probe now verifies concrete CODE-LEVEL claims, not just file existence.** Live on the origin host: a diff was correct, but the plan's own step asserted a specific, falsifiable fact ("delete the redundant instance constructor... added at lines 83-90") that provably did NOT exist in the worktree — a planner hallucination KI-E142A's original feasibility lens never caught because it only checked that named FILES exist, never that a specific claimed CODE PATTERN inside an existing file is actually true. Fix: the `plan-feasibility-probe` prompt now explicitly instructs grep/reading the relevant file to verify any step asserting a specific existing-code fact, not just resolving the file path. **KI-E143B — the plan-quality-probe lens gains a cross-service blast-radius check.** Live, same origin batch: one plan added a required constructor param to a shared library that broke a downstream consumer's tests; another added an overflow guard to one translator but missed a documented field-for-field mirror of it in a sibling service — both plans scoped to one service, never checking whether anything touched has a documented consumer or mirror elsewhere. Fix: `plan-quality-probe`'s prompt adds a third judgment dimension — check the target service's `CONTEXT.md` "Service Dependencies" section (and the architecture atlas if present) for a documented consumer/mirror of anything the plan touches, and require the plan to account for it or explicitly scope it out with a reason. **KI-E143C — realInfra-classification adjudication, extending KI-E142B's declare-and-adjudicate pattern to a second axis.** Live, same origin batch: an item pre-flagged `needsRealInfra=true` by the graph (a static classification made before any test exists), but the test-author correctly reasoned the actual defect was pure exception-wrapping logic testable via Moq — and wrote an in-memory test instead of a Testcontainers one. The mechanical marker probe is purely mechanical (greps for a `FACTORY::REALINFRA::` marker) and had NO path for a test-author's reasoned, documented judgment to override a wrong graph classification. Fix: `TEST_SCHEMA` gains `realInfraOverride` (string or null) — a test-author that judges the graph's classification wrong for THIS defect states why; a SILENT gap still fails exactly as before. A declared, explained override routes to the SAME `adjudicator` role used for gate-dissent and plan-deviation rulings — `adjudicator.md` gains a THIRD documented scenario, sharing the same UPHELD/OVERRULED schema and fail-safe default. **KI-E143D (post-amend-verify note wording) confirmed N/A, not silently skipped:** its origin mechanism (KI-E478/KI-E485's post-amend re-verify) doesn't exist in this repo (see the KI-E485 entry above) — there is no `rv.build`/`rv.evidence` self-report to improve the wording of. **Prerequisite verification before porting:** `TEST_SCHEMA`, the realInfra marker-probe block, the `test` variable's scope, `ADJUDICATE_SCHEMA`, and `R.adjudicator` were all confirmed present and byte-identical to the origin's pre-change state before applying E143C's hunk. **This entry is also what triggered actually porting KI-E138's fuller parity mechanism** (see that entry, revised) — `adjudicator:realinfra-override` is a colon-qualified gate key, not a bare role, exactly the shape E138's `extractFactoryGateKeys` exists to handle; `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` declares it UNPORTED (this port's `afterVerify` equivalent is a synchronous chain of deterministic guards, not an async phase — routing to an agent call from inside it needs a materially larger restructuring than plan-commitment-scan's, which was already phase-driven) and the KI-E112 pin is updated in the same commit. `opencode/schemas.mjs`'s `TEST_SCHEMA` gains `realInfraOverride` for schema-parity only. Verification: 2 real exec-smoke scenarios (a declared override the adjudicator OVERRULES proceeds, with the gate key and gateDetails headline durably recording it even though `res.note` gets overwritten by the item's eventual normal close; a silent gap with no override still fails with zero adjudicator calls) plus the KI-E138 pure-function coverage. Selftest 1532 → 1548 passed, 0 failed; opencode 191/0 (schema-parity + 1 new UNPORTED declaration only). |

| KI-E145 | **Ported from a host-mount session — main-drift detection becomes an INDEPENDENT PROBE, closing the last self-report gap KI-E144B left open. Because only the one probe is being ported, the origin commit's own two self-inflicted bugs (a missing `POSTAMEND_SCHEMA` exec-smoke stub branch cascading 36 unrelated failures, and a naming collision between `main-drift-probe`/`postamend-probe`/the pre-existing `marker-probe`) have no equivalent surface here — there is no `POSTAMEND_SCHEMA` in this repo, and `main-drift-probe` was given its own distinct name from the start; the full selftest run stayed green throughout with no such regression to chase. **`opencode/stage-parity.mjs` gains `main-drift-probe` as UNPORTED** — a materially larger gap than most entries in that manifest, since this port's `afterVerify`/`planNext` never call `driver.mjs main-check` AT ALL (confirmed: zero matches for main-check/mainCheck in `runtime.mjs`) — the port has no KI-E50 mid-band dispatch, no teed-file convention, and no probe; all three would need adding, not just the probe. This gap predates KI-E103's parity gate and was invisible to it until now, surfaced only because the gate's own extractor finally sees a role literal for it. The KI-E112 hard-pin is updated in the same commit; the KI-E50 pin's regex is loosened from an exact `(KI-E50)` closing-paren match to a `(KI-E50` prefix match, tolerating the label's growth to `(KI-E50/KI-E145)` — the identical adjustment the origin host's own diff independently made too. **Verification:** the existing 3-scenario exec-smoke block (innocent/guilty/negation) is re-shaped around the probe's mocked `{markerFound, line}` response instead of the runner's self-reported field, and gains a genuinely NEW assertion the old mechanism could never express — the negation scenario is now paired with `markerFound: true` and asserted to FAIL, proving the probe is authoritative over the runner's own prose in BOTH directions (not just immune to a false negative, but not fooled by a false negation claim either). | KI-E180 | **Ported from a host-mount session (origin commit `24947b360`) — CROSS-SERVICE VERIFY-SCOPE GAP. Origin owner question, verbatim: "can I improve the factory in a meaningfull way" — asked after a re-fix round failed the full gate band on a finding that 5 of 8 reviewers had missed despite a clean deterministic verify hint.** Root cause: `agents/runner.md`'s independent verify stage names exactly ONE solution per item (`sln = item.solution \|\| CFG.solution \|\| ...`) — a deliberate design choice, since the runner is kept blind to `fix.filesChanged` on purpose (KI-L56's tiny context-budget ceiling). On the origin host, an item's touch-set already spanned FIVE real services from the moment it entered the graph, but `verify.json` built+tested only ONE of them, and the resulting `verifyEvidenceHint` (KI-E152, already ported here) truthfully told every reviewer "build=pass, targetedTest=pass, suite={failed:0}" — correct, but silently scoped to 1 of 5 touched services; most reviewers trusted the hint at face value and never independently re-ran the other services' own suites. Same theme as KI-E152 one layer deeper: an unmissable-but-INCOMPLETE deterministic result is MORE misleading than none, because the reviewer has been told to trust it. **Fix (ported near-verbatim — the check itself is unchanged from the origin):** a new zero-cost, zero-agent-call mechanical check positioned immediately after the KI-E152 `verifyEvidenceHint` is built and before the pre-band chain (`checkpointProgress`) begins. Derives the SET of distinct top-level service directories the fix actually touched with a real `.cs` change from `res.filesChanged`, denylists generic tooling roots (`src`/`doc(s)`/`k8s`/`scripts`/`tests`/`tools`/`deploy`/`templates`/`config`/`_bmad-output`/`.github`, so a shared-library path like `src/Shared/SomeLib.Authorization/X.cs` never fabricates a false "service" named `src`), then cross-checks each against the runner's own `evidence` string for a build/suite mention. Gated on `svcDirs.size > 1`, not on `uncovered.length` alone — an ordinary single-service item whose one service happens not to be quoted verbatim in evidence prose is NOT this failure class, and firing on it would be a false positive on the overwhelmingly common case. On a genuine gap: fails the item pre-band, naming exactly which service(s) were touched-but-unverified, forcing a re-verify before the incomplete-but-confident evidence ever reaches a reviewer. Deliberately does NOT retrofit a second `FACTORY::SUMMARY::suite`/`FACTORY::BUILD::RESULT` marker into the same `verify-raw.txt` — this repo's own `lib/verify.mjs` `parseVerifyRaw`/`lastMatch` (KI-D3) parses both markers LAST-MATCH-WINS (the same semantics as the origin's `deterministicVerifyOverride`, confirmed by reading `lastMatch`'s own implementation rather than assumed from the name), so a second solution's suite teed into the same transcript would silently make the fold's own machine-authoritative check see ONLY the last service's numbers — a strictly WORSE bug than the one this closes. **Confirmed adaptation — the only structural gap between the two repos:** the origin populates `res.filesChanged` for KI-E486's phantom-manifest probe (accumulated across a STEPWISE fixer's per-step calls + its consolidation pass); both KI-E486 and KI-E470 (stepwise fixer) are confirmed absent in this repo (see the KI-E168 and KI-E487/155/165/173/174/178 entries above — this repo's fixer is a single monolithic call, `let fix = ... await call('fixer', ...)`, block-scoped inside `if (!verificationOnly) { ... }`), so `res.filesChanged` did not exist here at all before this port. Added a small, purely-additive capture — `if (Array.isArray(fix.filesChanged)) res.filesChanged = fix.filesChanged` — right after the existing `fix.applied`/`fix.scopeStop` guards, inside that same block, so the value survives past the block's closing brace for the KI-E180 check (which runs later, after the runner/verify call) to read. Nothing else in this repo reads `res.filesChanged`, so this is behaviorally inert everywhere except the new check itself. The check's own logic, denylist, and failure message are otherwise byte-for-byte the origin's. **`opencode/` binding:** none needed — this is a pure mechanical check with no new agent role/probe dispatched, so there is nothing for `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` to declare `UNPORTED`. **Verification:** real exec-smoke behavioral coverage, not source-text pins alone — adapted from the origin's own 4 scenarios (which intercepted a STEPWISE CONSOLIDATION step, KI-E470, to inject the second service's file) to this repo's single-shot fixer by overriding the one `SMOKE-CODE:fixer` call directly: (a) a fix genuinely touching two real services (`X` + `Y`) with verify evidence naming only `X` fails pre-band before the gate band ever dispatches, with the failure note naming both the probe and the specific uncovered service; (b) the identical two-service touch with evidence naming BOTH proceeds to a normal `CLOSED`; (c) a second touched path under a denylisted generic root (`src/Shared/...`) never fabricates a false second service, regardless of evidence; (d) the plain, unmodified default smoke fixture (single service `X`, non-literal stub evidence) is provably unaffected — the exact regression the `svcDirs.size > 1` gating exists to prevent. Selftest 1700 → 1706 passed, 0 failed (6 new exec-smoke assertions), re-run twice clean; opencode 195/0 unaffected (no new role/probe — nothing to register). |

| KI-E181 | **Ported from a host-mount session (origin commit `b287f6539`) — PARTIAL PORT: the origin's PREVENTION half applies here and is ported; its CURE half has no host and is confirmed NOT PORTABLE, for the same underlying reason as KI-E498 itself. Origin owner directive, verbatim: "i need you to think how to improve the developer not to false report and actualy fix as exected" — after a prior item's cycle-109 run FAILED pre-band when a stepwise fixer step over-claimed an already-correct file alongside its one genuine edit.** On the origin host the fix shipped in two parts: (1) **prevention** — `agents/fixer.md` gains a self-check explicitly defining `filesChanged` for the first time (only files edited THIS call, never one merely read/verified), mirrored as a `description` field on `FIX_SCHEMA.filesChanged` (kept byte-identical between `factory.js` and `opencode/schemas.mjs`); (2) **cure** — the KI-E498 per-step phantom-claim retry prompt is rewritten from one explanation into a 3-branch diagnosis (tool-persistence failure / already-correct file, drop it / unsure, compare) so the ONE bounded retry KI-E498 already grants has a real path to succeed by narrowing the claim, not just by re-persisting an edit. **Verified fresh before porting, not assumed stale:** `grep -rl "KI-E498\b"` across `_workflow`/`agents`/`verify`/`KNOWN-ISSUES.md` finds only the PRE-EXISTING KI-E487/155/165/173/174/178 entry (above) — confirmed, live, that this repo has ZERO per-step phantom-manifest mechanism (no `PHANTOM_SCHEMA`, no `phantom-manifest-probe`, no `PHANTOM-CLAIM RETRY` text anywhere in `factory.js`) because its fixer is a single monolithic call, not the origin's KI-E470 stepwise per-step loop that KI-E498's retry text lives inside. **The cure half is therefore not portable here for the identical reason KI-E498 itself is not** — there is no per-step retry prompt to rewrite into 3 branches, and inventing one would be porting an undeclared prerequisite (KI-E470/KI-E498), not this KI. **The prevention half DOES apply, and is not merely inert-for-parity here — it has a genuine, live consumer:** KI-E180 (immediately above, ported the same porting session) added `res.filesChanged` capture from this repo's own single-shot `fix.filesChanged` specifically to feed its cross-service verify-scope check's `svcDirs` derivation. An inaccurate `filesChanged` claim from the fixer misfires THAT check in either direction here — over-claiming a merely-consulted file can fabricate a false extra service and fail an otherwise-correct item; under-claiming a genuinely-touched file can hide a real touched service from the very check KI-E180 just added. **Ported:** `agents/fixer.md` gains item 13, `filesChanged` MEANS "I EDITED THIS" — NOTHING ELSE, citing a prior item's incident and explicitly naming the KI-E180 dependency as why this matters in THIS repo, not just the origin's; `FIX_SCHEMA.filesChanged` gains a matching `description` field in both `factory.js` and `opencode/schemas.mjs` (deep-equal, per the existing Fix #20 schema-parity gate in `opencode/_selftest.mjs`) referencing the same KI-E180 dependency. **`opencode/` binding:** none needed beyond the schema-parity mirror — KI-E180's own check is itself unported into the opencode runtime's `afterVerify`/`planNext` (confirmed: zero `svcDirs`/`KI-E180` matches under `_workflow/opencode/` before this change), so the description field is present for parity but structurally inert there today, the same posture `deviations` already documents for KI-E142B. **Verification:** source-text pins only, matching this file's own established convention for a documentation/schema-only change with no new branching logic to exercise behaviorally (compare KI-O2, KI-E94/95/96, KI-E156's fixer.md-side pins) — confirms `agents/fixer.md` item 13 exists and names KI-E181/KI-E180, confirms `FIX_SCHEMA.filesChanged.description` is present and identical (string equality) in both `factory.js` and `opencode/schemas.mjs`, and confirms the pre-existing Fix #20 schema-parity `deepEqual` assertion in `opencode/_selftest.mjs` (unchanged) is what actually enforces that identity going forward — a divergence between the two copies fails that gate, not a new assertion written for this entry alone. That prior item itself is the ORIGIN host's item and was never run here; this port addresses only the general `filesChanged` self-check the incident exposed, applied to this repo's own actual `res.filesChanged` consumer (KI-E180), not a replay of the origin's specific stepwise scenario. Selftest 1706 → 1710 passed, 0 failed (4 new source-text assertions: the fixer.md item-13 pin, its KI-E180-dependency pin, the FIX_SCHEMA description pin, and the direct cross-file identity check between factory.js and opencode/schemas.mjs), re-run twice clean; opencode 195/0 unaffected (schema-parity field addition only, covered by the pre-existing Fix #20 deep-equal, no new opencode-side assertion needed). |

MUST be re-derived from the tree... before you finish") for exactly this failure class, written for the AUTOMATED fixer and not self-applied by a human controller during ad-hoc recovery. **Fix, ported near-verbatim (no structural adaptation needed — this repo's `claimsHint`/`claimsVerifyHint` KI-E11 wiring in `factory.js` matches the origin site-for-site):** a new pure module `_workflow/lib/countclaims.mjs`, mirroring `lib/doclint.mjs`'s KI-E11 phantom-path linter exactly but for "N/M passed"-style claims — extracts count claims from ADDED `.md` lines (a line honestly narrating a SUPERSEDED count via "was"/"previously"/"up from"/etc. framing is exempt, the same line-granular precision as doclint's `NOT_YET_EXISTS_RE`, ported directly) and cross-checks each against every real `Passed!/Failed! ... Total: T` dotnet-summary line (or the keyed `FACTORY::SUMMARY::suite ... passed=P ...` marker) actually present in the item's OWN `verify-raw.txt`/`integrate-raw.txt` via `lib/verify.mjs`'s pre-existing `decodeTranscript` — never a guess. Wired identically to KI-E11's own two-layer pattern: a new `_workflow/countclaims-lint.mjs` CLI (`FACTORY::COUNTCLAIMS-MISS::<claim>` / `FACTORY::COUNTCLAIMS::<n>`, exit 1 when count>0), a `build-test.sh countclaims <worktree> <item-dir>` subcommand added immediately after the existing `claims` case, a fixer-facing self-check paragraph inserted into `agents/fixer.md` right after the existing KI-E51 paragraph (between it and KI-E95), and — confirmed by an exact `grep -c` before editing — the SAME 7 fixer/amend dispatch sites in `factory.js` that already carry `+ claimsHint` (the initial fix plus all 6 pre-band amend rounds) now also carry `+ countClaimsHint`, plus a `countClaimsVerifyHint` teed into `verify-raw.txt` for doc-touching items alongside the existing `claimsVerifyHint`. **Explicitly NOT a complete fix, disclosed honestly (same as the origin):** this catches ONLY the stale/invented-NUMBER class — it cannot and does not catch a qualitative mischaracterization of what a test proves, which remains the review band's job. **`opencode/` binding:** none — this is a pure mechanical, zero-agent-call check with no new role/probe/schema to dispatch, so there is nothing for `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` to declare `UNPORTED` (same posture as KI-E180's cross-service verify-scope check). **Verification:** real, imported, behavioral coverage of the pure core (`extractCountClaims`, `extractEvidencePairs`, `findUnevidencedCountClaims`) ported from the origin's own selftest — a plain N/M-passed claim extracts; a stale claim against real evidence is flagged, a matching claim is not, a repeated claim is deduped, and an empty evidence set flags every claim (correct — unverifiable is exactly as suspect as stale). Additionally verified LIVE end-to-end through the wired CLI (not just the pure-core unit tests): a synthetic worktree + `verify-raw.txt` with a real `Passed! ... Total: 2283` summary line correctly flags an unrelated stale `2257/2282 passed` claim (`FACTORY::COUNTCLAIMS::1`, exit 1) while leaving the historically-framed `2258/2283 passed (was 2257/2282...)` line untouched, and reports `FACTORY::COUNTCLAIMS::0` (exit 0) once the stale line is removed — confirming `build-test.sh countclaims` → `countclaims-lint.mjs` → `lib/countclaims.mjs` → `lib/verify.mjs`'s `decodeTranscript` are correctly wired together, not just unit-tested in isolation. | KI-E184 | **Confirmed NOT PORTABLE — same prior incident and origin commit (`057c83827`) as KI-E183, a second, distinct engine gap the same investigation found, affecting whether a "smart relaunch" (the origin's KI-E496 stepwise-resume, `driver.mjs group` — never a bare `resume`) actually fixes the real defect or just cheaply reproduces it.** On the origin host, that incident's real build failure (15 errors, all in a test project) traced to a production interface-signature change (an `ExistsAsync` → `GetByEventIdAsync`+`Update` repository-method rename; an entity's `Create` factory gaining a required `now` param) made in one fix step that was never propagated to 5 pre-existing test files still calling the old shape — a plan/step-scoping gap, not the thrashing itself. | KI-E185 | **Ported from a host-mount session (origin commit `29f91a505`, folded together with two same-session origin follow-ups — `a775ffdd4` and `5993820e8` — as one coherent unit, per the origin's own note that the fixup and registry entry only make sense applied alongside the feature they correct/document) — PRE-BAND EF-MIGRATION PENDING-MODEL-CHANGE PROBE, this repo's FIRST pre-band probe to shell out to a real `dotnet` invocation rather than a text/AST-shape diff.** On the origin host, a second attempt (after KI-E184's fix) got past build+test cleanly but then hit 6-of-8 CHANGES_REQUIRED across the gate/review band, 5 of them independently converging on the SAME finding: a persisted entity's schema changed (Guid id → string + 5 new columns + 1 index) with no EF Core migration and a stale ModelSnapshot — a real (non-in-memory) deployment would crash-loop on this while every mocked/in-memory-provider test stays green, since that provider has no schema to diverge from in the first place. Five independent gate/review roles each spent a full dispatch running `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes` by hand to confirm the SAME thing. **Fix, ported (the probe logic itself is unchanged from the origin — no structural adaptation needed, this repo's pre-band chain already has the identical `codeChange`/`verificationOnly`/`item.files`/`res.gates`/`res.gateDetails`/`finish()` contract every sibling probe here uses):** a new `EFMIGRATION_SCHEMA` + a pre-band dispatch in `factory.js` gated broadly on `item.files` naming a path under a recognizable `{Service}/src/{Service}.{Core,Persistence,Infrastructure}/` layout — deliberately wider than "only Persistence changed", since the origin incident's own entity edit lived under `.Core/Domain/` and never touched a path containing the word "Persistence" at all. A dirty verdict fails the item pre-band with the exact remediation command (`dotnet ef migrations add <Name> --project ../{Service}.Persistence`); a missing/empty/inconclusive result set proceeds, announced `SKIPPED` (never silently read as clean, the KI-E20/E41 posture every sibling probe here already follows). `verify/build-test.sh` gains a matching `efmigration <api-project-dir> <persistence-project-relative-path>` subcommand: `cd`s into the API project directory, runs `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes --project <persistence-path>`, and — since exit 1 is ALSO what this command returns on an unrelated build failure (it builds the project first) — distinguishes a genuine `dirty` verdict (the specific "Changes have been made to the model" message) from an `inconclusive` non-zero exit (fail-open, same posture as every pre-band probe here), emitting `FACTORY::EFMIGRATION::RESULT verdict=<clean\|dirty\|inconclusive> exit=<n>` plus the KI-E19 `FACTORY::SUMMARY::efmigration` evidence-manifest line. `agents/fixer.md` gains a matching self-check (appended to the end of item 5, the DB/schema-changes item, immediately before item 6 begins) telling the fixer to run this itself before finishing, not wait for a reviewer to catch it. **Self-correction folded in from the start (this port never shipped the bug live):** the origin's OWN same-session follow-up (`a775ffdd4`) found that its first version of the dirty-detection grep — `grep -qi 'Changes have been made to the model'` — is an UNANCHORED substring match, and the CLEAN EF Core CLI message ("**No** changes have been made to the model since the last migration.") contains that exact string as a literal substring, so the unpatched probe would have reported `verdict=dirty` unconditionally regardless of actual EF state (caught on the origin host by two independent live gate re-checks, not by the origin's own testing before it first shipped). This port's `build-test.sh` ships the CORRECTED, line-anchored form (`grep -qi '^Changes have been made to the model'`) directly — verified with the same real behavioral regression guard the origin's fixup added: a direct subprocess exec of the actual grep invocation against both literal EF Core CLI message strings verbatim (not just a source-text pin on which grep form is present), confirming the anchored form matches the real dirty message and does NOT false-match the real clean message. **Confirmed adaptation:** the origin labels its insertion point `4a5-ter` (a sub-step of a numbered `4a5`/`4a5-bis` pre-band sequence that exists on the origin host); this repo's own pre-band chain mixes numbered (`4a4. ROOT-CAUSE TOUCH PROBE`, `4a6. EDITORIAL HIGH-FINDING AMEND`) and unnumbered (`PRIOR-FINDING SCAN`, the `Editorial (Band C)` loop) steps in between, with no literal `4a5`/`4a5-bis` for a `-ter` suffix to be a variant of — so this port drops the numbering artifact and uses a plain `EF-MIGRATION PROBE (KI-E185, ported from a host-mount session)` heading instead, positioned identically: immediately after the pre-existing editorial advisory-gate-details loop closes and immediately before the pre-existing `4a6. EDITORIAL HIGH-FINDING AMEND (KI-E164, ...)` block, matching the origin's own relative placement byte-for-byte at the seam that matters (confirmed via `git show 29f91a505`'s own diff context, which shows the origin's new block inserted at exactly that same seam). **`opencode/` binding:** `efmigration-probe` is declared `UNPORTED` in `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` with a substantive reason (mirroring the origin's own `EFMIGRATION_SCHEMA`-kept-for-parity-only treatment) — unlike `rootcause-probe` (this port's sibling MECHANICAL entry, enforced via `afterVerify`'s `nonTestChanged` reading the worktree's own git diff directly), "does the persisted model match the migration history" cannot be answered by reading the worktree diff at all; it requires a REAL `dotnet ef` invocation, and this runtime's `call()`/`planNext`/`applyPhaseResults` have no equivalent real-command-relay capability to dispatch that onto. `EFMIGRATION_SCHEMA` is mirrored byte-identical in `opencode/schemas.mjs` (Fix #20's deep-equal schema-parity gate enforces this going forward) but deliberately excluded from the `SCHEMAS` registry, the same treatment already given to `PACK_HASH_SCHEMA`/`SHADOW_SCAN_SCHEMA` (schema-parity only, nothing in this runtime ever validates against it). The pinned `STAGE_PARITY.unported` key list in `_workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs`'s KI-E112 parity gate is updated in the same change to include `efmigration-probe` — the silent-growth failure mode that pinned list exists to catch. **Verification:** real, behavioral pure-function coverage of the service-extraction regex (multi-service extraction from Infrastructure-layer paths; an entity edit under `.Core/Domain/` triggers the check even with no path containing the word "Persistence" at all — the exact origin-host incident shape; the AOT-profile `{Service}.Adapters/Persistence/...` raw-SQL-runner shape correctly does NOT trigger, since `{Service}.Adapters` ≠ `{Service}.Persistence`; empty and null file lists never throw), source-text pins confirming the `factory.js` schema/dispatch/fail/skip wiring and the `build-test.sh` subcommand/marker/anchored-grep wiring (plus an explicit assertion that the UNANCHORED form does NOT survive anywhere in the file), the real subprocess-exec grep-behavior regression guard described above, source-text pins confirming the `agents/fixer.md` self-check text and its exact cited command, and confirmation that `opencode/stage-parity.mjs` declares the new role with a substantive (≥40-char) reason and that `opencode/schemas.mjs` exports the mirrored schema. Selftest 1737 → 1759 passed, 0 failed (21 new literal assertions + 1 additional dynamic-loop assertion from the pre-existing "every declared stage-parity entry carries a real reason" check now iterating one more `UNPORTED` entry — the full +22 delta is accounted for), re-run twice clean; opencode 195 → 197 passed, 0 failed (+2, both from the pre-existing Fix #20 schema-parity gate now iterating the one new schema name — an exports-check plus a deep-equal check — no new opencode-side assertion written for this entry, and no change to `runtime.mjs`'s actual dispatch behavior, matching the `UNPORTED` declaration honestly). |

| KI-E186 | **Efficiency audit, 2026-09-19 — confirmed OPEN sweep closure/eligibility gaps.** At revision `65fe8db`, exact-source, mocked producer-to-fold checks showed an applied/conforming SWEEP site can close after a missing pattern reviewer, a null verifier, or an explicit failed build: sweep-fold relies on mapped HIGH/CRITICAL findings without requiring completed review/verification. Sweep selection also bypasses normal readiness/dependency/autonomy checks, retains sites whose CLAIMED transition failed, and fold directly assigns CLOSED; an owner-BLOCKED synthetic item could close. No runtime fix in this documentation change. See `FACTORY-EFFICIENCY-REVIEW.md` F01/F02 for evidence and required closure prerequisites. |
| KI-E187 | **Efficiency audit, 2026-09-19 — confirmed OPEN recovery/fold contract gaps.** Gate reuse loses `reviewPackHash` when later progress checkpoints overwrite the hash-bearing post-preband checkpoint; review-pack regeneration also changes its timestamped hash. `missingStageFrom` does not recognize native `[...,REAUDITED,FAILED]` terminal shapes. Reused prose-only plans synthesize `recommendEscalate:false` although fresh planner escalation continues until late sign-off, so killed-run reuse can lose that requirement. An accepted realInfra classification override leaves `needsRealInfra:true`, causing deterministic fold to reject the marker-free proof the override allowed. Reproduced with pure helpers/mocked producer-consumer execution; unresolved here. See `FACTORY-EFFICIENCY-REVIEW.md` F07–F10. |
| KI-O6 | **Efficiency audit, 2026-09-19 — confirmed OPEN binding contracts despite 197/197 adapter selftests passing.** Reachable plan/ledger probes name schemas absent from `SCHEMAS`; positive leftover/ledger markers do not match candidate parsers; single-clause and doc-only routing skip applicable plan/ledger checks; mechanical verify/integrate can apply a permissive no/partial-evidence parser result without requiring subprocess/marker completeness. Additional resume identity, concurrent editorial writer, routing and launch-enrichment gaps are documented in `FACTORY-EFFICIENCY-REVIEW.md`. Current official OpenCode v2 documentation also says v1-style `instructions` are accepted but not loaded; installed-v2 compatibility was not exercised, so this is a conditional version gap, not a reproduced host failure. No implementation fix in this change. |
| KI-E188 | **Efficiency audit, 2026-09-19 — OPEN prompt-delivery/accounting gaps.** Native `readRoleBriefs` silently caps at 12,000 characters; the fixer brief exceeds 18,000 and loses several tail self-checks plus its explicit artifact-write instruction, while OpenCode inserts the complete brief. Some omitted guidance survives elsewhere in native prompts/schemas, so not every tail rule is wholly absent. Cost maps count successful logical calls rather than physical retries and omit checkpoint calls; shared output-counter deltas do not establish causal per-item attribution without verified runtime update semantics. The existing consolidated-scan shadow samples only all-approved cases and cannot establish false-negative performance on rejected cases. See `FACTORY-EFFICIENCY-REVIEW.md` for measured synthetic counts, evidence limits and staged remediation. No runtime fixes or production savings claimed. |

| KI-E189 | **Efficiency implementation — SWEEP closure and scheduling repaired (KI-E186).** SWEEP now uses normal eligibility/readiness, dependency/autonomy and dirty-overlap guards, records run/claim provenance, excludes unsuccessful claims, and folds through legal/idempotent transitions. Closure requires completed required reviews, successful complete machine verification for every trusted target, and readable conforming site files. Advisory or isolated dissent can still permit unaffected sites; missing review/build evidence cannot. GC groups all canonical references to a shared worktree and refuses removal while any item is unfinished. Native SWEEP preserves per-site admission/progress using existing apply/verifier/reviewer calls. Behavioral driver/native tests cover blocked sites, failed/missing execution and mixed-state GC. |
| KI-E190 | **Efficiency implementation — recovery contracts repaired (KI-E187).** Validated structured planner state preserves escalation and steps; legacy prose cannot infer waived sign-off. Gate reuse retains a full-content/contract identity through checkpoints and verifies the complete effective required reviewer portfolio, including blocking/applicability changes. Timestamped/truncated presentation packs are no longer the reuse identity. Missing-stage recovery distinguishes unavailable execution from completed failure. Independent infra override evidence is carried consistently through execution/fold/recovery. Fresh artifact reuse creates unique run/claim identities and launch files; exact replay retains original bytes. Recovery remedies use fresh bound transcripts and before/after/current source identity, never silently replace new failure evidence with historical green. |
| KI-E191 | **Efficiency implementation — final-snapshot evidence.** Initial/integration verification use fresh attempt-bound outputs, preserving historical transcripts without letting an earlier failure poison a corrected pass. Every required invocation must carry matching target/filter, completion and nonvacuous counts. Source mutations trigger independent final verification and current semantic checks; accepted plan alternatives remain adjudicated obligations, not silently restored original requirements. Subsequent review/integrator mutations invalidate prior proof. Driver independently verifies contained transcript paths, current identities and complete trusted affected-target coverage; owning solutions take precedence over nearby production projects for suites. **Constraint:** existing baseline allowances remain count-based and are reused per suite invocation, not allocated per target/test; no stronger regression identity is claimed. |
| KI-O7 | **Efficiency implementation — OpenCode phase and evidence contracts repaired (KI-O6).** Reachable schemas are registered and exercised through real CLI submissions; positive lint candidates match producer contracts; single-clause/doc-only applicability no longer skips plan/ledger checks; explicit rejection cannot advance. Editorial writers are serialized, final mutations invalidate evidence, and handoff integration consumes immutable machine proof without duplicate build/suite instructions. Enriched launch/config/brief/profile snapshots are preserved. Required plan/prior-finding/main-drift/RED-coverage/breadth/cross-target/EF and adjudication mechanisms are implemented; inconclusive required EF is unavailable, not approved. Optional observational consolidated shadow remains the sole declared unported stage. |
| KI-O8 | **Efficiency implementation — durable versioned dispatch.** Zero-dependency v1/v2 server adapters drive pending-only descriptors across items with unique dispatch IDs, idempotent submissions, shared atomic persistence, phase locks, configured effective worker models and separate agent/build capacities. Fresh reviewer sessions stay independent. Completion requires settled tools and idle execution; v2 also reconciles durable inbox state. Cancellation deletes pending input and interrupts active work; every possibly-live/uncertain state remains fenced until settlement, including restart. Confirmed invocation receipts survive failed execution and travel in finalized envelopes; unknown model/usage is not invented. Local fake-server/real-CLI and child-process lease tests pass; live provider/server compatibility remains a host check. Leases govern wrapper commands, not arbitrary shell execution; orphaned process trees require inspection. |

Offline checks require every locked transitive archive, verify archive integrity, then use NuGet locked restore and actual target assets instead of regex-scanning all nuspec framework groups. E2E broken-tag injection replaces the complete fixture script and proves exit73/marker propagation; portable command-injection plus real Node/Bash gate tests execute zero Git mutations. Full mutating-Git transaction/release E2E remains a separate human-run check. |

| KI-E460 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a host-opt-in research-phase role (`archaeologist`) that establishes validated ground truth for a doc-less target before the planner drafts an approach, then writes that ground truth into the host's own conventional documentation.** Motivation: on a large, old codebase with no reliable documentation, a planner/fixer working from the raw finding text alone is working from assumptions that compound the same way the codebase's own stale comments already have. Gated (mirroring the existing `planner` conditional): host policy `policies.archaeology` is on (default OFF, same "must not assume one host's shape" reasoning as other host-local policies) AND the item is non-mechanical AND the target's doc-map (doc/data-flows, CONTEXT.md, AGENTS.md equivalents) is empty — reusing the existing doc-less-target signal rather than inventing a second detector. Runs as its own research phase strictly before planning. The role brief binds to one rule — "Never assume. Validate." — every claim needs a file:line citation or a command's actual output; naming/convention-based inference is explicitly not evidence; an honest open-question gap beats a confident wrong claim; the codebase's own existing comments/docs are themselves unverified claims to independently confirm, not ground truth to propagate. The agent writes validated findings into the worktree's real doc files so the NEXT item on this target inherits the coverage for free, plus a full evidence-trail artifact. Findings thread onto the item so they reach every later role's prompt (planner, fixer, review band), not just the next stage. Enrichment only, never a gate: a null/thin result does not fail the item. Model routing deliberately mid-tier (not the top budget-gated tier), since on a genuinely doc-less host this role can fire on close to every non-mechanical item's first touch of each target, so it needs a cost-predictable default; cost is front-loaded onto first contact and tapers as the doc-map stops reporting the target doc-less. |
| KI-E461 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a "no-dissent" direct-recovery carried its gates map FORWARD from the prior attempt unchanged whenever the last failure was a deterministic-override (a stale/flaky test) rather than a reviewer rejection — correct when the remedy touches nothing the prior gates already reviewed, but the recovery protocol had no mechanism distinguishing that case from one where the controller applies REAL new code while investigating the failure's root cause. A carried-forward gates map in the latter case means the new code was reviewed by nobody.** Live, twice, same session: two items each failed on a deterministic-override traced to a pre-existing, unrelated bug; the controller's fix introduced a NEW gating shape with no precedent anywhere else in the repo — an undocumented divergence. Both recoveries folded CLOSED with every gate carried forward APPROVED, because the "no dissent" scaffold generates zero re-gate prompts — not one of those verdicts had ever looked at the new code. The architect-gate checklist already had the exact item that would have caught this; the gap was never a missing check, it was the recovery mechanism skipping the gate entirely. One of the two was caught only because an unrelated dissent happened to still require a fresh adversarial re-gate, and that reviewer noticed the undisclosed pattern on its own initiative.
