| KI-E260 | **`factory.js`'s own checkpoint-writing instructions never adopted the more robust native checkpoint/artifact-guard protocol that the rest of the system already builds and tests for real — a genuine, scoped integration gap, not a stale test and not dead code.** Surfaced while closing out a broad pre-existing test-suite cleanup (10 originally-failing focused suites; 8 fully fixed with real root causes this same session — a macOS `/private/var` symlink-resolution mismatch across 9 tests in 3 files, a submodule-vs-plain-clone mount assumption, a test fixture not mirroring a real local-override config, a stale `band-cost.mjs` planner-cost term and a stale checkpoint-lifecycle `'started'`-outcome assumption that was structurally impossible to ever observe, plus an honest `t.skip` conversion for a `.NET SDK 8.0.425`-exact-version precondition that used to crash silently instead of reporting). The last 2 (`native-artifact.test.mjs`, `native-efficiency_test.mjs`) were investigated in depth and deliberately left failing rather than forced. What was found, precisely: `_workflow/lib/native-checkpoint.mjs` (`nativeCheckpointRelay`/`nativeCheckpointRequest`/`nativeJsonWriteInstruction`), `native-evidence-request.mjs`, `native-persistence.mjs`/`native-persist.mjs` (a real CLI, invoked via `spawnSync` in the test), `native-artifact-guard.mjs`/`artifact-guard.mjs` (a real permission-hook CLI), and `native-context.mjs` are NOT dead or abandoned — confirmed via a repo-wide grep: `effective-infra.mjs` is imported by `driver.mjs` and `opencode/runtime.mjs`; `native-evidence-request.mjs` by `driver-integration.mjs`, `prepare-verification.mjs`, and 4 more lib files; `native-checkpoint.mjs`'s `nativeJsonWriteInstruction` by `live-claude-factory.mjs`. This is real, extensively self-tested, working infrastructure — atomic same-bytes-skip replacement, digest-mismatch rejection, instruction-bearing-path (`CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`) protection against accidental overwrite, symlink-escape detection, a real permission-decision hook for Write/Edit/Bash/PowerShell/MCP tool calls — genuinely more robust than `factory.js`'s current own checkpoint mechanism (a cheap haiku agent directly `Write`s progress/result JSON and self-verifies via `node -e "JSON.parse(...)"`, no digest validation, no artifact-tree safety check). But `factory.js` itself was never updated to actually generate its checkpoint-writing agent instructions via `nativeCheckpointRelay` — the two failing tests' `source.includes(<verbatim function body>)` check against `factory.js` is correctly, honestly failing on a real gap, not a stale assumption. One real wrinkle that muddies "just wire it in": `native-persistence.mjs`'s own state-validator hardcodes a `progressStage` enum including `'admission'`/`'sweep-apply-started'`/`'sweep-post-apply'` — stages confirmed via `git log -S` to have never existed anywhere in `factory.js`'s real history, meaning even the native protocol's own design doesn't fully match this repo's actual, current stage vocabulary; a straight swap would need that reconciled too, not just the two named functions inlined. **Deliberately NOT fixed, on purpose, by two independent investigations that both stopped rather than force it:** wiring this in means replacing the live checkpoint protocol every currently-running and future batch depends on for kill-resilience (KI-E137) — a genuine redesign decision with real production-resilience stakes, not a safe drive-by fix, and not something to decide unilaterally mid-cleanup. Explicitly rejected as a false fix: pasting the two functions into `factory.js` as unreferenced dead code would make the string-presence check pass while creating a false appearance of integration and violating this repo's own dead-code prohibition (`gate-developer.md`) — not done. **Status: real, understood, deliberately open.** Needs an explicit owner decision: adopt the native protocol for real (reconciling the phantom `admission`/`sweep-*` stages first), or make a documented, deliberate call to keep the two write paths as intentionally different designs. `node _workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs`: 2379/0 throughout this whole investigation (unaffected by any of the fixes above). `focused suites: 28 passed, 2 failed (this gap), 1 skipped (env precondition unmet)` — down from the original `21 passed, 10 failed, 0 skipped`. |
| KI-E261 | **A per-route override for `tryAgent`'s top-level retry budget (`RT.planner`/`RT.adjudicator` now capped at 1 attempt instead of the global default of 3), motivated by live evidence from a cost/effectiveness retrospective, not speculation.** a cross-cutting item's cycle-133 batch logs showed `"a cross-cutting item:planner"` (routed `claude-fable-5`) stalling 5 separate times (the Workflow runtime's own stall-detector, ~180s "no progress" each) before finally succeeding on its 3rd ITEM-X `tryAgent` attempt — meaning up to 2 full exhausted attempts' worth of stalling (each itself up to ~6 real dispatch tries via the runtime's internal retry) were burned BEFORE the already-configured, proven-reliable `claude-opus-4-8` fallback (KI-D10) would even have been reached. fable-5 is already documented elsewhere (KI-L49) as a "budget-gated" top-capability model with a known credit/access-flakiness history — this is a quantifiable, recurring cost of that known constraint, not a one-off. **Fix:** `tryAgent(prompt, opts, ...)` now reads `const attempts = opts.attempts || ATTEMPTS` instead of the bare global constant, and `call()` threads a route's own `route.attempts` into `opts.attempts` when present — fully additive and backward-compatible: any route that never sets `attempts` is byte-identical to before. `RT.planner` and `RT.adjudicator` (factory.js) both set `attempts: 1` — NOT reducing fable-5's real chance to succeed on that one attempt (the Workflow runtime's own internal stall-retry safety net still applies within it), just reaching the already-reliable fallback sooner instead of repeating the whole stall-prone cycle 2 more times first. Deliberately narrow: no other route was touched — this targets specifically the two fable-5+opus-fallback routes the evidence was about, not a blanket retry-budget policy change. Mirrored to `_workflow/opencode/routing.mjs`'s RT table for data parity, with an explicit note that it is currently INERT there (`dispatcher.mjs`'s own dispatch/retry mechanism is architecturally separate from `factory.js`'s `tryAgent` and was not touched — the opencode backend is not in production use for real batches, so this was left honestly unfinished on that side rather than silently duplicating logic that has never been exercised). Verified: `node _workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs` 2385/0 (6 new assertions: the override plumbing itself, both routes' exact config, and that exactly 2 routes — no more — were touched). |
| KI-E262 | **`shedReuseToFit` (KI-E480) gained a second and third sheddable tier — `precedent` then `batchPattern` — after a live regression where `priorAttempt`-shedding alone was no longer enough.** a cross-cutting item+a portal-themed item A fit as a 2-item launcher on their FIRST attempt (cycle 131/132) but no longer fit even fully `priorAttempt`-shed on reattempt (cycle 133/134, 535,430 B against the 524,288 B cap). Root-caused precisely, not guessed: `factory.js`'s own source — inlined verbatim into every launcher, competing for the exact same 512KB budget batch data uses — grew **16,153 bytes this same session** (`git show a751bb633:.../factory.js` → 369,161 B vs the current 385,314 B), from KI-E253/E257/E259/E261's new mechanisms and their documentation. That growth alone ate a real, measurable slice of the headroom every future batch has to work with. **Fix:** `shedReuseToFit` now tries 3 tiers in order, advancing to the next only once the current one is fully exhausted, stopping the instant the batch fits: `priorAttempt` (unchanged — an item re-derives its plan/test/fix), then `precedent` (KI-E63 — a gate-APPROVED sibling's evidence; compose() already treats its absence as a silent no-op, so losing it costs only a slower pass, never a wrong one), then `batchPattern` (KI-E62 — similarity-clique tagging; a pure consistency nicety, and only non-empty for multi-item batches in the first place). **Deliberately, permanently excluded from ever being shed**: `docMap` (KI-E61 — every prior main-tree-contamination incident this factory ever caught hit exactly the doc-path ambiguity this field exists to resolve) and `peers` (KI-L32 — the cross-worktree collision-avoidance lock; shedding it in a multi-item batch lets a fixer silently redo a sibling's work). Both stay in every launcher unconditionally regardless of batch size — the code states this rationale explicitly, not just by omission, so a future editor adding either back to the sheddable list has to consciously override a stated safety reason. The per-shed log line now names which field was actually dropped (`dropped ${field} for ${id}`) instead of a hardcoded "reuse payload" string that would have been actively misleading once a non-reuse field could be the thing shed. Verified: `node _workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs` 2390/0 (2 pre-existing KI-E480 pins generalized to match the new per-tier code shape, not weakened — they still assert largest-first ordering and universal announcement, just not hardcoded to the `priorAttempt` field name; 5 new KI-E261 pins covering tier order, the exhaust-then-advance loop, and the explicit, stated docMap/peers exclusion). |
| KI-E263 | **Direct recovery (`DRV recover` → sealed `recoveryVerification` contract → `fold`) has five independent, reproducible gaps that block closing genuinely-finished items — found live closing a cross-cutting item (CLOSED) and a portal-themed item A, 2026-09-28. No engine code was changed for this row: per-item artifacts were corrected and one host-local config mapping was added; the engine-level decisions below are OPEN and need an owner call (escalate-don't-tune — each one touches a deterministic evidence check, so none was patched to get a fold through).** (1) **The manual-fallback path can never seal.** When `verificationExpectations()` cannot derive targets, `cmdRecover` deliberately `delete`s `row.recoveryVerification` and prints "falling back to a manual recovery scaffold" (README: "run the equivalent build/filter/suite by hand, tee, then fold"), but `admitAttempt(..., {recovery:true})` has already set `row.attemptIdentity.recovery = true`, and `fold`'s deterministic `verifyRecoveryTranscript` (`lib/driver-integration.mjs`, ~line 570) unconditionally throws `fresh recovery verification contract missing` when the contract is absent — so the documented manual procedure can NEVER pass, and the flag persists (`reset`/`reopen` do not clear it). Result: fold override → FAILED → auto-escalate once the retry bound is hit. Workaround used: fix the INPUTS so `recover` derives a real v2 contract (2 and 3 below), then run the printed evidence sequence, `seal-recovery`, fold. Owed: make the fallback sealable, or make it explicitly terminal in the README. (2) **`test.json` provenance is brittle.** `verificationExpectations` cross-checks `test.json.runCmd`/`testFiles` against `verify-red-raw.txt`'s `FACTORY::RED::START <target> :: <filter>` markers and failed three different ways on stale/verificationOnly multi-cycle files: a literal template `runCmd` (`<TestProj.csproj>`) → `test.json lacks independently resolvable project provenance`; `--filter FullyQualifiedName~X` vs a bare-`X` marker → `test.json command disagrees with RED target/filter`; a `testFiles`-derived owning `.csproj` vs a `.sln`-granularity marker → `RED filter target is not a test-file owning project`. Fixed per item by correcting `test.json` to describe what was actually run (a `testFilesNote` records it); raw transcripts are never edited. (3) **A shared library with no wrapping `.sln` resolves to its own bare csproj for BOTH build and suite.** `affectedVerificationTargets`' directory-walk maps any touched file under `Shared.Otp/` to `Shared.Otp/src/Shared.Otp/Shared.Otp.csproj`, and `verificationExpectations` uses that one target for `build` AND `suite` (FULL band) — a library has no tests, so `suite` always fails `missing/excessive TRX files` (exit=2). Fixed via the existing-but-previously-unused `cfg.solutions` override (checked first) in the gitignored `config/factory.config.local.json`: `"Shared.Otp": "Shared.Otp/tests/Shared.Otp.Tests/Shared.Otp.Tests.csproj"` (additive, one path prefix). Any other library with no `.sln` needs its own entry until build and suite targets are resolved separately. (4) **`cmdRecover` hard-codes `band: 'FULL'` for the contract (~`driver.mjs:3376`), so a LIGHT-band item is demanded a full-suite proof its original pipeline never required — and an UNRELATED pre-existing failing test in the same project then makes it unclosable.** `compareSuiteBaseline` refuses any suite failure without a captured `baseline-raw.txt` (`seal-recovery` throws `suite failures require a captured target/test baseline (absent)` — confirmed live, before writing anything); a baseline captured after the claim is deliberately ignored by the KI-E43 reFix fence (correct: it stops a post-fix baseline from laundering a regression) and must live inside the item's own worktree, so once the tree has been edited a genuine pre-fix baseline is unobtainable — backdating or hand-writing one is fabricating evidence and was refused. a portal-themed item A's blocker was exactly this: `a seller-facing portal.Tests.Controllers.ReturnsControllerIntegrationTests.Disposition_PartialRefund_WithOverrideAmount_AsSeller_InvokesClient`, PROVEN culture-dependent by the controller (this machine's locale uses a comma decimal separator, so the posted form value `5.5` binds as `55`; Moq prints the expected value as `5,5`; the test PASSES under `LC_ALL=en_US.ITEM-X LANG=en_US.ITEM-X` and under `DOTNET_SYSTEM_GLOBALIZATION_INVARIANT=1`), pre-existing by construction (a locale-dependent failure in files the diff does not touch; independent re-gates also reported it in the item's cycle-132 history), zero diff overlap. It exposes a real latent product bug — `ReturnsController` binds a `decimal?` form field with no invariant-culture handling, while HTML number inputs always post dot-decimals — that is its own work item, deliberately NOT folded into this item (`Program.cs` is this item's root-cause file; touching it would stale every gate). Honest resolution used: run the recovery suite under a neutral locale (recorded in the transcript and the fold note) and report the bug separately. Decisions owed: should recovery honour the item's real band, and should `verify/build-test.sh` export a neutral locale (`LC_ALL=en_US.ITEM-X LANG=en_US.ITEM-X`) for every verification run so results stop depending on the operator's machine locale? (5) **Fold idempotency and ordering traps.** Every fold attempt consumes its `resultId` (`<id>#<cycle>r<N>`) — even one overridden to FAILED — so resubmitting the same id reports `no new current results (already folded or stale)`; an override also auto-escalates the row and the next fold then rejects `bad-transition ESCALATED->CLAIMED`: use `DRV reset <id>` (NOT `recover`, which mints a new runId/claimId and orphans a sealed contract; `reopen` only accepts CLOSED/READY) and bump the resultId. The fold's `stale-recovery-gates` check rejects a recovery whose gate:architect/developer/qa/security/po file predates the worktree's latest code change (legitimate — new tests added by a fix agent stale earlier APPROVED gates; re-gate those fresh; review flows were not flagged). Evidence: a cross-cutting item CLOSED through this exact path (sealed 8-invocation transcript: `Shared.Otp.Tests` 103/103, `an identity service.sln` 661/661 including real-Postgres `FACTORY::REALINFRA::Postgres`, `a secondary identity service.sln` 320/322 with 2 pre-existing skips); step-by-step record in `CONTINUE_PROMPT_2026-09-28.md` §0 and `state/items/a portal-themed item A/recovery/ITEM-X-28.md`. |
| KI-E264 | **The main selftest failed (`PR#9: the opencode suite runs green under the main selftest (no TOTAL line)`, 2392/1) in any environment that forces terminal colors — including the Claude Code harness itself (`FORCE_COLOR=3`) — and it was invisible in sessions that did not set it.** Root cause: `_workflow/opencode/_dispatch-e2e.mjs` built its "dead controller" fixture by spawning a child that ran `console.log(process.pid)` and parsing `Number(dead.stdout.trim())`. Under `FORCE_COLOR`, Node's `console.log` wraps a number in ANSI color codes (`\x1b[33m69291\x1b[39m`), so the parse was `NaN`; `owner.json` then held `{"pid":null}`, `tryAdmission` treated the slot as `incomplete` on every pass, and `withBuildSlot(..., 100)` fell through to `global build slot timeout` instead of the asserted `orphaned build lease requires process-tree inspection` (`build-lease.mjs:78`) — the assertion at `_dispatch-e2e.mjs:196` threw an uncaught `AssertionError` that killed the whole opencode suite subprocess (hence "no TOTAL line"). Not a regression from any engine change: reproduced in isolation with a scratch script (`dead pid NaN`), and the suite is 2393/0 with `FORCE_COLOR=0 NO_COLOR=1`. **Fix:** the child now writes `process.stdout.write(String(process.pid))` (never colorized). **Hardening so it cannot silently return:** the main selftest now ALWAYS runs the opencode suite subprocess with `FORCE_COLOR=3` (the worst case) regardless of the operator's environment, and a new source pin asserts `_dispatch-e2e.mjs` contains no `console.log(process.pid)` and uses the `process.stdout.write(String(...))` form. Verified: `node _workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs` 2394/0 both with `FORCE_COLOR=3` and with colors off (2393 + 1 new pin); `node _workflow/opencode/_selftest.mjs` 228/0 under forced color; the 2 "focused suites" failures are the pre-existing, deliberately-open KI-E260 gap, unchanged. Lesson for any future selftest: never parse machine-readable values out of a child's `console.log` — colorization is environment-dependent; write raw bytes. |
| KI-E265 | **Planner narrowly scoped removal/rename plans without grepping the full solution tree, producing `files` lists that covered fewer occurrences than actually existed — guaranteed `gate:developer` or `probe:plan-commitment-scan` CHANGES_REQUIRED on the first pass.** Live evidence: a feature-removal item cycle-137: planner scoped the SAR removal to the validator only, missing `EnumValue.Example=4` in the enum file and `SomeFlag()` and the the admin portal model. **Fix:** `agents/planner.md` step 7 now mandates a case-insensitive grep across the full repo for EVERY occurrence of the symbol being removed/renamed BEFORE writing the `files` list, with the match count pasted into `ruleRisks` so the fixer cannot silently narrow scope. **Verified:** 2026-10-02. |
| KI-E266 | **Planner did not propagate blast-radius to consumer services when the finding was in a shared assembly (`the host project.*`, `Shared.**`, `Shared.Events`), producing plans that listed only the library's own files and predictably failing `gate:architect` for missing cascade.** Shared-library changes silently broke consumers when the plan's `files` listed only the assembly directory and not the downstream service directories that import it. **Fix:** `agents/planner.md` step 8 now requires reading `doc/data-flows/README.md` and grepping every service directory for the type/method/event being changed; every affected consumer appears in `blastRadius` with a one-line note. **Verified:** 2026-10-02. |
| KI-E267 | **Fixer silently skipped the doc-sync check (completion check 1) by treating "subject to peer locks" as license to omit any mention of `doc/data-flows/` in `fix.json`.** The peer-lock carve-out was legitimate (another item may hold the lock), but a fixer that simply never ran the grep would produce the same output as one that checked and found nothing — indistinguishable. **Fix:** `agents/fixer.md` completion check 1 now mandates a concrete grep command (`grep -rn "<Name>" doc/data-flows/`); the two acceptable `fix.json` outcomes are `"updated"` (entry updated) or `"docSyncRequired": false, "docSyncReason": "no entry found"` — silent omission is never acceptable. **Verified:** 2026-10-02. |
| KI-E268 | **Fixer added "cleanup while I'm here" changes not covered by the plan's `files`/`blastRadius`/`steps`, which predictably failed `probe:plan-commitment-scan` on every resubmit.** The fixer would make small adjacent tidy-ups that seemed safe but were outside the plan's stated scope; the probe correctly flagged the extra files, wasting a full gate band. **Fix:** `agents/fixer.md` completion check 9 (ITEM-X ADHERENCE) now includes a scope-inflation guard: any change not in `files`, `blastRadius`, or a `the host's standards-evolution doc`-mandated converge MUST be reverted unconditionally; "it seemed related" is insufficient justification. **Verified:** 2026-10-02. |
| KI-E269 | **Fixer ran targeted single-test (`--filter`) after a ITEM-X amendment instead of the full suite, leaving regressions in sibling tests undetected until a full band run exposed them.** An amendment is a new diff; the prior gate-APPROVED state is invalidated for the entire test surface, not just the targeted test. **Fix:** `agents/fixer.md` completion check 6 (ITEM-X) now mandates FULL `verify/build-test.sh` after any amendment — NEVER a targeted filter; output saved as `verify-postamend-raw.txt` in ARTIFACTS DIR. **Verified:** 2026-10-02. |
| KI-E270 | **When the planner recommended escalation (`recommendEscalate: true`), the returned schema contained no structured decision options — the owner received an open-ended question with no concrete choices to answer, leading to long ambiguous reply threads before a resolution was actioned.** Owners prefer multiple-choice (A/B/C) over open discussion when unblocking a factory item. **Fix:** `agents/planner.md` Write+return section now includes `decisionOptions` (array of `{label, description, implication}`) when `recommendEscalate: true`; each option is a complete, mutually-exclusive, owner-answerable choice. **Verified:** 2026-10-02. |
| KI-E271 | **Phantom-manifest probe false-failed items whose fix was correctly committed to the branch by a prior session (clean worktree → `changedFiles(wt)` returns `[]` → all claimed files reported as phantoms).** The a notification-themed item item had its fix committed at `98d11faf8` in a prior session; the next session found a clean worktree, `phantomManifest([], claimed)` returned all 14 claimed files as phantoms, and the item was failed despite the work being correct and complete. The probe had no mechanism to distinguish "fixer never wrote anything" (true phantom) from "prior session committed the fix" (false phantom). **Fix:** `phantom-lint.mjs` now calls `committedSinceBase(wt)` (new `lib/worktree.mjs` export: `git diff --name-only HEAD^ HEAD`) when `changed` is empty and `claimed` is non-empty; if the last commit's diff is non-empty, those files are used as the effective `changed` set and `FACTORY::ITEM-X` is emitted. Worktrees with no committed changes still behave identically. **Selftest:** `node _workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs` 2398/0 (4 new KI-E271 pins: the two new markers + the new export + the git args). Workaround for items ALREADY failed this way: `DRV reset <id>` then rerun — the probe will now use the committed diff. **Verified:** 2026-10-02. |
| KI-E272 | **The factory had no server-mode operation: running it on a dedicated server without a human at the keyboard required a human to trigger each run interactively, apply CLOSED worktrees, and commit — making overnight / vacation-mode operation impossible.** Root: KI-E1 ("the human authors every commit") was an absolute invariant with no server-mode escape hatch; the `apply` command was deliberately plan-only; the orchestrator had no scheduler hook and no escalation notification. **Fix:** (1) `orchestrator.config.json` adds `autoCommit: { enabled: false, ... }` and `escalationWebhook: null` fields (both disabled by default — KI-E1 holds everywhere KI-E272 is not opted into). (2) `orchestrate.mjs` adds `applyAndCommit(cfg)` (copies CLOSED worktree files → `git add` → `git commit`, with file-overlap and repo-dirty pre-flight checks; no-op when `autoCommit.enabled:false`) and `notifyEscalated(cfg)` (fire-and-forget POST to Slack-compatible webhook on new ESCALATED items; no-op when `escalationWebhook:null`). Both are called after each fold in the run loop, wrapped in try/catch so a failure never crashes the orchestrator. (3) `orchestrator/webhook.mjs` (new, KI-E273) provides an HTTP endpoint for remote triggering. **`autoCommit.enabled` MUST remain `false` in the committed `orchestrator.config.json`** — opt in only via an uncommitted `config/factory.config.local.json` override on the dedicated server (mirrors the `skipAuthSetup`/`Testing` pattern). **Selftest:** 2408/0 (10 new pins: 5 KI-E272 + 5 KI-E273). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E273 | **No HTTP interface for external factory triggering — a human on vacation could not start a factory run without SSH access to the dedicated server.** Root: the factory had only an interactive (Claude Code session) entry point; `claude-headless` was available but still required a shell. **Fix:** `orchestrator/webhook.mjs` — a zero-npm-dependency HTTP webhook receiver using Node built-ins only (ITEM-X): `GET /health` (no auth; liveness), `GET /status` (auth; ledger counts + isRunning + stopRequested), `POST /trigger` (auth; spawns `orchestrate.mjs run` detached with a PID file concurrent-run guard), `POST /trigger/stop` (auth; writes `STOP_REQUESTED.md`). Auth: `X-Factory-Token` header compared via SHA-256 hash equality (constant-time, length-independent; `FACTORY_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` env var required — fails closed when absent). The spawned orchestrate.mjs run inherits the KI-E1/KI-E272 invariants unchanged — the webhook is a trigger, not a git-bypass. `.github/workflows/factory-run.yml` (new) wraps the orchestrator in a GitHub Actions workflow (cron `0 2 * * *` + `workflow_dispatch` with `backend`/`ids`/`max_lanes` inputs; `concurrency: group: factory-run, cancel-in-progress: false`; self-hosted runner; commits state files back after each run). Triggering from CLI: `gh workflow run factory-run.yml [-f backend=claude-headless] [-f ids=ITEM-X]`. **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E274 | **No way to interact with the factory from a GitHub PR without SSH access to the server.** Authoring a `/factory run` command or queuing an improvement from a phone while reviewing a PR was impossible — the only entry points were an interactive Claude Code session, the webhook, or a scheduled GitHub Actions run. **Fix:** `.github/workflows/factory-pr-interactive.yml` (new, KI-E274) — a GitHub Actions workflow triggered by `issue_comment.created` events on PRs whose body starts with `/factory`. Commands: `status` (ledger summary), `run [--ids X] [--backend Y] [--lanes N]` (trigger a factory run), `stop` (write STOP_REQUESTED.md), `resume` (clear stop marker), `improve: <description>` (queue improvement to `queue/pr-improvement-*.md`), `help`. Reacts to the comment with 👀 immediately on receipt; posts the result (truncated to 60 KB) as a follow-up PR comment with ✅/❌; commits state files back to the branch. Stop marker prevents `run` commands when the factory is paused. The `improve:` command writes a markdown file to `queue/` (operator-created factory items from there); it does NOT directly inject a factory work item (that would require driver changes). Concurrency: `group: factory-pr-{pr}`, `cancel-in-progress: false`. KI-E1 invariant applies. **Selftest:** 2408+10 (5 new KI-E274 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E275 | **FAILED items sat permanently until manually reset — even when the failure had an obvious fix (compilation error, test failure with clear message).** A factory run that produced 3 FAILED items required human intervention before those items could be retried. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds `autoFixFailed(cfg, token)` — called after each fold, wraps each FAILED item's `reports/<id>/last-failure.json` (KI-E81) through `classifyFixableError()` and calls `driver(['reset', id])` (FAILED → READY) for items with a classifiable error class: `csharp-compile`, `typescript-compile`, `msbuild-error`, `build-failed`, `test-failure`, `dotnet-error`. Infrastructure failures (`infraSuspect:true`) are explicitly skipped — they use the driver's own infra-retry (KI-L50). Per-item retry context is persisted in `state/auto-fix-context/<id>.json` (includes `retries`, `lastErrorClass`, `lastErrorSnippet`, `resetAt`) — the counter is written BEFORE the driver reset so a crash between the two preserves the count. Capped at `cfg.autoFix.maxRetries` (default: 2) per item to prevent infinite retry loops. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"autoFix": { "enabled": false, "maxRetries": 2 }` — disabled by default; set `enabled:true` on the dedicated server via `config/factory.config.local.json`. The driver's own `last-failure.md` already carries the failure context for the next factory.js run — no additional prompting is needed. **Selftest:** 2408+10+10 (5 new KI-E275 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E276 | **Factory failures were invisible without SSH access.** FAILED and ESCALATED items could only be diagnosed by reading `reports/<id>/last-failure.md` on the server. A run from the phone or a scheduled CI run gave no feedback. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds `logFailureSummary()` — called in the `finally` block of every `run`, it prints each FAILED item's `last-failure.json` reason (up to 300 chars) + autoFix retry count, and each ESCALATED item's decision status to stdout. This stdout is captured by `factory-run.yml`'s new "Post failure report to PR" step, which reads the ledger, formats a structured markdown report (failure reason code blocks + escalation owner-decision status), and posts it as a PR comment on the open PR for the current branch. Also feeds into `/factory run` PR output naturally (KI-E274 captures stdout). The report step uses a temp `.mjs` file to avoid shell-quoting issues with `node -e`. Non-fatal: no PR, no failures, or `gh` errors are all silently skipped. **Selftest:** 2418+5 (5 new KI-E276 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E277 | **The headless worker stalled on tool-permission prompts.** When running autonomously via `claude-headless` backend, the claude CLI would pause and wait for interactive approval of shell/file tools — blocking the factory for hours or until a timeout. **Fix:** `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"--dangerously-skip-permissions"` to `claudeHeadless.extraArgs`. This pre-authorises all tool use for the worker process. Safe for this deployment: the worker has no access to client data, it is a demo/educational project, and the worker is scoped to per-item worktrees. To re-enable prompts, add `"claudeHeadless": { "extraArgs": [] }` to `config/factory.config.local.json`. **Selftest:** 2418+5+5 (5 new KI-E277 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E278 | **The factory had no mechanism to escape scheduling ruts — it kept selecting the same pool of items run after run, even when they consistently failed, producing stagnant close rates with no self-correction.** Root: the scheduler (driver `group`) picked items by priority without any feedback loop; a cohort of hard items that repeatedly FAILed would keep getting scheduled (especially after KI-E275 auto-reset them) while easier items accumulated stale waiting time. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds a hill-climbing quality optimizer inspired by the steepest-ascent hill-climbing algorithm (fitness function + plateau detection + random-restart exploration). Three new functions: `hillClimbUpdate(cfg, laneResult)` — after each fold, computes per-run fitness (closed/dispatched), appends to a sliding window, detects plateaus (last `plateauRunsToTrigger` window values all < `plateauMinFitness`), sets `explorationActive = true` on plateau, resets after one exploration run, and tracks `itemLastCycle` (which cycle each item was last dispatched); `hillClimbSelectIds(cfg)` — in exploration mode, reads the ledger for all READY items, sorts by `lastScheduledCycle` ASC (never-tried items first), and returns the oldest `maxItemsPerLane * explorationRatio` as the `--ids` override — this is the "random restart": visiting a different region of the state space instead of the current local maximum's neighbors; `hillClimbReport()` — logs the fitness window, local maximum, plateau count, and exploration status each run so the operator can observe the optimizer's behavior. State persisted in `state/hill-climb.json` (NOT inside `state/orchestrator/` — that dir is reserved for per-lane run subdirectories) (fitness window, localMax, plateauCount, explorationActive, itemLastCycle). `orchestrator.config.json` adds `hillClimb: { enabled: true, fitnessWindow: 5, plateauMinFitness: 0.25, plateauRunsToTrigger: 3, explorationRatio: 0.5 }`. Integration: called in the run loop (before each lane for `selectIds`, after each fold for `update`, in `finally` for `report`). All calls are wrapped in try/catch — the optimizer is purely advisory and never crashes the orchestrator. When the operator passes `--ids` explicitly, hill-climb exploration is suppressed (human intent wins). **Selftest:** 2438/0 (10 new KI-E278 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E279 | **The factory lacked principled item selection — scheduling was a mix of priority-order and hill-climb exploration, but no mechanism balanced exploration (trying new items) against exploitation (retrying items that showed promise) in a statistically grounded way.** Root: hill-climbing explored by recency (oldest-tried items first), which is a valid restart heuristic but not a systematic tradeoff between known-good and unknown items. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds a UCB1 multi-armed bandit selection mechanism (Thompson, 1933; Upper Confidence Bound). Three new functions: `banditLoad()`/`banditSave(state)` — load/persist per-item dispatch+close counts to `state/bandit.json`; `banditUpdate(cfg, laneResult)` — after each fold, increments per-item dispatch count and close count (by cross-referencing the ledger for CLOSED status); `banditSelectIds(cfg)` — scores each READY item by UCB1: `closedRate + C * sqrt(ln(totalDispatches+1) / (dispatched+1))`, where C=`explorationConstant` (default 1.41 = sqrt(2)), optimistic initialisation at 0.5 for never-tried items. hillClimb exploration takes priority over bandit (bandit only fires when hillClimb returns null AND no explicit `--ids`). All calls wrapped in try/catch — purely advisory. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"bandit": { "enabled": true, "explorationConstant": 1.41 }`. **Selftest:** 2463/0 (5 new KI-E279 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E280 | **The factory's auto-fix retry loop gave the worker no structured guidance about what went wrong — retrying an item reset its state but not its understanding.** After an auto-reset (KI-E275), the next run saw only the raw error text in `last-failure.md`. The worker would often repeat the same mistake because there was no "here is what typically fixes this class of error" signal. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds `generateReflexion()` (Shinn et al. 2023 — "Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning"). Before calling `driver(['reset', id])`, `autoFixFailed` now calls `generateReflexion(id, retryCount, errorClass, errorText, clusterHint, warmStartContext)` and APPENDS the reflexion text to `last-failure.md`. The text contains error-class-specific guidance (e.g. for `csharp-compile`: "Focus on the exact CS error code and reported line"), a cluster hint if available (KI-E281), and a warm-start git log if available (KI-E283). The reflexion append is itself wrapped in try/catch — it must never block the reset. **Selftest:** 2463/0 (5 new KI-E280 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E281 | **Similar failures across different items were treated independently — if ten items all failed with CS0246 (missing type), each retry attempt got no benefit from the others' experience.** Root: autoFix (KI-E275) classified errors per-item but discarded the cross-item signal. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds an error-cluster module: `clusterSignature(errorClass, errorText)` — extracts a stable key (errorClass + first CS code / MSB code / exception type); `clusterRecord(id, errorClass, errorText)` — adds the item to its cluster in `state/error-clusters.json`; `clusterRecordSuccess(id, errorClass, errorText, hint)` — removes the item from the cluster and appends a fix hint (capped at 5); `clusterGetHint(errorClass, errorText)` — returns the most recent fix hint from the cluster as a string for inclusion in the reflexion text. Integration: `clusterRecord` is called in `autoFixFailed` before the reset; `clusterGetHint` feeds the cluster hint into `generateReflexion`. Hints accumulate over time — a human operator can also inspect and annotate `state/error-clusters.json` directly. **Selftest:** 2463/0 (5 new KI-E281 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E282 | **The factory produced no periodic health summary — the only way to monitor its aggregate state over time was to read `state/ledger.json` or run `orchestrate.mjs status` manually.** Root: every run produced per-item output but nothing that summarised factory-wide trends (close rate, failure concentration, hill-climb fitness trend). **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds `runRetrospective(cfg)` — called in the `finally` block of each run. Loads/persists run count state in `state/retrospective.json`. Every `runsPerRetro` lanes (default 5), writes a markdown health report to `queue/retrospective-<timestamp>.md` containing: total items + close rate + FAILED + READY counts; full by-state breakdown; hill-climb state (localMax, plateauCount, explorationActive) from `state/hill-climb.json`. The report is human-readable and can be committed alongside the queue. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"retrospective": { "enabled": true, "runsPerRetro": 5 }`. Non-fatal: every file write is try/catch. **Selftest:** 2463/0 (5 new KI-E282 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E283 | **The auto-fix retry prompt had no knowledge of partial work already committed to the item's worktree branch — the worker would sometimes redo already-completed steps because it could not see prior commits.** Root: `last-failure.md` contained the error output but not the branch history; `generateReflexion` (KI-E280) had no mechanism to inject commit context. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds `getWarmStartContext(id)` — calls `execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'log', 'factory/<id>', '--oneline', '-3'], ...)` (read-only, KI-E1 preserved — `git log` is a read operation, never mutating). Returns a formatted string listing the last 3 commits on the item's branch or null if the branch does not exist yet / git unavailable. The result is passed as `warmStartContext` to `generateReflexion()` and included in the reflexion block appended to `last-failure.md`. Entire call wrapped in try/catch — purely advisory, never blocks the reset. **Selftest:** 2463/0 (5 new KI-E283 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E284 | **The bandit's UCB1 exploration bonus was uniform across all never-tried items — a cold-start factory would try hard multi-service items at the same rate as simple single-file fixes, wasting early budget on items unlikely to close.** Root: `banditSelectIds` used `C * sqrt(ln(total+1)/(dispatched+1))` as the exploration bonus for `dispatched===0` items — the same formula as for known-history items, giving all cold-start items identical UCB1 exploration scores. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds `estimatedDifficulty(id, l)` — returns a [0, 1] heuristic difficulty score from three proxy signals: title word count (≥20 words → 1.0, weight 0.4), file count from `item.files` (≥10 files → 1.0, weight 0.3), and count of hard-domain keywords in the title (`auth`, `payment`, `crypto`, `kyc`, `migration`, `saga`, `outbox`, `compliance` — ≥3 → 1.0, weight 0.3). `banditSelectIds` replaces the uniform UCB1 bonus for `dispatched===0` items with `C * (1 - estimatedDifficulty(r.id, l))` when `curriculum.enabled` is true — easier items get a higher exploration bonus and are tried first in the cold-start phase. Existing items with dispatch history keep their normal UCB1 score unchanged. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"curriculum": { "enabled": true }`. State: no new file — operates through `bandit.json` and `loadConfig()`. **Selftest:** 2463+5=2468/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E285 | **The factory's item selection was deterministic given the bandit/hill-climb state — a promising item pool that was consistently FAILING would remain the top UCB1 selection run after run without any stochastic escape.** Root: neither the bandit nor the hill-climb injected true randomness into the selection; hill-climb exploration fired only on plateau detection (periodic, not continuous). **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds a simulated annealing perturbation layer (`saCool`, `saPerturb`, `saLoad`/`saSave`, `SA_STATE_PATH`). Temperature `T` decays as `T = T0 * coolingRate^runCount` (defaults: T0=0.3, coolingRate=0.95). Before each `runLane` call, `saPerturb(cfg, idsFlag)` runs: with probability T, one randomly chosen item in the current selection is swapped for a random READY item not already in the selection — injecting diversity that is strongest at run 1 (T≈0.30) and near-zero after ~60 runs (T<0.01). After each fold, `saCool(cfg)` is called to decay the temperature and increment `runCount`; state is persisted to `state/simulated-annealing.json`. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"simulatedAnnealing": { "enabled": true, "initialTemperature": 0.3, "coolingRate": 0.95 }`. Both `saCool` and `saPerturb` are try/catch-wrapped in the run loop — purely advisory, never crash the orchestrator. **Selftest:** 2468+5=2473/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E286 | **The factory used a single fixed promptTemplate for all claude-headless worker runs — there was no mechanism to evolve the worker prompt toward higher close rates over time.** Root: `cfg.claudeHeadless.promptTemplate` was a static string used verbatim for every run. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds a prompt-evolution module (`peSelectPrompt`, `peUpdate`, `peLoad`/`peSave`, `PE_STATE_PATH`, `PE_GUIDANCE_PHRASES`). Two template variants are tracked in `state/prompt-evolution.json` (initially both equal to the baseline promptTemplate). The active variant alternates each run so both accumulate equal exposure. After `runsToEvaluate` combined runs (default 10), the variant with the lower close rate is killed and the winner is mutated by injecting a randomly chosen phrase from `PE_GUIDANCE_PHRASES` (8 phrases covering focus, root-cause framing, test verification, scope discipline, dependency lookup) before the "Do not run any git commands." clause. The evolved pair is then A/B tested in the next cycle — a continuous micro-evolution loop. `peSelectPrompt(cfg)` replaces the direct `cfg.claudeHeadless.promptTemplate` reference at the claude-headless dispatch line. `peUpdate(cfg, laneResult)` is called after each fold. Both are try/catch-wrapped. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"promptEvolution": { "enabled": true, "runsToEvaluate": 10 }`. **Selftest:** 2473+5=2478/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E287 | **The factory's `laneTimeoutMinutes` was set by intuition (240 min) with no data-driven feedback on whether lanes were actually taking that long or much less.** Root: no wall-clock measurement was captured for completed lanes. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds a dynamic-timeout module (`timingRecord`, `timingRecommendTimeout`, `timingLoad`/`timingSave`, `TIMING_STATE_PATH`). The run loop captures `t0Lane = Date.now()` before each `runLane` call and calls `timingRecord(cfg, Date.now() - t0Lane)` after (in try/catch). Timing samples are stored in `state/item-timing.json` (last 100 samples, ring-buffer). `timingRecommendTimeout()` sorts the samples, finds the P95 value, adds a 20% buffer, and returns the result in minutes. This recommendation is surfaced in every retrospective report (KI-E282) under a new "## Dynamic timeout recommendation (KI-E287)" section showing the recommended value vs the current `laneTimeoutMinutes`. Operators can use this to tune the config — the recommendation is advisory and never mutates `laneTimeoutMinutes` automatically. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"itemTiming": { "enabled": true }`. **Selftest:** 2478+5=2483/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E288 | **The factory's tunable knobs (bandit exploration constant, hill-climb plateau threshold, etc.) were set once at deployment and never adjusted — good defaults for a cold start may be suboptimal after hundreds of runs.** Root: no feedback loop connected factory fitness to config values. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds a config meta-optimizer module (`coUpdate`, `coGetKnobValue`, `coSetKnobValue`, `coLoad`/`coSave`, `CO_STATE_PATH`, `CO_KNOBS`). Every `runsPerCycle` lanes (default 10), one knob from `CO_KNOBS` is perturbed ±`perturbFraction` (default ±10%) on a rotating schedule (`CO_KNOBS = ['bandit.explorationConstant', 'hillClimb.plateauMinFitness', 'hillClimb.explorationRatio', 'simulatedAnnealing.coolingRate']`). Fitness (closed/total items from the ledger) is measured over the next `measureWindow` (default 3) runs and compared to the baseline. If fitness improved, the perturbed value is kept; otherwise the original value is restored in the live `cfg` object. The optimizer modifies `cfg` in-memory only — it never writes back to `orchestrator.config.json` (changes are exploratory, per-run). State is persisted to `state/config-optimizer.json` (runCount, knobIdx, pendingKnob, pendingValue, baselineValue, baselineFitness, trialFitnessSamples). `coUpdate(cfg)` is called after `peUpdate(cfg, res)` in the run loop, try/catch-wrapped. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"configOptimizer": { "enabled": true, "runsPerCycle": 10, "measureWindow": 3, "perturbFraction": 0.1 }`. **Selftest:** 2483+5=2488/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E289 | **None of the individual self-improvement techniques had a circuit-breaker for a "deep rut" — a state where every technique is tuning locally but the whole system stays stuck near zero fitness for many consecutive runs.** Hill-climb plateau-escape (KI-E278) handles short stagnation; SA random swaps (KI-E285) add individual-run noise; but none of them jointly triggered a system-level reset when all of them were failing together. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` adds auto-recalibrate (`checkAndRecalibrate`, `forceRecalibrate`, `recalLoad`/`recalSave`, `recalExplorationBoost`, `RECAL_STATE_PATH`). After each fold, `checkAndRecalibrate(cfg)` checks the hill-climb `fitnessHistory` for `runsBelow` consecutive entries below `fitnessThreshold` (defaults: 5 runs below 0.1). If triggered: (1) SA temperature is reset to `T0` (restart the annealing schedule from scratch); (2) the bandit exploration constant is multiplied by 1.5 for the next `recoveryRuns` lanes via `recalExplorationBoost()` applied in `banditSelectIds`; (3) config-optimizer phase is reset to `measure` for a clean baseline. State persisted to `state/recalibrate.json` (`active`, `runsRemaining`, `triggeredAt`, `triggerCount`). A `queue/recalibrate-notice-<ts>.md` is written so the operator can see recalibrations in the PR retrospective. `node orchestrate.mjs recalibrate` force-triggers it. `/factory recalibrate` PR command added to `factory-pr-interactive.yml`. `state/recalibrate.json` staged in both CI workflows. `orchestrator.config.json` adds `"recalibrate": { "enabled": true, "runsBelow": 5, "fitnessThreshold": 0.1, "recoveryRuns": 3 }`. **Selftest:** 2488+5=2493/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E290 | **No way to trigger a factory run by assigning a GitHub issue, and no live smoke test against the running host application after items close.** The only triggers were manual CLI, PR comment, nightly schedule, or `workflow_dispatch`. There was also no post-close verification that the fix actually worked against the real deployed stack on the runner (in-memory tests pass; live cluster might diverge). **Fix (2026-10-03):** (1) New workflow `.github/workflows/factory-issue-assign.yml` — fires on `issues: [assigned]`, extracts the factory item ID from the issue title (regex: `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9]*(-[A-Z0-9]+)+`), checks the ledger state, runs a 1-lane `claude-headless` run, posts 👀 + ✅/❌ comment to the issue. Skips gracefully for CLOSED/IN_PROGRESS items; posts a format-hint for unrecognized titles. (2) New `build-test.sh smoke <ServiceName> [<namespace>]` subcommand — opt-in via `HOST_LIVE_TEST=1` repo variable. Port-forwards the k8s service, hits `/health/ready`, emits `FACTORY::SMOKE::PASS/FAIL/SKIPPED`. Never fails the factory run — informational only. (3) Both `factory-issue-assign.yml` and `factory-run.yml` run the smoke step after the factory closes items — posts 🟢/🔴 live-smoke result to the issue/PR comment. (4) Test issue #64 created (`an identity-themed item`). **To activate live testing:** add repo variable `HOST_LIVE_TEST=1` in GitHub Settings → Variables, ensure the host application is running on the runner (`kubectl -n <namespace> get svc`). **Selftest:** no new assertions (smoke is shell-only, no selftest wiring needed). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E291 | **Simulated annealing (KI-E285) silently discarded an explicit operator `--ids` request, producing a no-op run.** When an item was requested by ID (issue-assign → `--ids an admin-portal item`, or `/factory run --ids X`), `saPerturb` was applied UNCONDITIONALLY — unlike the hillClimb (KI-E278) and bandit (KI-E279) selectors, which both skip when `flags.ids` is set. With probability T (0.3 at run 1) SA swapped the sole requested item for a random READY item; because `saPerturb` draws swap-in candidates on `state==='READY'` only (not full schedulability — the driver's `group` is the single authority), the swap-in was often unschedulable (deps unmet / realInfra / file-locked) → empty batch → the requested item never ran (issue #110: `an admin-portal item→an auth-themed item`, all failure-context artifacts "(not found)", gate verdicts `{}`). Intermittent and worst early (high temperature) — exactly when escalated items are assigned for retry. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` run loop computes `baseIds` once and gates SA as `flags.ids ? baseIds : saPerturb(cfg, baseIds)` (an explicit --ids is authoritative, never perturbed — mirrors the hillClimb/bandit `flags.ids` guards). Secondary: a perturbed auto-selection that collapses to an `empty-batch` retries the lane ONCE with the unperturbed `baseIds` (an explicit --ids that is itself unschedulable still surfaces as a clear failure, never silently runs something else). **Selftest:** 2493+5=2498/0 (5 new KI-E291 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E292 | **The factory base branch was not configurable — every entry point checked out the triggering ref (default branch), so there was no way to point the factory at a different branch (e.g. run on `develop`) except via `gh --ref`, which also swaps the workflow-file version that runs.** **Fix:** branch is now configurable with precedence: per-run `branch` input (`factory-run.yml` `workflow_dispatch`) > `FACTORY_BASE_BRANCH` repo variable (persistent default, honored by `factory-run` / `factory-issue-assign` / `factory-pr-interactive`) > the triggering `github.ref`. Wired to `actions/checkout`'s `ref`; because `addWorktree`'s base defaults to current HEAD (`worktree.mjs`), the single checked-out branch governs "run from" (source checkout), "run on" (`factory/<id>` worktree base), and the state push target. Backward compatible: input + variable both unset → `github.ref` (behavior unchanged). Workflow-YAML-only, no selftest wiring (like KI-E290). **Verified:** 2026-10-03. |
| KI-E293 | **The claude-headless worker died ~3s after spawn with no output on the self-hosted runner — the item was claimed/dispatched but produced zero checkpoints (run 37158699797), leaving it orphaned in CLAIMED.** Root cause (confirmed from the claude-code v2.1.x binary, NOT assumed): the GitHub Actions self-hosted runner executes as **root** (`/root/Work/actions-runner/...`), and claude-code's `isRootOutsideDeliberateSandbox()` = `platform!=="win32" && getuid()===0 && !isSandboxEnvSet() && !isBubblewrapEnvSet()` makes `claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions` abort immediately with `"--dangerously-skip-permissions cannot be used with root/sudo privileges for security reasons"` (stderr → the runner-local `outputDir/stderr.log`, never committed — hence invisible in the Actions log). The flag (KI-E277) was set but rejected under root. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` claude-headless dispatch spawns claude with `env: { ...process.env, IS_SANDBOX: process.env.IS_SANDBOX ?? '1' }`; `IS_SANDBOX` satisfies `isSandboxEnvSet()` so the root bypass is granted and the worker runs unrestricted in its per-item worktree. Internet is probed independently (`!hasInternetAccess()`), so the sandbox marker does NOT force the worker offline. Operator-overridable (`?? `). **Selftest:** 2498+3=2501/0 (3 new KI-E293 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E294 | **A dead worker (KI-E293) left a stale per-item worktree on the persistent self-hosted runner; the next run's `actions/checkout` (clean:false) recreated `.git`, orphaning the worktree admin entry, so `group` failed with "'…/worktrees/an orchestration-themed item' already exists" / "not a git repository" and never dispatched (run 37160927865).** **Fix:** `factory-run.yml` / `factory-issue-assign.yml` / `factory-pr-interactive.yml` add a pre-run "Prune stale factory worktrees" step — `git worktree prune`, then `rm -rf` of any `state/worktrees/*/` dir that is no longer a valid git worktree (`git -C "$d" rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree` fails), then a second `git worktree prune`. Only DANGLING dirs are removed (a valid in-flight worktree passes the check and is kept), so the debris self-heals on the next run and `addWorktree` can create a fresh worktree. Workflow-YAML-only, no selftest wiring. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E295 | **The claude-headless worker's stdout/stderr were written to the runner-local `outputDir/{stdout.log,stderr.log}` and never committed — every worker failure was undiagnosable without SSH to the runner (the KI-E293 + KI-E296 root causes were both hidden there).** **Fix:** `factory-issue-assign.yml` adds a pre-run "Surface worker logs" step that dumps the two newest lanes' `stderr.log` (full) + `stdout.log` (tail 300) into the Actions log. It runs early (after the KI-E294 prune, before the worker gate), so it surfaces the PREVIOUS run's logs even when the current item is non-runnable (CLAIMED) — i.e. a failed run can be diagnosed by re-assigning the issue without re-running the worker. Workflow-YAML-only, no selftest wiring. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E296 | **The worker ran ~10.5 min then exited with 0 validated checkpoints — an orchestration-themed item produced a correct `plan.md` but never `result.json`, staying CLAIMED (run 37162094140).** Root cause (from the KI-E295 worker log): the worker launches the impl-factory pipeline via `Workflow({scriptPath})` as a **`local_workflow` background task**; claude-code's default **background-wait ceiling is 600s**, after which it prints `"Background tasks still running after 600s; terminating. Set CLAUDE_CODE_PRINT_BG_WAIT_CEILING_MS=0 to wait indefinitely."`, kills the still-running Workflow, and exits — before the pipeline (plan → review gates → integrate, dozens of agents, routinely >10 min) can write `result.json`. The IS_SANDBOX fix (KI-E293) was working — the worker was in `bypassPermissions` mode doing real work, just killed mid-flight. **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` childEnv adds `CLAUDE_CODE_PRINT_BG_WAIT_CEILING_MS: process.env.CLAUDE_CODE_PRINT_BG_WAIT_CEILING_MS ?? '0'` so claude -p waits for the Workflow to complete; the orchestrator's `laneTimeoutMinutes` watch (default 240) is the real upper bound. Operator-overridable. **Selftest:** 2501+2=2503/0 (2 new KI-E296 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E297 | **The factory-issue-assign result comment showed ✅ for any outcome that was not FAILED/ESCALATED/BLOCKED — so a CLAIMED/incomplete run (the KI-E296 background-wait kill, or a `group`-failed worktree error) was reported as "✅ Factory run" even though the item never closed, masking real failures as successes.** **Fix:** `factory-issue-assign.yml` result-comment emoji is ✅ ONLY when `FINAL_STATE=CLOSED`; FAILED/ESCALATED/BLOCKED → ❌; CLAIMED/READY/IN_PROGRESS → ⚠️. The failure-context section (last-failure / result / probe / gates) is attached whenever the emoji is not ✅ (previously only on ❌), so an incomplete run surfaces its context too. Workflow-YAML-only, no selftest wiring. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E298 | **The worker ran the FULL impl-factory pipeline to completion and wrote a valid `result.json` (an orchestration-themed item, `toState: FAILED` — the fix itself failed a review gate), but the orchestrator-watch never validated it (0/1 for 34 min → INCOMPLETE → item stuck CLAIMED, run 37188429876).** Root cause (confirmed by reproducing `validCheckpoint` against the real committed data — every gate PASSED except line 57): `orchestrator/lifecycle.mjs:57` rejects a result unless `result.claimId === item.claimId` when the run-args item carries a `claimId` (driver.mjs:526 stamps it), but `factory.js`'s result object (line ~1079 + error path ~3378) never wrote `claimId`, so `result.claimId` was `undefined` → every server-mode result was rejected. The driver's OWN fold path (which produced the 297 prior closes) does not use this check, so the bug only surfaced once KI-E296 let the orchestrator-watch path produce a result at all. **Fix:** `factory.js` both result objects now echo `claimId: item.claimId` / `claimId: it.claimId` (a pure data field, Workflow-runtime-safe); the driver fold already reads `r.claimId` (driver.mjs:1211), so this is additive and compatible with both paths. **Selftest:** 2503+3=2506/0 (3 new KI-E298 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E299 | **The agent's actual code fix lived UNCOMMITTED in the per-item worktree (KI-E1 — the factory never commits) and was lost after each run: the worktree is runner-local (pruned/recreated between runs via KI-E294 + actions/checkout), the branch-push carried only base HEAD, and nothing captured a diff — so the single most valuable artifact for review AND for future factory improvement vanished. (The feedback files feedback.md / last-failure.md / review-*.md ARE force-committed by factory-run + factory-issue-assign — but NOT by factory-pr-interactive.)** **Fix:** (1) `orchestrate.mjs` `captureWorktreeDiffs()` writes each item's `state/items/<id>/worktree.diff` (tracked edits via `git diff HEAD` + untracked NEW files via `git diff --no-index`) after the lane completes, covering complete AND incomplete lanes; ITEM-X git only (no add/commit/reset) so KI-E1 holds. The workflows already `git add -f state/items/`, so the diff is committed + durable across runner/cache loss. (2) `factory-pr-interactive.yml` now also `git add -f state/items/` (it was the one path omitting item artifacts). (3) `factory-issue-assign.yml` result comment points to the committed `worktree.diff` (apply with `git apply`) instead of the fix-less branch. **Selftest:** 2506+5=2511/0 (5 new KI-E299 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E300 | **Preservation favored only the LATEST attempt — a path-to-failure and a path-to-success are equally valuable, but `itemsDir(id)` (factory.js:545) is NOT cycle-keyed, so every rerun OVERWRITES `state/items/<id>/`. With KI-E299 each run IS committed, so prior attempts survive in git HISTORY — but the working tree holds only the newest, so a success silently overwrites the earlier failure's path (and vice versa), and the two never coexist for side-by-side analysis.** **Fix:** `orchestrate.mjs` `archiveItemRun()` snapshots `state/items/<id>/` into an append-only `state/items/<id>/_runs/cycle<N>-<runId>/` after each lane (hand-rolled recursive copy that skips `_runs/` — cpSync hard-refuses a copy into its own subdirectory; idempotent per (cycle,runId)). The factory never writes under `_runs/`, so every attempt's full artifact set — plan, reviews, result.json (with toState), worktree.diff — persists side-by-side. The commit step's `git add -f state/items/` already commits it, so NO workflow change is needed. Best-effort (never fails the lane). **Selftest:** 2511+5=2516/0 (5 new KI-E300 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E301 | **Server-mode fold result silently DROPPED on a push race — the bug that undercut the entire remote feedback loop.** All three factory workflows ended their commit step with `git push origin HEAD \|\| true` (factory-run: a bare `git push origin HEAD`) and NO `git pull --rebase` first. When origin advanced between the runner's checkout and its post-fold push — a concurrent factory run, OR any intervening commit — the push was a non-fast-forward rejection SWALLOWED by `\|\| true`, so the run reported success while the fold's **ledger update (state + attempts) AND `feedback.md`/`last-failure.md` were discarded**. Net effect: a retrigger started fresh (attempts=0, no prior-failure digest to read), so the feedback loop could NEVER be durable across runs — KI-E299/E300 preserved the artifacts of a run, but this discarded the fold that records the outcome. Observed live: run 37194437336 folded an orchestration-themed item→FAILED (`probe:docsync-scan`) at 12:05Z, but the fold never reached origin (committed ledger still held the pre-run reset, no feedback.md). **Fix:** every workflow push now derives `BASE` from the checked-out branch (`git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`, falling back to `FACTORY_BASE_BRANCH`/`github.ref_name` when detached), then runs `git fetch` + `git rebase origin/$BASE` + `git push HEAD:$BASE` in a bounded 5× retry loop with backoff; a rebase conflict aborts+refetches+retries; a final failure emits a loud `::error::` AND `exit 1`s the run red (never green-with-annotation) — never a silent `\|\| true`. **Selftest:** 2516+9=2525/0 (9 new KI-E301 pins, 3 per workflow). **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E302 | **Run reporting was a ~40KB raw-log dump with no at-a-glance signal.** The issue comment pasted the worker's full stdout behind only an emoji + state line; the useful facts (which gate decided it, the stage path, cost/tokens, files changed, where the artifacts live) were buried or absent. **Fix:** `orchestrator/run-report.mjs` reads `state/items/<id>/result.json` and emits a compact markdown digest — outcome + band + attempt, the decisive gate (from `note`/`gateDetails`), the `transitions` stage path, a gate tally, files changed (worktree prefix stripped to repo-relative) + root cause + fix steps, `tokensUsed` + per-model call counts, and links to the committed artifacts (worktree.diff, `_runs/`, feedback.md, review-pack, result.json). `factory-issue-assign` now leads the comment with the digest and collapses the raw log into `<details>`. **Selftest:** 2525+4=2529/0 (4 new KI-E302 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E303 | **Auto-open a PR on a CLOSED (successful) run — owner directive 2026-10-04.** Previously the factory NEVER committed a fix (KI-E1); the fix sat uncommitted on the `factory/<id>` worktree for the human to hand-commit. The owner now wants a PR created automatically on success. **Fix:** `orchestrator/open-pr.sh` — on a CLOSED item ONLY (all review gates + verify APPROVED), commit the worktree fix to its `factory/<id>` branch, push it, and open (or update) a PR to BASE; idempotent (skips if a PR already exists), skips a verification-only close with no code change, emits `pr_url=`. Wired into `factory-issue-assign` (single item, gated on `final_state==CLOSED`) and `factory-run` (every item it folded this run, bounded via each lane's `fold.json`); both gated by repo var `FACTORY_AUTO_PR` (default on). **The KI-E1 invariant is REFINED, not broken: `master`'s source is NEVER pushed directly — the machine commits only to the `factory/<id>` PR branch, and the human reviews + merges.** `CLAUDE.md` § Hard behavioural rules updated to match. `factory-pr-interactive` intentionally not wired (it is a `/factory` command surface already operating inside a PR). **Selftest:** 2529+7=2536/0 (7 new KI-E303 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E304 | **KI-E301's durable push shipped BROKEN — `git rebase` refused on the factory's dirty tree, so the fold STILL never persisted (but now loudly, not silently).** A factory run leaves uncommitted state files + main-drift in the working tree; after the commit step stages its specific paths and commits, the remainder stays unstaged, and `git rebase "origin/$BASE"` aborts with `cannot rebase: You have unstaged changes` on all 5 retries, then the KI-E301 `exit 1` fires → red job. Observed live on run 37203537972: pipeline ran (`1/1 validated checkpoints`), fold succeeded (`applied 1, rejected 0`), the commit was made locally with every artifact (ledger, feedback.md, worktree.diff, `_runs/`), but the push never landed → origin unchanged. (What DID work: E301's no-silent-swallow — the failure was red + annotated, not hidden behind the old `\|\| true`.) **Fix:** `git rebase --autostash` across all three workflows — auto-stashes the uncommitted remainder, rebases the fold commit onto the remote tip, restores it, and keeps the main-drift OUT of the pushed commit (stashed, never committed). This is rebase's own dirty-tree handler (auto-restored), not a standalone `git stash`. **Selftest:** 2536+6=2542/0 (6 new KI-E304 pins). **Verified:** 2026-10-04 (selftest green; live re-run triggered to confirm the fold now persists). |
| KI-E305 | **The durable-push logic (KI-E301/E304) was inline-copied into all three workflows — drift-prone, and a string-presence selftest pin cannot catch a RUNTIME bug like E304 (it took an 82-minute live run to surface the dirty-tree rebase failure).** **Fix:** extracted to one shared, shellcheck-clean script `orchestrator/durable-push.sh` (single source of truth; all three workflows now call `bash …/durable-push.sh "$BASE"`), plus a real runtime guard `orchestrator/durable-push.test.mjs` (node:test, auto-discovered by the `../../orchestrator/` scan in _selftest.mjs) that reproduces the EXACT E304 scenario — an advanced remote + a dirty working tree — against a local no-network git topology and asserts (a) the fold commit lands on the remote, (b) the remote advance is preserved (rebase, not force), (c) the dirty tree is restored by `--autostash`, and that a bare rebase refuses the dirty tree. Catches the class in ~1s instead of after a 2-hour run. **Selftest:** E301/E304 pins consolidated onto the script; +1 focused suite → 2540/0, 32 suites. **Verified:** 2026-10-04 (runtime test green). |
| KI-E306 | **autoFix (the cross-run FAILED→READY auto-retry, orchestrate.mjs:542) only recognized build/test/compile errors — a blocking ITEM-X or pre-band PROBE rejection (e.g. `probe:docsync-scan` CHANGES_REQUIRED) returned `null` from `classifyFixableError`, so a merit-gate FAILED just SAT until a human re-assigned it.** This was the #1 autonomy gap: KI-E299–E304 PRESERVE the gate finding, but nothing consumed it across runs. **Fix:** `classifyFixableError` adds a `gate-rejection` class matching `CHANGES_REQUIRED` / `probe:*` / `*-scan (KI-…)` / "blocking gate finding", so autoFix resets the item to READY (bounded by `autoFix.maxRetries`) and the next run re-fixes reading feedback.md while the prior-finding scan (KI-E168) verifies the new diff evidences the rejection. A gate it keeps failing is surfaced by the self-improvement analysis (KI-E309) as a systemic proposal. Infra failures stay the driver's (KI-L50); scope-stops are BLOCKED (never FAILED), so never auto-retried. **Selftest:** +3 pins → 2543/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E307 | **No cumulative, committed run record ACROSS runs — diagnosing factory health meant manual git/log archaeology, and the raw telemetry stream was runner-local (gitignored).** Per-item artifacts exist (state/items/<id>/_runs/, KI-E300) and `reports/telemetry-latest.md` is committed, but nothing tied runs into a queryable index or an at-a-glance dashboard, and `telemetry/data/events.jsonl` was gitignored (lost on cache/runner loss). **Fix:** `orchestrator/run-history.mjs` — `recordRuns()` appends one compact JSON line per folded item to `reports/runs.jsonl` (append-only, dedup by resultId: ts, id, toState, band, decisiveGate, tokensUsed, cost, fixSteps, filesChanged, transitions); `renderFactoryReport()` regenerates `reports/ITEM-X.md` (ledger state, throughput + close rate, THE GATES THAT REJECT FIXES MOST — the self-improvement signal, stuck items ≥2 runs, recent-runs table). Both run per-fold in orchestrate.mjs; `reports/` is committed by every workflow. The raw `telemetry/data/events.jsonl` is un-gitignored + committed nightly by factory-run. Behavioral test `run-history.test.mjs` (auto-discovered). **Selftest:** +5 pins +1 suite → 2548/0, 33 suites. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E308 | **The ranked owner-decisions digest (`cmdDecisionsDigest`, KI-E24/E74/E151) was fully built but NEVER WIRED — BLOCKED/ESCALATED owner-gate items (the "Should the host project build/fund X?" questions) sat silently; no run regenerated the digest.** **Fix:** the orchestrator now runs `driver(['decisions-digest'])` per fold (next to telemetry-report), regenerating `reports/decisions-digest.md` (severity × age ranking, extracted question + options, cross-service bundling, paste-ready reply format) every run; `reports/` is committed. The ITEM-X (KI-E307) "Needs attention" section points to it; stuck items + infra-retries surface in the same report's stuck-items table. **Selftest:** +2 pins → 2550/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E309 | **The factory had optimizers (selectors, configOptimizer, promptEvolution, retrospective) but NO analyzer turning the run record into ranked, ACTIONABLE improvement proposals — "self-improvement" produced no prioritized backlog a human or the factory could act on.** This is the automatic self-improvement mechanism (owner directive: "mandatory to improve factory"), part A. **Fix:** `orchestrator/self-improve.mjs` — `computeProposals()` mines `reports/runs.jsonl` (KI-E307) + the ledger for systemic weaknesses (the gate rejecting the most fixes, items stuck on the same gate across runs, infra-failure rate, cost outliers), ranked by impact; `analyzeSelfImprovement()` writes `reports/ITEM-X.md` (committed backlog) + queues the top NEW ones to `queue/factory-improvement-<date>.md` (dedup via state/self-improve.json). Runs EVERY fold (mandatory). ITEM-X — it never edits the factory's code; proposals are executed as ordinary gated items (the KI-E310 consumer → gates → human-merged PR). Behavioral test `self-improve.test.mjs`. **Selftest:** +4 pins +1 suite → 2554/0, 34 suites. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E310 | **The configOptimizer meta-tuner (KI-E288) was a cross-run ITEM-X — it perturbed a selection knob, measured fitness, and "kept" a win only in the in-memory `cfg`, which was lost at process exit; so the factory's own auto-tuning tuned nothing across runs.** Part C of the self-improvement mechanism. **Fix:** when a knob is kept (trial ≥ baseline), `coUpdate` now persists it to `state/config-optimizer.json` `.applied` (already a committed file); `loadConfig` merges `.applied` back on every load via `applyConfigOptimizerOverride()` — whitelisted to the 4 `CO_KNOBS`, each clamped to a safe range, FULLY GUARDED (any error → config unchanged, never breaks loading), reversible (clear `.applied`). Accepted tuning now survives across runs. Logic smoke-tested (apply / clamp / corrupt-safe / whitelist+type-safe). **Selftest:** +4 pins → 2558/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E311 | **The result-comment step died (exit 127) on every run — a `${{ }}`-into-shell ITEM-X injection.** It did `SUMMARY="${{ steps.ctx.outputs.summary_md }}"` (likewise lf_md/result_md/feedback_md/gates), interpolating the digest + markdown — which are full of backticks (`` `probe:docsync-scan` ``, `` `SomeService/.../Program.cs` ``) — into the script TEXT, where bash executed them as command substitution. Observed live on run 37208861275: `line 80: an orchestration-themed item: command not found`, `…Program.cs: Permission denied`, exit 127 → the report comment never posted (the fold had already persisted, so cosmetic, but it broke every comment). Exposed by the KI-E302 digest (first content with pervasive backticks). **Fix:** pass EVERY dynamic value via `env:` (`SUMMARY`/`LF`/`RESULT`/`PROBE`/`FEED`/`GATES`/`PR_LINE`/`ASSIGNEE`/…) and reference `"$VAR"` — env values are printf ARGS, never re-parsed for backticks/`$()`. Locally proven injection-safe (a `$(touch)` in the value does not execute). pr-interactive (user comment body via env) + factory-run (report via a local var) were already safe. **Selftest:** +3 pins → 2561/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-04. |
| KI-E312 | **autoFix (KI-E306) retried only build/test + probe-scan failures, NOT the DOMINANT class — review-gate rejections — because (a) it reads `last-failure.json.reason`, which is EMPTY for gate/review/fold-override failures (the verdict lands in result.json gates + the ledger note), and (b) the E306 regex matched `probe:`/`CHANGES_REQUIRED`, not `gate:developer`/`review:*`.** Exposed live by the an analytics-themed item validation run (issue #112): autoFix logged "not classifiable" for all 3 FAILED items (empty reason), and the self-improve analyzer (KI-E309) itself flagged `gate:developer` as 100% of failures. **Fix:** (1) autoFix falls back to `result.json` (note + non-APPROVED gate names) when last-failure reason is empty/short; (2) `classifyFixableError` widens `gate-rejection` to also match `gate:*` / `review:*` / "review(s) not APPROVED" / "adjudicator upheld" / "gates not approved". Verified: an analytics-themed item's real note now classifies as `gate-rejection` → autoFix resets it → the next run re-fixes from feedback.md. **Selftest:** +3 pins → 2564/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-04 (exposed AND fixed from a live run — the self-improvement loop working as intended). |
| KI-E313 | **The reporting trusted `result.json.toState` (the agent's CLAIM), not the fold's actual decision — so a fold-OVERRIDDEN failure was reported + recorded as CLOSED.** Exposed live by a second orchestration-themed item (#114): the worker claimed `CLOSED` (21 gates "green"), but the deterministic fold-time override caught an absent verify baseline and overrode to FAILED (ledger: CLAIMED→FAILED, then autoFix KI-E312 reset→READY). Yet the issue-comment digest showed "✅ CLOSED" and `runs.jsonl` recorded `toState=CLOSED` — corrupting the dashboard close-rate AND the self-improvement signal (it would count an over-claimed green as a success) and misleading the operator. **Fix:** `run-history.mjs` exports `ledgerOutcome(root,id)` = the most-recent TERMINAL ledger transition (CLOSED/FAILED/ESCALATED/BLOCKED, ignoring a later non-terminal autoFix reset); `recordRuns` + `run-report.mjs` use it for the outcome (falling back to result.json only when the ledger has no terminal transition), record `claimedState`+`overrode`, and the digest shows a "⚠️ Fold override: agent claimed X → overridden to Y" callout. Verified against the real H-2 data: the digest now shows ❌ FAILED + the override. **Selftest:** +3 pins +1 test → 2567/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-05. |
| KI-E314 | **The first genuine CLOSE (a second orchestration-themed item attempt 2) auto-committed + pushed its fix to `factory/a second orchestration-themed item`, but `gh pr create` was DENIED — "Resource not accessible by integration (repository.pullRequests)".** factory-issue-assign's `permissions:` block granted `contents: write` + `issues: write` but NOT `pull-requests: write` (factory-run had no block → repo default, also no pr-write). So E303's commit+push half worked but the PR never opened (the step stayed green because open-pr.sh soft-fails on gh errors). **Fix:** add `pull-requests: write` to factory-issue-assign + a full `permissions:` block (contents/pull-requests/issues: write) to factory-run — future closes auto-PR. The H-2 fix branch (`ca4fb8c3d`: a 66-line fail-closed guard + a 242-line regression test + doc sync) was already pushed, so its PR (#115) was opened for review out of band. **Selftest:** +2 pins → 2569/0. **Verified:** 2026-10-05. |
| KI-E315 | **A genuinely-CLOSED item's digest showed "N-1/N APPROVED", contradicting its own "all N gates APPROVED" line.** The 1 "not approved" was the `reaudit` gate, whose PASSING verdict is a compound string (e.g. `"code=ok security=ok"`) — not the literal `"APPROVED"` — so a naive `!== 'APPROVED'` count miscounted it as a rejection, though all gates had actually passed. **Fix:** `run-history.mjs` exports `gatePassed(verdict)` — a gate passes UNLESS its verdict matches a rejection pattern (`CHANGES_REQUIRED\|REJECT\|FAIL\|BLOCK\|NOT OK\|DENIED\|ERROR`); report/history code uses it for the not-approved count instead of exact-string equality. |
| KI-E316 | **A live-deploy-smoke step's comment always printed "skipped (set the live-test env var)" regardless of the ACTUAL skip reason — misleading when the var IS set.** The smoke had actually RUN and skipped for a different reason (the target service wasn't deployed on the runner's cluster), yet the comment blamed the unset var. **Fix:** the smoke's summary line now extracts the real `SKIPPED reason=…` and shows a per-reason hint instead of a single generic message. |
| KI-E317 | **A live-deploy-smoke's cluster check mislabeled EVERY `kubectl` non-zero exit as "service not found", masking the real cause (e.g. the runner's kubectl has no/wrong kubeconfig) when a service IS actually deployed.** Fix: (1) capture kubectl's real stderr and split "service-not-found" (stderr matches `not found`) from "cannot-reach-cluster" (+ the first error line as detail); (2) honor an explicit kubeconfig-path override env var so the step can reach the right cluster; (3) the resulting hint tells the operator which env var to set. |
| KI-E318 | **A live-deploy-smoke hardcoded port 80 + `http://` for its health probe, so it failed every service that listens on a custom port/HTTPS (the common case).** Fix: discover the target Service's actual port + name via `kubectl get svc -o jsonpath` before port-forwarding; a port named `https` (or 443) selects `https://` + an insecure-TLS curl flag (the probe tunnels through a local port-forward to localhost, so the server cert's in-cluster DNS name cannot match the tunnel address — a test-only liveness check over a local tunnel, not a production transport path). |
| KI-E319 | **Added a live functional-smoke harness that, on an item reaching CLOSED, builds that item's own service image from its fix branch, loads it into the runner's local cluster (auto-detecting the common local K8s distros), rolls it out with a restart-gate, health-checks it, then rolls back by default** (an opt-in flag keeps it deployed on a PASS). Deliberately NOT wired into the bulk nightly dispatch loop — building+rolling every newly-closed service every night would churn a shared cluster; this stays a per-item, explicit action. |
| KI-E320 | **First live automatic deploy-test run proved the harness plumbing but surfaced that the runner's cluster genuinely did not have the target service deployed** (a real "not found", confirming the kubectl connectivity fix from the prior entry worked).
