| KI-E456 | **A self-tuning parameter optimizer's trial-perturbation step had a floor but no ceiling on a freshly-computed trial value, while a separate persist-time clamp correctly bounded the same parameters — so a live trial could temporarily push a parameter (e.g. a cooling-rate parameter governing simulated annealing) past its intended upper bound for the whole trial window, even though the value that eventually got persisted was always correctly bounded.** Fix: hoisted the per-parameter bounds to a single shared constant used by both the trial-perturbation step and the persist-time clamp, and changed the trial-value computation to clamp against both the floor and the ceiling. |
| KI-E457 | **A verification-contract check rejected any item whose test-evidence proof was captured against a whole solution file rather than the one specific project file owning the test — even though the project's own verification tooling documents a solution-level target as valid.** Root cause: the path-resolution helper only special-cased a directory target (resolving to its single owning project file); a solution file resolves to its own literal path, and the cross-checks then compared that literal path against a specific project-file path via exact match only, which can never succeed for a solution-level target regardless of correctness. Fix: added a helper that parses a solution file's own project-reference lines into resolved project paths, and a matching function that accepts either an exact match OR the target being a solution file that genuinely references the project in question — a solution file that does NOT reference the project is still correctly rejected. |
| KI-E458 | **The agent-side telemetry seam only had a start and an end event — an agent that dies mid-stage (killed, hung, crashed) leaves a completely silent gap with no error, no event, nothing, until (if ever) a later retry starts the same stage again. A forked investigation into one such incident found the dead starts sat in complete silence for 40-60 minutes each with zero other events for that item, while sibling concurrent lanes completed cleanly in the exact same window (ruling out host-wide resource contention) — the root cause of what actually hangs stayed unconfirmed.** Fix: added a third, best-effort agent telemetry event — a short free-text progress "heartbeat" an agent can emit immediately before any single operation expected to run more than a couple of minutes (a build/test invocation, a real-infra wait). The telemetry report's "agent died mid-stage" table now shows the last heartbeat note and timestamp for any unmatched start, so the next silent-hang incident is diagnosable instead of an invisible gap. This does not attempt to diagnose or kill the hang itself, only to make the next one diagnosable. |
| KI-E459 | **Non-mechanical items had no pre-band mirror of the late, expensive adversarial review gates — a weak fix could run the entire full, most-expensive review band only to fail at those gates after the item's progress had already been checkpointed. Cross-referencing an effectiveness report (non-trivial items closing at roughly a third the rate of mechanical ones), a telemetry report (100% of terminal failures having already run the full band to completion), and a sample of escalated items (several failing four different adversarial gates SIMULTANEOUSLY with a human-review step siding with the rejectors) confirmed this was genuinely weak fixer output on hard items, not one overly-strict gate.** Fix: added a new cheap pre-band probe for non-mechanical code-change items, deliberately modeled on two of the expensive gates' own established judgment criteria (a behavioral-impact-analysis checklist, and a "hunt list" of commonly-missed defect classes: unstated assumptions, missing error/cancellation handling, half-done acceptance, untraced contract changes, evidence that looks like proof but doesn't discriminate the defect). On a credible high-severity finding, one bounded fixer amend and re-scan runs; if the finding survives, the item fails cheaply pre-band instead of burning the full expensive band on a defect the probe already caught. |
| KI-E499 | **Ported from a host-mount session — `verdictFromParse` silently passed real regressions after the baseline contract switched to an object.** While consuming an independently-evolved capability from a sibling copy of this engine, `driver.mjs`'s `deterministicVerifyOverride` had already been updated to call `verdictFromParse(p, baseline, options)` with `baseline` as a `captureBaseline(...)` object (the target/test-identity baseline system in `lib/baseline.mjs`), but `verdictFromParse` itself still used the old contract: `const base = baselineFailures \|\| 0` on an object argument is truthy, so `p.suite.failed - <object>` evaluates to `NaN`, and `NaN > 0` is `false` — the fold-time pass/fail gate returned `pass:true` ("build clean, tests green") for a real, unambiguous regression. Reproduced empirically: 5 genuine new suite failures against an empty captured baseline still reported a pass. Fix: `verdictFromParse` updated to the object-shaped baseline contract, with a regression test asserting the exact NaN-comparison failure mode is caught. A cautionary example for any engine that evolves a shared contract (like a baseline/evidence shape) across two independently-maintained copies of the same codebase — a silent type-contract mismatch at one call site can invert a pass/fail gate with no error, no crash, just a wrong green. |
| KI-E500 | **Ported from a host-mount session — a known, deliberate architecture gap: the Workflow-runtime pipeline script lacks the evidence-identity/admission-tracking verification architecture the driver/CLI layer gained independently.** One side of the engine (the CLI driver + an alternate-runtime port) independently grew a `captureIdentity`/`EVIDENCE_IDENTITY_VERSION`/admission-relay architecture that replaces prose-hint verification with a machine-derived exact-command contract. Porting it into the Workflow-runtime pipeline script was deliberately deferred: that script runs with no filesystem access, so every pure helper it needs must be hand-inlined byte-for-byte from the shared lib and kept in sync — a substantial, separately-scopable piece of work, not something to rush inside a routine consume/verify pass. Confirmed safe to leave as-is for now: no hard-throw on the newer identity format exists anywhere in the merged tree, so the CLI driver runs today without the pipeline script supplying the new identity fields. Concretely un-covered while this gap stands: the native admission-relay assertions in the test suite that exercise the pipeline script's own dispatch path never call the mechanism. Needs a dedicated, carefully-tested follow-up once prioritized — not a silent permanent state. |
| KI-O21 | **The opencode-runtime self-test crashed with `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'some')` at `_selftest.mjs:620` — a previously-noted "pre-existing opencode-runtime failures, out of scope" aside was this exact crash, now root-caused and fixed.** Root cause: a prior change ("consume a sibling copy's evolved capability") added an evidence-freshness guard to `cmdNext` (`runtime.mjs`): whenever `progress.phase` is one of the late-pipeline phases (`gates`, `gates_adjudicate`, `gates_regate`, `po`, `refute_reaudit`, `escalatecheck`, `integrate`, `integrate_judge`, `checkpoint`) and `evidenceIntact(progress)` is false, the guard reads a field that is `undefined` for a progress record predating the new identity format, and calls `.some()` on it — crashing instead of degrading gracefully. Fix: the guard now checks the field exists before calling `.some()` on it, treating an absent/legacy identity record as "not fresh" (fail closed to a re-verify) rather than crashing. A general lesson for any evidence-freshness check added after older progress records already exist on disk: validate the SHAPE of old data defensively, don't assume every record was written under the new contract. |


## Verified design properties (recorded so they are not re-investigated)

- The global agent limiter cannot deadlock — every `agent()` routes through one limiter; an
  item never holds a slot while awaiting another.
- Per-item results are isolated — a thrown item degrades to `FAILED`; siblings still
  complete and fold (the batch never rejects as a whole).
- A null/again-null gate result fails closed (`!== 'APPROVED'` → `FAILED`).
- `foldResults` is idempotent and self-heals a partial application (it re-walks from the
  current state rather than corrupting it).

## How to add an entry

Give it a stable `KI-<class><n>` id, one row stating the issue/constraint and its
mitigation or status, and — if it is a correctness fix — a matching assertion in
`_workflow/lib/_selftest.mjs`. Resolved entries stay (marked resolved), never deleted.
